# Linear A: strategies log

One row per strategy: method and control, result, verdict. Earlier work is summarised in FINDINGS.md.

| ID | Method and control | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| la1:LA-1.1a | Build tables. Linear B (DAMOS): unit letter followed by a number, credited to the last commodity ideogram on the document (648 capacity-unit rows on commodities shared with Linear A). Linear A: fraction letters by carried-over commodity (307 rows, 13 letters with n>=3). | LB: T is dry-only (GRA 264, HORD 88, NI 28, OLIV 16, AROM 10, CYP 5; OLE/VIN 0 of 323). S is liquid-only (OLE 63, VIN 22; 85/86). V on both (liquid 75/212). Z on both (14/19 liquid). LA: no letter is restricted to one family. | Tables built. LB sub-units are commodity-locked; LA letters are not. |
| la1:LA-1.1b | First scoring: sum of raw LB log-lift (add-0.5 smoothing), best role per letter. Label-shuffle null, LB role-shuffle null, planted control. | Every letter goes to Z (to P once weight units are added). Label shuffle P = 0.17. Weight-unit run degenerate (P = 0.51 / 1.0, planted recovery = chance 0.14). | Method broken: rare roles get inflated lifts, and the LA vs LB commodity marginals dominate. Replaced. |
| la1:LA-1.1c | Corrected: log-likelihood of each letter's commodities under P(c given f) proportional to P_LA(c) x lift_LB(u,c), Dirichlet prior (alpha 5) centred on the role marginal. Many-to-one argmax (13 letters, 4 roles). Nulls: (i) permute letters across LA quantities, 2,000 runs; (ii) permute unit labels across LB rows, 500 runs. Planted: random true roles, commodities redrawn from the model at real LA counts, 300 runs. Bootstrap stability, 500 runs. | Assignment: J, E, D, K, JE, A, H -> V; B, L2, DD -> Z; W, F, X -> T; nothing -> S. G = 7.9. Label shuffle: mean -1.7, P = 0.0025. LB role shuffle: mean 9.2, P = 0.66. Planted: recovery 0.80 (chance 0.25), planted G mean 66.9, so the real G is 8x below what a true sub-unit system at this n would give. Bootstrap: J, JE, A, B, L2, F, X >= 0.95; E 0.64, D 0.77, K 0.73, W 0.63, DD 0.54. | The only thing that beats a null is the already known letter-commodity dependence (label shuffle). The real Linear B role profiles fit no better than shuffled LB labels (P = 0.66). Arrow not supported. |
| la1:LA-1.1d | Sensitivity: weight units M, N, P added as roles; HORD merged into GRA. | Same assignment. With weights: G 8.0, label P = 0.007, LB role P = 0.81, planted 0.63 (chance 0.14). HORD merged: K moves to Z; LB role P = 0.58 / 0.73. | Robust to both choices; still no fit to Linear B specifically. |
| la1:LA-1.2a | 31 KU-RO sections (30 auto from totals_test.py + hand-split HT 123+124a *308 column); 8 carry fractions (letters A, E, F, H, J, JE, L2). LB-ABS: each letter takes its cycle-1 role's Linear B value by entry family (dry T 1/10, V 1/60, Z 1/240; liquid S 1/3, V 1/18, Z 1/72). Null: each letter draws independently from a pool of 118 values (unit fractions 1/2..1/16 and all pair sums < 1), 10,000 draws. | LB-ABS balances 0 of 8 fraction sections (overall 8/31, against 9/31 with integers only: HT 13 is lost). Single random draw: mean 0.009 of 8. Gaps: HT 104 short by 29/30, *308 column short by 19/20, HT 9a short by 337/180. | Fails. Linear B absolute sub-unit sizes are far too small to close any Linear A total. |
| la1:LA-1.2b | LB-SCALE: keep the role ratios (T = 6V, V = 4Z, S = 6V) but let V's value range over the 118-value pool; keep the best. Control: best of 118 random value sets (same search size), 300 runs. | Best: 1 of 8, and each scale closes a different single section: V = 1/2 closes HT 104 (= J 1/2), V = 1/3 closes the *308 column, V = 2/3 closes HT 9a. No scale closes two. Best-of-118 null: mean 0.64, P = 0.64. | Fails. No ratio set derived from the assignment beats random values. |
| la1:LA-1.2c | Reference: lineara.xyz conventional values on the same sections. | 1 of 8 (HT 104). Against one random draw P = 0.009, but these values were not drawn blind (HT 104 is the classic J = 1/2 case), so this is not a fair test. | Reference only. Repeats Attack 1: too few clean totals. |
| la1:LA-1.3a | 61 compound fractions, 34 ordered pair instances with both letters assigned. Implied size T > V > Z (S > V > Z). Count larger-first, smaller-first, and ties (same role = same value, which the strict order forbids). Null A: permute roles among letters, 20,000 runs. Null B: random pool values. | Larger-first 23, smaller-first 6, ties 5. Ties: J=E, J=A, J=H, J=K, H=K all forced equal yet always written in one order. Reversals: E before F x5 (F is T, the larger). Direction: P = 0.19 (A), 0.24 (B). Ties fewer than role permutation (P = 0.086) but far more than real values allow (null B mean 0.3). | Fails. The assignment collapses letters the order shows are distinct, and its size order is not better than chance. |
| la1:LA-1.4a | Cycle 4 (direct test of the T/S signature). LB: T liquid 0/323, S liquid 85/86. LA: count letters (n >= 8) with >= 90% one family. Control: shuffle letters across quantities, 10,000 runs. Planted: relabel 20% of dry-commodity J as a new letter (~11 tokens). | LA: 0 exclusive letters (J 38/94 liquid, E 7/40, D 7/34, B 7/31, K 11/28, JE 8/22, A 7/15, L2 6/13, H 6/12). Null mean 0.14. Planted dry-only letter found in 180/200. Only rare letters lean dry-only: W 0/5, X 0/4, F 0/3 liquid (together 0/12; upper bound < 3/12 = 0.25 liquid share). | No Linear A letter behaves like T or S, and the test could have seen one. Fraction letters are not commodity-locked sub-units of the Linear B kind. W, X, F remain a possible small dry-only set (n too small). |
| la1:LA-1.final | Cycles 1-4: profile assignment to LB roles (label-shuffle, LB role-shuffle, planted controls); exact-sum test with LB values and with LB ratios at a free scale (random-value and best-of-k nulls); writing order vs implied size (role-permutation and random-value nulls); dry/liquid exclusivity (shuffle and planted controls). | Assignment J,E,D,K,JE,A,H -> V; B,L2,DD -> Z; W,F,X -> T; none -> S. It beats letter shuffling (P = 0.0025; known letter-commodity dependence) but not shuffled Linear B labels (P = 0.66), at 1/8 of the planted-signal strength. Totals: 0/8 with LB values, 1/8 at best scale (null 0.64, P = 0.64). Order: 5 forbidden ties, direction P = 0.19. No letter is dry- or liquid-locked (0 vs planted detection 90%). | REJECTED. No fraction value set derived from Linear B sub-units beats the controls. Linear A fractions are not commodity-locked named sub-units as in Linear B; the commodity dependence is a weaker preference. Not cracked. |
| la7:LA-7.final | 100 signs coded blind from rendered glyphs under random IDs and frozen before any context (31 predictive: plant 12, vessel 7, animal 3, body 9). Pre-set predictions: plant->grain/tree/spice, vessel->liquid, animal->animal, body->VIR. Tested on word + commodity pairs (adjacent n=192, window n=296), first sign vs last sign, single-sign words, word types, and logogram ligature adjuncts (189 tokens, 32 types). Null: picture-label permutation. Control: the same codes on Linear B (DAMOS, 2,457 word pairs, 217 adjuncts). Planted control: the test detects the effect when >=30% of predictive-class words obey it (34/60 at q=0.3, 60/60 at q=0.5). | No LA test reaches P<0.05 (best: strict single-sign words P 0.09, strict adjuncts P 0.10, both carried by one or two signs). LB is at or below chance throughout. Strong sign-commodity ties exist (NI->VIN, *304->OLIV, *308->OLE, *306->CYP, I->GRA), but 1 of the 6 BH-significant cells is picture-consistent, against about 1.5 expected by chance. Surviving picture-consistent alignments, all grade C: OLE+KI (vessel sign inside the oil logogram; 1 type, 21+ tokens, HT); TE (branch-like) before GRA 5/11 and VIN 4/11; SI (sprout-like) before CYP 5/6, a Khania list habit. | REJECTED at the strength we could detect. In Linear A use, the depicted object of a syllabic sign does not predict the commodity beyond chance. Linear A behaves like Linear B here: pictures are not doing semantic work in administrative words. A weak residue (<30% of words) cannot be excluded. Not cracked. |
| la6:LA-6.1a | Parser: entries are cut at every word (except NI = figs) and at label logograms; commodity logograms carry over to bare numbers; single-sign *304/*308 'words' followed by a number count as commodities; quantities after a bare KU-RO number form a 'post' entry. Linear A: 123 entries with 2+ commodities (20 pairs with n >= 4). Linear B (DAMOS) is the positive control at document level (PY Ab, KN Fs etc. are single-entry tablets); 167 multi-commodity documents, 25 pairs. Units are converted with the conventional ratios (dry 1 = 10 T = 60 V = 240 Z; liquid 1 = 3 S = 18 V = 72 Z). | Parse check: HT 14 gives GRA 30 / OLE 6 / *304 9 / OLIV 13 and GRA 45 / OLE 9 / *304 6 / OLIV 14; HT 27a gives the after-total package CYP 10 / NI 10 / VIN 7. | Data built. |
| la6:LA-6.1b | Ratio fit. For each pair, r = log(q_b/q_a). SD of r and HIT = the most distinct tablets whose ratio lies within +-10% of one common ratio. Null N1 'shuffle within commodity': each commodity's quantities drawn from its own corpus-wide pool (one- and many-commodity entries), 2,000 runs. Null N2: q_b permuted among the entries of that pair. | LB control: global HIT 166 vs N1 51.4 (P = 0.0005). It recovers the known rations: GRA:NI = 1:1 on 23 of 27 PY Ab tablets, MUL:NI ~1/3 (11), FAR:HORD 1:6 (10), HORD:VIN, OLE:VIN 2:1 (9 of 9), NI:OLE. LA: global HIT 50 vs N1 39.5 (P = 0.0005). SD is below the nulls for GRA:OLE, CYP:NI, *304:GRA, NI:VIN, GRA:NI and *304:OLE (P <= 0.04): bigger entries carry more of everything (a scale effect, not a rate). The best LA cross-tablet clusters are 3 tablets each and almost all at ratio 1 (CYP:NI on HT 27a, 94a, 99a; GRA:NI on HT 18, 90, KH 8; NI:VIN; *304:OLE). | The method works on Linear B. Linear A shows proportional scaling and repeated numbers, but no rate yet. See 1c. |
| la6:LA-6.1c | What drives the LA excess? (a) Exactly equal quantities in one entry vs N1. (b) Cross-tablet HIT with equal pairs removed. (c) Within-tablet agreement: pairs of entries on one tablet whose ratios match within 10%. (d) Power: plant b = a/2 (rounded to 1/4) in 25% or 50% of real LA pair-instances, then 100 runs at a 300-run null. Sensitivity: integers only (fractions dropped). | (a) Equal quantities: 23 of 196 pair-instances vs 5.5 (P = 0.0005); integers only 22 of 134 vs 8.5. (b) Without equal pairs the cross-tablet rate signal vanishes: 41 vs 38.7 (P = 0.22); integers only 33 vs 29.4 (P = 0.08). (d) Power: a planted 1:2 rate in 25% of instances is detected in 100 of 100 runs, so a corpus-wide rate the size of Linear B's would have been seen. (c) Within-tablet: 7 of 29 entry pairs agree vs 0.8 (P = 0.001). HT 14: GRA:OLE = 5:1 in both entries (30:6, 45:9). HT 123+124a: OLIV ~ 3.7 x *308 in all 4 entries (31:8E, 31J:8JE, 16:4A, 15:4E), integers 31:8, 31:8, 16:4, 15:4. | **No economy-wide exchange rate or standard ration ratio exists in Linear A at the power we have (upper bound: < 25% of pair-instances could follow any one rate).** The two real regularities are (1) a strong preference for equal amounts of two goods (x4 the null) and (2) tablet-local rates: one scribe applies one proportion within one tablet (HT 14 GRA:OLE 5:1, HT 123+124 OLIV:*308 ~4:1). This is the opposite of Linear B, where the same ratio recurs across 9 to 23 tablets. |
| la7:LA-7.1a | Blind coding. 100 syllabic signs (>=3 tokens inside words) were rendered from Noto Sans Linear A under random codes (G105..G995). No value, frequency or context was shown. Each glyph was coded as plant, vessel, tool, animal, body, building or abstract, with a weak flag, and the codes were frozen in data/la7_picture_codes.json before any context was opened. Limit: the coder knows the signary, so recognition cannot be fully excluded. | abstract 41, building 14, tool 14, plant 12, body 9, vessel 7, animal 3. 45 of the 100 calls are weak. Predictions fixed beforehand: plant -> grain/tree/spice; vessel -> liquid (OLE, VIN); animal -> HIDE/CAP etc.; body -> VIR. | Codes frozen. Only 31 signs fall in a predictive class (12 plant, 7 vessel, 3 animal, 9 body). |
| la7:LA-7.1b | LA test. Pairs: a word directly followed (same line, dividers skipped) by a commodity logogram, 200 pairs (GRA 78, CYP 36, OLE 24, VIN 22, VIR 21, AROM 7, OLIV 6, HIDE 5, CAP 1); 192 have a coded first sign. Statistic H = pairs whose first-sign class hits its predicted family; also MI(class; family). Null: permute picture labels among the 100 signs, class sizes kept (2,000 runs). Run on tokens, word-commodity types, single-sign words and multi-sign words, under full and strict coding (weak calls -> abstract). | Full coding: tokens H 23 vs 19.7 (P 0.33), MI P 0.60; types H 18 vs 16.5 (P 0.41); single-sign words 10 vs 5.5 (P 0.16); multi-sign 13 vs 14.7 (P 0.55). Strict coding: tokens 11 vs 7.8 (P 0.25); single-sign words 7 vs 2.1 (P 0.09); multi-sign 4 vs 5.5 (P 0.56). No MI test below P 0.36. | No effect. The single-sign-word excess (P 0.09 to 0.16) does not reach significance across about 16 LA tests. |
| la7:LA-7.1c | Linear B control. The same codes were carried to LB through AB numbers. 3,066 DAMOS word + ideogram pairs (animal 968, person 786, grain 603, cloth 330, liquid 159, flax 127, spice 74, tree 19); 2,457 have a coded first sign. Same statistics and null. LA was also re-run on AB-numbered signs only, for a like-for-like comparison. | LB tokens: H 81 vs 133 (P 0.87). Types: 67 vs 87 (P 0.74). Single-sign: 7 vs 16 (P 0.65). All MI P >= 0.21. LA, AB signs only: tokens 21 vs 14.8 (P 0.22); types 16 vs 12.2 (P 0.24). | LB shows no effect, and is even below chance. LA sits slightly above its null, but the LA-LB contrast is not significant on its own, because LA P is only 0.22. |
| la7:LA-7.1d | Power (planted control). On the real LA pairs, each token whose first sign is in a predictive class has its commodity redrawn from the predicted families with probability q. 60 replicates per q, 300-run permutation each. | Detected at P<0.05: q 0.1 0/60, q 0.2 6/60, q 0.3 34/60, q 0.5 60/60. | The test would see a picture effect if about 30% or more of predictive-class words obeyed it. An effect that weak or weaker cannot be excluded. Absence is an upper bound, not proof. |
| la7:LA-7.2a | Wider link. A word is paired with the first commodity logogram after it, before the next word (310 pairs, 296 coded). Same H/MI statistics and the same picture-label permutation (2,000 runs), full and strict coding. | Full: tokens H 29 vs 29.4 (P 0.49), types 21 vs 22.1 (P 0.55), MI P 0.37/0.68. Strict: tokens 12 vs 11.7 (P 0.42). | No effect. The slight cycle-1 excess disappears when n grows by half. |
| la7:LA-7.2b | Counter-intuitive slot: the LAST sign (as an Egyptian-style determinative), not the first. Adjacent and window links; LB last sign as control. | LA adjacent last: H 25 vs 20.0 (P 0.27); window last 32 vs 29.9 (P 0.39); MI P >= 0.30. LB last: 68 vs 101 (P 0.72). | No determinative-like picture slot either. |
| la7:LA-7.2c | Alignments. For each sign with >=3 tokens (first sign of multi-sign words, and single-sign words, separately), each commodity cell with x>=2 is tested with the hypergeometric upper tail, then BH over 67 cells. "Picture-consistent" means the frozen class predicts that family. | BH q<0.05: NI(single)->VIN 16/23 (exp 5.7; class tool), TA-(first)->AROM 3/3 (all TA-I-AROM, a glued ligature), *304->OLIV 4/11 (tool), I(single)->GRA 5/7 (coded vessel), SI(single)->CYP 5/6 (exp 1.4, q 0.035; coded plant, CONSISTENT), *308->OLE 3/3 (abstract). Weaker: TE(single)->GRA 5/11 (q 0.20; plant, consistent), *306->CYP 4/5 (body). Of the 6 cells at q<0.05, 1 is picture-consistent. With 31/100 signs predictive and predicted families covering about half the pairs, chance expectation is about 1.5. | Strong sign-commodity ties exist (NI, *304, *308, *306, I: single signs that are themselves commodity-like entries in lists), but their pictures do not predict the commodity. Only SI->CYP and TE->GRA fit, and that is the chance rate. |
| la7:LA-7.3a | LA adjuncts. Syllabic signs written inside a commodity logogram (OLE+KI, GRA+PA, VIR+KA ...): 189 tokens, 32 ligature types. Adjunct picture class vs base family; H/MI, picture-label permutation (5,000 runs), full and strict coding. | Picture-consistent: only OLE+KI (vessel adjunct on oil, 21 tokens; plus OLE+KI+U, OLE+KI+ME). Full: tokens H 23 vs 16.1 (P 0.32), types 1 vs 2.4 (P 0.90). Strict: tokens 23 vs 6.4 (P 0.10), types 1 vs 1.0 (P 0.59). MI is BELOW its null (P 0.81 to 0.998): adjunct pictures spread across commodities more evenly than random labels do. The most frequent adjuncts (U, PA, DI, TA, KA, KU on OLE/GRA/VIR) are all abstract shapes. | No effect. One type (OLE+KI) carries the whole token excess. |
| la7:LA-7.3b | LB control: the same codes on DAMOS ligatures (217 tokens, 33 types; TELA+TE, OLE+PA, SUS+SI, TELA+PU, CYP+O ...). There they are known sound abbreviations, so no picture effect is expected. | H 0 vs 5.3 (P 1.0); types 0 vs 0.9; MI P 0.47 to 0.84. | LB has no effect, as expected. LA does not differ from it in kind: one hit (OLE+KI) against zero. |
| la7:LA-7.3c | Spread check for the best word-level alignment, SI->CYP. By site, against SI's overall use and CYP's site mix. LB check: single-sign words before spices. | SI->CYP 4 Khania + 1 HT. SI as a one-sign word: 103 tokens at HT, of which only 1 precedes CYP. Khania holds 45/68 CYP pairs, so 4/7 Khania SI tokens next to CYP is about what Khania's commodity mix gives. In LB, SI never precedes a spice; ko (x7) does, the coriander abbreviation, which is a SOUND value. | SI->CYP is a Khania list habit, not a picture effect. Demote to C. |
| la5:LA-5.1a | Adjacency information beyond slot: held-out bits per sign saved by knowing the previous sign, over a position-class baseline (fit one half of the types, score the other), minus the same on P-shuffled types. Controls: LB, LBpers (language), HTS, ICD (designed codes), randID / slot (planted codes, expected 0). | LA +0.146 [0.038, 0.288]; LA_HT +0.100 [-0.022, 0.248]; LA_nonHT +0.107 [-0.016, 0.206]. LB +0.281 [0.179, 0.390]; LB_KN +0.252 [0.137, 0.358]; LB_PY +0.385; LBpers +0.319. PE +0.131. HTS +0.300; ICD +0.097. LA_randID -0.011, LA_slot -0.002, LB_randID -0.004, LB_slot +0.009. | LA has real adjacency structure (above every planted code), but about half of LB's. The statistic does NOT separate language from designed codes (HTS 0.300 sits with LB; ICD 0.097 sits with LA). Not decisive alone. |
| la5:LA-5.1b | Phonotactic gaps: share of ordered pairs among the 15 commonest signs never adjacent inside a word, minus G and minus P shuffles. | Excess over P: LA +0.021 [-0.026, 0.067]; LA_HT +0.013; LA_nonHT +0.014. LB +0.108 [0.058, 0.180]; LB_KN +0.083 [0.039, 0.142]; LBpers +0.114. PE +0.059; HTS +0.064; ICD +0.026. Planted codes -0.004 to +0.010. Over G: LA +0.034, LB +0.179, ICD +0.191 (its gaps are positional). | LA has no phonotactic gaps beyond position: 0.021, inside the planted-code band (upper bound 0.067), while all three LB sets have them (lower bound 0.039). Code-like on this item; LA and LB_KN CIs touch, so not a clean split. |
| la5:LA-5.1c | Closed edge sets: top-10 initial / final sign coverage of types minus frequency-matched iid strings (S-DARK-56.3d). | Final: LA +0.030 [0.001, 0.068]; LA_nonHT +0.005; LB +0.215 [0.170, 0.271]; LB_KN +0.249; LBpers +0.250; HTS +0.254; ICD +0.083; PE +0.085; LB_slot +0.216 (kept by construction); LA_randID -0.019. Initial: LA +0.083, LB +0.131, LBpers +0.106, HTS +0.033, PE -0.054. | LA has almost no closed final set (one-seventh of LB; disjoint CIs). Greek inflection gives LB its closed finals; a slot code keeps them too, so the item says "no suffix paradigm" in LA, not "code" by itself. |
| la5:LA-5.1d | Positional asymmetry (mean abs(P initial - P final) over signs with >= 5 tokens) minus G. | LA +0.131 [0.083, 0.184]; LB +0.168; LBpers +0.183; HTS +0.185; ICD +0.373; PE +0.134; randID +0.007; LA_slot +0.115. | Every real system has positional sign roles; LA sits with all of them. Not discriminating. |
| la5:LA-5.1e | Markov-2 nulls for the claimed structures (LA-2f affixes: share of types equal to another type plus one sign at the start / end; gaps; adjacency gain), observed minus order-2 regeneration fit on the same sample. | Every population, LB included, is BELOW its M2 regeneration on every item: suffix extension LA -0.150, LB -0.166, LBpers -0.166, HTS -0.131; prefix LA -0.034, LB -0.020; gain LA -0.65, LB -0.56. Raw extension shares: LA prefix 0.044 / suffix 0.049 vs LB 0.010 / 0.019. | The M2 null has no power at m = 371 (it re-creates and inflates local structure even for Greek, which certainly has morphology). LA's affix extensions (LA-2f) are not beyond Markov-2; neither are LB's. Structures beyond Markov-2 cannot be shown at this corpus size. |
| la5:LA-5.1 | Cycle verdict | On the three language-sequential items (adjacency beyond slot, gaps beyond slot, closed finals) LA is consistently weaker than every LB set: 0.52x, 0.2x and 0.14x of LB. Planted codes are 0 on all three; LA is above 0 only on adjacency. Designed codes (HTS) match LB on all three, so none of the generic items separates language from a hierarchical code. | Intermediate. LA admin types look like a weakly structured vocabulary: some local sequential structure, no gaps, no closed endings. Generic statistics cannot decide; a statistic tied to the sign VALUES is needed (cycle 3). Usage statistics next (cycle 2). |
| la5:LA-5.2a | Uniqueness (types / tokens), hapax share, Heaps exponent at n = 532. | Uniq: LA 0.772 [0.741, 0.803]; LB 0.666 [0.490, 0.791]; LB_KN 0.684 [0.636, 0.752]; PE 0.928. Hapax: LA 0.662, LB 0.553, LB_KN 0.550, PE 0.894. Heaps: LA 0.924 [0.907, 0.941], LB 0.881, LB_KN 0.885 [0.866, 0.910], PE 0.979. LA_relabel identical to LA within noise (0.763 / 0.921). | LA vocabulary is more open than LB's and less than PE's. Usage statistics are invariant to relabelling (planted code = LA), so they describe how often heads recur, not whether the heads are words or codes. Not decisive by construction. |
| la5:LA-5.2b | Uniqueness against a sign-bigram regeneration of the same tokens (LA-2d / S312 rule: names and IDs score near 1). | LA 0.922 [0.881, 0.983]; LB 0.658 [0.549, 0.769]; LB_KN 0.637 [0.588, 0.740]; PE 1.135. | LA admin words are close to a name/ID population (CI disjoint from LB_KN); LB admin text has a reused core vocabulary (pa-ro, ko-wo, o-na-to, to-so). LA lacks LB's repeated function-word layer. S312 caution: high uniqueness does not prove names, and a code would show the same. |
| la5:LA-5.2c | Reuse across sites: share of repeated types seen at >= 2 sites, divided by the same with site labels permuted over tokens (20 permutations). | LA 0.339 [0.221, 0.454]; LB 0.188 [0.103, 0.310]; PE 1.8 (Susa-only, uninformative). Relabelled LA 0.370. | LA heads travel between Cretan sites more than LB words travel between KN/PY/TH/MY (CIs overlap). Invariant to relabelling, so it cannot tell codes from words. 63% of LA tokens are Haghia Triada. |
| la5:LA-5.2d | Frame/content split: held-out unigram bits carried by word tokens vs frame tokens (logograms, numbers) on lines. | Word share of bits: LA 0.743 [0.726, 0.770]; LB 0.604 [0.552, 0.707]; LB_KN 0.614 [0.592, 0.652]. | LA lines are mostly word + number; LB lines carry more frame (ideogram + unit + number). A layout difference, not a code/language test. |
| la5:LA-5.2e | Product-code signature: held-out bits a word saves on the NEXT logogram (word -> commodity), over the logogram unigram. Codes for products or account heads should predict the commodity. | LA 0.067 [-0.106, 0.229] (98 pairs per half); LB 0.363 [0.098, 0.823]; LB_KN 0.317 [0.122, 0.551]. | Against the product-code arm: LA words do not predict the commodity that follows (upper bound 0.23 bits), while LB words do (series-bound vocabulary). LA admin words behave like holder or place designations, not commodity codes. |
| la5:LA-5.2f | Word-internal vs boundary predictability on running lines: held-out bits saved by the previous sign inside words (beyond position class) minus across a word boundary (last sign -> first sign of the next word). A concatenated random ID gives about 0. | LA +0.324 [0.133, 0.544] (internal 0.222, boundary -0.103; 72 boundary pairs per half); LB +0.533 [0.207, 1.224]; LB_KN +0.657 [0.338, 1.075]. | LA words are cohesive units (internal > boundary, lower bound 0.13), like LB, at about half LB_KN's strength (CIs overlap). Same picture as LA-5.1a. |
| la5:LA-5.2 | Cycle verdict | Usage items cannot decide (relabel-invariant). LA is more name/ID-like in repetition (0.92 vs 0.64) and has no commodity-predicting words (0.07 vs 0.32 bits). Internal cohesion is present at half LB strength. | Product-code arm rejected (LA-5.2e). Account-head / personal-mark arm still open: generic statistics put LA between LB and planted IDs. Decisive test must use sign values (cycle 3). |
| la6:LA-6.2a | For every quantity after a commodity (entries and heads, totals excluded; 1,505 LA quantities, 2,084 LB): share with a fraction sign, share with integer part <= 1, share of integers >= 5 that are multiples of 5, share of integers >= 10 that are multiples of 10, median. Null: commodity labels permuted across all quantity tokens, 5,000 runs, two-sided P. Positive control: Linear B, where counted goods (VIR, MUL, OVIS, SUS, BOS, TELA, CAP) must never take a sub-unit and measured goods must. | LB control: every counted good 0% sub-units (P <= 0.03 each); measured goods 24-100% (GRA 74%, HORD 94%, OLE 78%, FAR and AES 100%). The test separates counted from measured. LA: VIR 2/35 = 6% fractions (P = 0.007 low; the two are HT 93a VIR 10F and an ambiguous carry-over on KH 9), *305 0/10, HIDE 2/15 (both L L on PH): these behave as counted. Measured goods split into three bands: (i) **CYP 72% fractional, 70% at or below 1, median 0** (P < 0.001 both) and *308 9/9 fractional: a large unit used for small amounts; (ii) NI 46% fractional (P = 0.004), OLE 33%, VIN 32%, *304 26%, OLIV 27%: medium; (iii) **GRA only 18% at or below 1 (P < 0.001), median 15**, 22% fractions: a unit small relative to a typical entry. Rounding: no commodity rounds to 5 or 10 more than the pool; bare numbers (no commodity) round more (x5 57% vs 52%, P = 0.011); CYP rounds less (P = 0.013); OLIV x10 1/10 (P = 0.048, one of ~50 tests). | Unit structure is readable from numbers alone: VIR, *305, HIDE are counted; CYP and *308 are a 'precious' class measured in a big unit (amounts mostly below 1); GRA is a bulk good whose unit is small against the amounts. **Opposite of Linear B, where GRA is the big-unit good (74% sub-units).** So the LA GRA unit is not the LB GRA unit, or LA grain entries are much larger. Rounding to 5/10 is not commodity-specific (no unit-size signal from rounding). |
| la6:LA-6.3a | Zones on the 35 tablets with a KU-RO or PO-TO-KU-RO: entries before the first total, the total, entries after it. Count, per commodity, entries in the after zone. Null: zone labels permuted among the entries of each tablet (keeps each tablet's composition and its number of after-entries), 10,000 runs. | Before the total: VIR 8, GRA 5, OLIV 5, *308 4, AROM 3, plus *86, *305 and bare numbers (counted lists). After the total: NI 7 (expected 3.7, P = 0.004), CYP 6 (3.75, P = 0.045), VIN 4 (1.9, P = 0.023), VIR 2 (4.2, P = 1.0). GRA, OLIV, *308, AROM never after (0 of 17; upper bound < 3/17). The after-zone package is CYP + NI + VIN (+ OLE once), labelled SA-RA2 or unlabelled: HT 27a, 89, 94a, 100, 130 (and HT 110a NI). On HT 27a, 89, 94a, 100 the list before the total is VIR / *86 / *305 (counted people or animals). | **A two-stage flow: a counted list (persons, *86, *305) is totalled, then a small package of CYP, NI and VIN is written against that total.** GRA, OLIV and *308 are summed in their own lists and never appear as an add-on. |
| la6:LA-6.3b | Levy test: is the after-total package proportional to the total? Spearman rho between the KU-RO number and the package amount, exact permutation P (n = 4 or 5, so the smallest possible P is 1/24). | CYP: HT 89 2.75 of 87 (3.2%), HT 100 5.25 of 97 (5.4%), HT 94a 5.4 of 110 (4.9%), HT 27a 10.1 of 335 (3.0%); rho = 1.0, P = 0.042. NI: rho 0.6, P = 0.18, share 2.5-75% (HT 110a NI may include a separate 60). VIN: n = 3, not testable. | CYP ~ 1/30 to 1/18 of the counted total on all 4 tablets (grade C, n = 4). Would support: HT 130 (CYP 8 after a KU-RO with the number lost) had a total of about 150-270; HT 30 (SA-RA2 CYP 14J with no total) belongs to a list of about 270-480. Would kill: a clear tablet where CYP after a KU-RO is far outside 2-6% of it. NI and VIN are not proportional. |
| la6:LA-6.3c | What the first KU-RO counts: entries before it summed by commodity and in all; match within 1. (Descriptive; the entry-cutting differs from totals_test.py, which remains the reference.) | 11 of 35 match the sum of all entries before the total, 1 matches one commodity (HT 123+124a OLIV 93J; the *308 column is separate), 23 do not (two-sided tablets, damage, or totals over several sections). | Nothing new beyond FINDINGS 2a: KU-RO sums one list. The only commodity-specific total is OLIV on HT 123+124a. |
| la6:LA-6.3d | Heading commodity: a main logogram on the first line with no number, against the commodities quantified in the body. Null: headings permuted across the 20 tablets that have one, 10,000 runs; statistic = headings whose commodity is absent from the body. | 12 of 20 headings are absent from the body vs 16.0 expected (P = 0.017): headings match the body more than chance, but most still do not. VIN heads lists of GRA/OLE/*304/OLIV (HT 14) and GRA/VIR (HT 62+73), and AROM-type lists (HT 9a); VIR heads OLE/OLIV (HT 58) and HIDE (PH 12a). | A heading logogram is often not the good being counted. VIN as a heading over a mixed food list (HT 14) is consistent with a heading for the purpose or destination, not the content (grade C). n = 20. |
| la6:LA-6.4a | Fingerprint of each entry word on >= 3 tablets (63 words; 1,008 labelled entries): residual log quantity (minus its commodity's mean), commodity profile, fraction share, multi-commodity share, after-total share. Word identity in the numbers: F = between-word / within-word variance of the residual. Null: quantities shuffled within commodity class (bare numbers a class), 2,000 runs. Per-word two-sided P. | With totals: F = 2.48 vs 1.01, P = 0.0005. Without KU-RO / KI-RO / PO-TO-KU-RO: F = 1.27 vs 1.00, P = 0.094. Per word (P < 0.05, 7 of 63 where 3.2 are expected): KU-RO x12 the typical amount (P = 0.001); A-DU x4.8 (0.023); E x2.7 (0.026); TE x2.0 (0.029, 20 tablets); TU x0.3 (0.033); WA x0.25 (0.046); KU-PA x0.2 (0.015). SA-RA2 x1.1 (P = 0.69), but the most multi-commodity word (NI 10, OLE 10, GRA 8, VIN 7). | Outside the totals, words carry little numeric identity beyond their commodity. The numbers grade only a few words (see the final grades). |
| la6:LA-6.4b | Functional synonyms: Euclidean distance on standardised fingerprints; for each pair of words P = share of 300 label-shuffle runs in which the pair is as close. | 88 of 1,953 pairs at P < 0.01 against about 20 by chance. The tight pairs are all 'bare-number list words' with similar magnitudes (DU ~ SI-PI-KI ~ PA-DE, TA-NA-TI ~ KU-PA3-NU, ZU, QA-*310-I, *324-DI-RA); most share 0-1 tablets. | One real functional class, 'entries in a bare-number list' (person-like lines counted without a logogram). The numbers cannot split it into offices or synonyms. No commodity-word synonym pair. |
| la6:LA-6.4c | Converters: a word whose quantity is a fixed multiple of the entry before it on the same tablet. HIT = distinct tablets within +-10% of one ratio; null: both quantities drawn within commodity, 2,000 runs. Check: exact repeats of the previous quantity against the corpus-wide repeat rate (146 of 656 = 0.223), binomial with Bonferroni over 42 words. | Raw HIT hits: MI-NU-TE, DI-DE-RU, KU-PA3-NU, KA, SA-RU (P 0.001-0.012), all at ratio 1.0 (they repeat the amount above). Against the corpus repeat rate: DI-DE-RU 3/3 (P = 0.011, Bonferroni 0.46), KU-PA3-NU 4/7 (0.048, Bonferroni 1). KI-RO has no fixed ratio to the entry above (P = 0.69; ratios 0.0-8). | **No converter word.** The 'hits' are the general habit of writing equal amounts in a row. KI-RO is not a fixed share of the entry above it. |
| la6:LA-6.4d | Fixed allotments: does the same word (2+ signs) get the same amount on different tablets? 73 words on >= 2 tablets; a match = equal first quantity (same class) on two tablets. N1: quantities shuffled within commodity; N2: shuffled within tablet (keeps each tablet's standard amount), 2,000 runs. LB control: first word of a document and its first amount. | 17 words with a repeat (26 tablet pairs) vs N1 13.1 (P = 0.11) and N2 16.6 (P = 0.52). The visible case is the HT 86a / 95a / 95b dossier: DA-ME, MI-NU-TE, KU-NI-SU, DI-DE-RU, SA-RU with GRA 10 each on HT 95a/b and 20 each on HT 86a (HT 86a = 2 x HT 95b for all 5 shared names). The within-tablet null explains it fully: the tablet sets the amount, not the name. LB control is also weak (12 vs 8.3, P = 0.067), so this test has low power. | No word-specific ration across the corpus. Amounts are set per tablet (a standard of 10 with doubling between tablets in one dossier), the same 'tablet-local rate' found in LA-6.1c. |
| la6:LA-6.final | Economy from numbers only (loops/la6_cycle1-4.txt, tools/la6_*.py): commodity-pair ratio fit (shuffle-within-commodity and within-pair nulls, planted-rate power, Linear B positive control); unit and rounding rates per commodity; position relative to KU-RO (within-tablet zone permutation); word fingerprints, converters and allotments (within-commodity and within-tablet nulls). | LB control recovers known rations (GRA:NI 1:1 on 23 PY Ab tablets; HIT 166 vs 51). LA: no cross-tablet rate beyond equal amounts (41 vs 38.7, P = 0.23; power 100% at 25%). Equal amounts x4 the null; tablet-local rates (HT 14 GRA:OLE 5:1, HT 123+124 OLIV ~3.7 x *308). Counted goods (VIR, *305, HIDE), a large-unit class (CYP 72% fractional, *308), bulk GRA (median 15). A CYP+NI+VIN package follows person totals (P 0.004-0.045); CYP is 3-5% of the total on 4 of 4 tablets (P = 0.042). Words carry no numeric identity outside totals (P = 0.094); no converter word. | No economy-wide prices or ration ratios. The structure is tablet-local proportions, totalled counted lists, and a small CYP/NI/VIN allowance per total. Grades: SA-RA2 B (allowance label), CYP B (large unit), CYP levy C, TE/A-DU C (headings). All from Hagia Triada. Not cracked. |
| la5:LA-5.3a | Vowel echo: share of adjacent valued pairs inside a word with the same vowel, divided by the P-shuffle expectation. | LA 0.930 [0.849, 1.011]; LA_HT 0.925; LA_nonHT 0.926; LA_noLB 0.931; LA_valued 0.923. LB 1.281 [1.183, 1.392]; LB_KN 1.238; LB_PY 1.315; LBpers 1.287. Codes 0.976 to 1.022. Full sets: LA ratio 0.932, z -1.50 (p_lo 0.067); LB 1.265, z +13.9; LB_KN z +7.6. | LB has strong vowel echo (its dead-vowel spelling of clusters: ko-no-so). LA has none (slight avoidance, within the code band). LA does not follow LB's cluster-spelling rule. Code-like on this item, or a different orthography / syllable structure. |
| la5:LA-5.3b | Consonant echo: same for the consonant series (pure vowels excluded). | LA 0.631 [0.503, 0.792]; LA_HT 0.557; LA_nonHT 0.656; LA_noLB 0.636; LA_valued 0.641. LB 0.373; LB_KN 0.404; LB_PY 0.363; LBpers 0.364. Codes: LA_randID 1.083, LA_slot 0.921, LB_randID 1.027, LB_slot 0.987 (band 0.751 to 1.389). Full sets: LA 0.624, z -4.13, p 0.0005; LA_HT z -3.66; LA_nonHT 0.665, z -2.33, p 0.0095; LA_noLB z -3.84; LB z -18.7; codes z -1.0 to +0.9. | LA avoids putting two signs of the same consonant series side by side, as LB and LB names do (dissimilation), and no planted code does. Holds at non-HT sites and without LA words shared with LB. Language-like, at about 60% of LB strength. |
| la5:LA-5.3c | Pure vowels word-initial: initial rate of A E I O U over other valued signs, divided by the same for random 5-sign sets. | LA 2.270 [2.045, 2.903]; LA_nonHT 2.559; LB 2.594; LB_KN 2.643; LBpers 2.409. randID 1.01 / 1.03; but slot codes keep it (LA_slot 2.363, LB_slot 2.497). | LA matches LB exactly, but a slot code (vowel signs used as prefixes, cf. LA-2f A-, I-) reproduces it. Not discriminating. |
| la5:LA-5.3d | Kober alternations: type pairs (>= 3 signs) differing only in the final sign; share of the two finals that share a consonant series, over random pairs from the same pool. | LA 0.918 [0, 2.763] (40 pairs); LB 3.592 (825 pairs); LB_KN 4.076 [2.224, 8.652]; LBpers 3.155; codes 0.45 to 2.26. | LA has no inflection grid (no consonant-sharing final alternations), unlike LB. With 40 pairs the band is wide (n too small to exclude a weak grid). Agrees with LA-5.1c (no closed endings). |
| la5:LA-5.3 | Cycle verdict | Of four value-tied items, one separates language from code cleanly (consonant avoidance) and LA falls on the language side. LA lacks LB's vowel echo and Greek-style inflection grid. | LA admin words carry a phonological constraint keyed to the conventional consonant values. The code arm is contradicted on this item. |
| la5:LA-5.4a | Calibration: for 8 form and value statistics, the gap between the language band (LB, LB_KN, LB_PY, LBpers) and the code band (planted randID and slot codes on LA and LB; HTS, ICD where defined), in mean CI half-widths; LA's position f = (LA - code) / (language - code). | Only two statistics separate the bands: consonant avoidance +1.06 half-widths (language 0.250 to 0.572, code 0.751 to 1.389) and vowel echo +0.12 (barely). Overlapping: gaps beyond slot -1.80, vowel-initial -2.85, Kober -2.85, adjacency gain -3.11, closed finals -3.21, positional asymmetry -8.68. LA on consonant avoidance: f = 0.59 [0.34, 0.80]; HT 0.71; non-HT 0.55 [0.23, 0.89]; noLB 0.59; valued-only 0.58. LA on vowel echo: f = -0.28 [-0.57, 0.02] (code side). | The deciding statistic is consonant-series avoidance. LA's CI (0.503 to 0.792) lies under the code band's mean and lower edge (0.751) for most of its range, and it never reaches the code means (0.92 to 1.08). Vowel echo barely separates the bands; it marks LB's spelling rule, which LA lacks. |
| la5:LA-5.4b | Artefact checks on the full type sets (50 cached P-shuffles): (i) drop identical-sign doublings; (ii) specificity: the same ratio for 500 random partitions of the valued signs into classes of the real consonant-series sizes. | LA: real partition 0.626, random partitions median 0.926 [0.721, 1.127], 0/500 as low; without doublings 0.580 vs 0.987, 0/500. LA_nonHT: 0.692, 16/500 (p 0.03); without doublings 0.633, 13/500 (p 0.026). LB_KN 0.391 and LBpers 0.359, 0/500. LA_randID 1.104 (94th percentile), LA_slot 0.936 (45th): not special. | The avoidance is not mark-repetition avoidance (it survives without doublings), and it is specific to the conventional consonant classes. A code using the signs as marks would show it for no partition in particular. Replicated on held-out non-HT sites. |
| la5:LA-5.4 | Cycle verdict | Language-like on the one statistic that separates calibrated language from calibrated code. Code-like or weaker on the generic items (gaps, closed finals, no vowel echo, no inflection grid, more ID-like reuse). | The arrow fails at its core: LA admin words are written phonetically, with a consonant-dissimilation constraint keyed to the conventional values. They are not arbitrary codes. They are a less inflected, more name-like vocabulary than LB's. Not cracked. |
| la5:LA-5.final | One battery, the same code for every population: LA admin words (742 types / 1,065 tokens), LB admin words (DAMOS; positive language control, same tablet layout), LB personnel names, Proto-Elamite middles, HTS and ICD-10 designed codes, and planted codes (random IDs and slot codes on the LA and LB sign sets). Half-sample CIs. Nulls: global, position-class and order-2 Markov. Items: adjacency gain, phonotactic gaps, closed edge sets, positional asymmetry, affix extension vs Markov-2, uniqueness, Heaps, bigram-uniqueness ratio, cross-site reuse, frame/content bits, word -> commodity gain, internal vs boundary predictability, vowel echo, consonant avoidance, vowel-initial rate, Kober alternations. Calibration: language band vs code band. | Deciding statistic: same-consonant-series adjacency within words over a position-class shuffle. Language 0.36 to 0.40 (LB, LB_KN, LB_PY, LBpers); codes 0.92 to 1.08 (band 0.75 to 1.39). LA 0.631 [0.503, 0.792], z -4.1, 0/500 random partitions as extreme; non-HT 0.656 (p 0.01; partition p 0.03). It survives removing doublings (0.580) and LB-shared words (0.636). Elsewhere LA is weaker than LB: gaps beyond slot 0.021 vs 0.108; closed finals 0.030 vs 0.215; no vowel echo (0.93 vs 1.28); no inflection grid; more ID-like repetition (bigram ratio 0.92 vs 0.64). Product-code arm: LA words carry 0.07 [-0.11, 0.23] bits about the next commodity, vs 0.32 for LB_KN. The Markov-2 null has no power at this n (it beats every population, LB included). Usage items cannot decide, because relabelling leaves them unchanged. | REJECTED (the arrow). LA administrative words behave like LB on the one calibrated discriminator, at about 60% of LB strength [34-80%]: they are written phonetically (language-encoded names or terms), not as codes. They differ from LB Greek in being uninflected-looking and name-heavy. Caveats: the values are LB-derived (backward projection); 63% of tokens are from HT. Not cracked. |
| la8:LA-8.1a | Planted control, strict DL (every lexicon type pays log2 K bits for its class label). PFLAT is a known flat form: HEAD (5, closed), NAME (open), LOGO (6, closed), NUM, FRAC, TOTAL (2, closed). 1 seed, 2,500 generations, 80,000 evaluations. | Evolved K=5 (truth 6), 1 open class (truth 1). ARI vs the true classes = 0.953 (token-weighted). Fitness 20,302 vs 20,649 for the true genome, so the GP beat the generator's own partition: it merged TOTAL into HEAD. 1 loop, NAME -> NUM -> NAME. | PASS: the method recovers a planted form. |
| la8:LA-8.1b | Planted control, strict DL. PREC is a small language with centre-embedding relative clauses: DET, REL, P closed; N and V open (suffix-marked); NUM. Same compute. | K=5 (truth 6), open 3 (truth 3), ARI 0.933, fitness 29,381 vs truth 29,449. | PASS: open classes and suffix-keyed classes are recovered. |
| la8:LA-8.1c | Real corpora, strict DL, equal compute (seed 1; PE and LAS stopped at 1,450 and 1,350 generations when the run was switched to v2, both stable since about gen 300). | LA K=2: {words + logograms, OPEN} and {NUM, FRAC + a few W1}. LB K=3. PE K=2. LAS K=2. Held-out bits/token: LA 7.88, LAS 8.12, LB 9.19, PE 6.54. | Under strict DL, about 4,000 tokens cannot pay for more than 2-3 classes in any real corpus. The order signal is real: LA beats its own shuffle by 931 bits of fitness (0.24 bits/token), and the transition cost is 1.22 vs 0.80 bits/token for LAS, which spends more on emission. But strict DL is too coarse to compare machines, so cycle 2 uses a kind-conditional label code. |
| la9:LA-9.1a | Data. LA: 30 KU-RO sections (totals_test.py cut rule), 7 with fraction letters (A, E, F, H, J, JE, L2). LB control: 27 to-so/to-sa sections from DAMOS, 12 with capacity sub-units (dry T, Vd, Zd; liquid S, Vl, Zl; truth 1/10, 1/60, 1/240; 1/3, 1/18, 1/72). Ur III control: 871 undamaged szunigin grain sections from the CDLI ATF dump (no '[', 'x', '...' or '$ broken' in the section), 814 with sub-units barig, ban2, sila3 (truth 1/5, 1/30, 1/300 gur). Exact integer arithmetic in units of 1/14400. | Exact under the true values: Ur III 376/871 (43%), LB 2/27 (7%). LA: 9/30 with integers only, 9/30 with lineara.xyz values, 8/30 with the attack-1 binary set. Only 10 LA sections can balance under ANY of 49^7 grid assignments (the 9 integer-exact ones plus HT 9a); 20 LA sections are 'pure errors'. | Data built. The controls bracket LA: Ur III is large and often exact, LB is LA-sized and rarely exact. |
| la9:LA-9.1b | Discrepancy classification of each LA mismatch under three candidate value sets (integers only, lineara.xyz, attack-1 binary). A mismatch counts as explained by a mechanism if that mechanism gives P > 1e-3: skip/double a line, dropped written carry, counting-board slip +-10^k, tally +-1..3, rounding/ignored fractions, misread fraction sign. | Same picture under all three sets. Integer gaps: +2 (HT 9a), +4 (HT 11a, HT 100), -16 (HT 25b = a skipped line), +13, +72, +42, -1 (HT 94a), +4, -10 (HT 102: slip, skip or carry), +1 (HT 104, HT 119), +121, +79, -20 (HT 118: dropped carry), +9, +50, +93, -68, -7, -4, +75. 8 of 21 mismatches are big gaps (|D| >= 42) that no arithmetic slip explains (lost lines or wrong section cut). Fraction-only gaps: HT 13 (-1/2 lineara.xyz; -5/16 binary: a misread sign under both), HT 104 (+1 integer, balanced by 2J = 1 under lineara.xyz). | The mismatches are a mix; no single mechanism stands out by eye. Of 21 mismatches only ~6 have an arithmetic-slip reading (+-1, -10, -16, -20), and 3 of those also fit a skipped line. |
| la9:LA-9.1c | Gibbs fit (4 chains x 600 sweeps; Ur III 2 x 120) of letter values (49-value grid, uniform prior) + Dirichlet(1) weights over exact/skip/double/carry/slip/tally/round/misread/background (BG density 1e-3). Positive controls: Ur III and LB with values hidden; recovery = posterior mode equals truth. 'Errors only': only sections that cannot balance under any grid assignment are kept, so the values can be learnt from the mistakes alone. | Ur III all (871): barig 1/5, ban2 1/30, sila3 1/300 all recovered, posterior mass 1.00 each. Ur III errors only (334): all 3 recovered (mass 1.00, 1.00, 0.99), mostly through 'skip' (17% of the errors: total = sum minus one line) and slips. LB all (27): 1 of 4 letters (Vl 1/18, mass 0.35); T, Vd, S wrong (truth ranks 9, 9, 22); chains agree 0.5. LB errors only (20): 0 of 4. LA all: no letter concentrates beyond J (top 2/3 0.16, 7/12 0.14, 1/2 0.13) and E (5/6 0.15, 2/3 0.13, 3/4 0.10); A, F, H, JE, L2 stay at the prior (0.02-0.03); chains agree 0.25. LA errors only: all 7 letters at the prior. BG 1e-2: J moves to 1/2 (0.25); BG 1e-4: as BG 1e-3. LA mechanism weights: exact 0.25, background 0.31, tally 0.15, skip 0.07, carry 0.05, misread 0.05, round 0.05, slip 0.04, double 0.03. | The machinery CAN read values from errors alone when there are hundreds of erroneous sections (Ur III). At LA/LB size it cannot: LB (27 sections, known values) is not recovered, and LA gives no stable value for any letter. LA mechanism weights are spread (no method > 0.15). Cycle 2 tests mechanisms by simulation (ABC); cycle 3 measures power at LA size. |
| la9:LA-9.2a | ABC on Linear A, 3,000,000 draws, 1,500 accepted (eps 1.93). Null: totals permuted across sections, 4 runs (2 x 1M, 2 x 0.5M). | Fit: real LA eps 1.93; shuffled eps 7.7-9.3 (the error model reproduces the real discrepancies, not shuffled ones). Mechanism posterior (prior 0.11 each): lacuna 0.32, round 0.12, carry 0.10, tally 0.09, exact 0.09, misread 0.09, double 0.07, skip 0.06, slip 0.06. Values: the only letter above 2x prior is J, mode 1/2 at 2.65x prior; shuffled runs also put J at 1/2, at 2.6-5.5x prior. All other letters 1.4-1.9x prior. | The real mistakes look like ordinary scribal error plus lost lines (eps in the planted range, below the shuffled range). No arithmetic mechanism rises above its prior. J = 1/2 is a structural attractor of the entries (it also wins on shuffled totals), not evidence. |
| la9:LA-9.2b | Positive controls with known values. LB: 27 to-so sections, 2M draws. Ur III: 4 LA-sized subsamples (22 integer-only + 8 fraction sections), 0.5M each; 1 larger subsample (120 sections), 0.4M. | LB: 0 of 4 letters recovered (truth ranks 12-42, mass at truth 0.7-0.9x prior). Ur III LA-sized: 0 of 12 letter-runs recovered (truth ranks 3-36, 0.6-2.4x prior). Ur III 120 sections: barig 1/5 recovered (3.2x prior), ban2 and sila3 not (ranks 19, 12). | Summary-statistic ABC cannot read fraction values at LA size even when they are known, and only starts to at ~100+ sections. It is not a value test for Linear A. |
| la9:LA-9.2c | Planted mechanism test on the REAL LA entries: totals simulated with lineara.xyz-like values and p = 0.4 on one mechanism (+ exact 0.3, lacuna 0.3), 6 mechanisms x 0.5M draws. Recovery = planted mechanism has the top posterior weight. | Top-ranked: carry (0.20), tally (0.23), round (0.26) yes; slip 2nd (0.17, round 0.25 above), misread 2nd (0.21, exact 0.27 above), skip 4th (0.12). 3 of 6 recovered, posterior weight shrunk from 0.4 to 0.12-0.26. Planted values: J = 1/2 top in 4 of 6 (it is also top on shuffled data); every other letter recovered in at most 1 of 6 runs (E, JE, L2 once each). Ur III subsamples give carry 0.16-0.21 and misread 0.15-0.19, unlike LA (0.10, 0.09). | At LA size the ABC can just about tell a dominant (40%) carry, tally or rounding habit, and not slip, skip or misread. Real LA shows no such dominant habit: every arithmetic mechanism sits at 0.06-0.12 (prior 0.11); only lacuna rises (0.32). Upper bound (for the 3 mechanisms the test can see): no dropped-carry, tally or rounding habit at the planted 40% level; slip, skip and misread cannot be judged. |
| la9:LA-9.3a | Power at LA size with KNOWN values. (i) Ur III, 8 subsamples of 22 integer-only + 8 fraction sections (LA's shape). (ii) Ur III errors only, 4 subsamples of 30 sections that cannot balance under any grid values. (iii) LB bootstrap, 4 resamples of the 27 sections. (iv) Planted: totals simulated on the REAL LA entries with lineara.xyz-like values (J 1/2, E 1/4, JE 3/4, A = H 1/6, F 1/8, L2 3/20) and a mixed error habit (exact 0.3, lacuna 0.3, 7 mechanisms share 0.4), 4 runs. Hit = posterior mode equals truth (chance 1/49 = 0.02). | (i) 5 of 24 letter-runs (21%); all three right in 1 of 8 subsamples; barig in 3 of 8. (ii) 3 of 12 (25%). (iii) 1 of 16 (6%; Vl 1/18 once). (iv) 5 of 28 (18%): J 4 of 4, A 1 of 4, E, F, H, JE, L2 0 of 4. Wrong runs are confident: mean modal mass 0.35 (Ur III), 0.45 (errors only), 0.44 (LB), 0.14 (planted LA). | At LA size the method finds the right value for about 1 letter in 5, and it is often confidently wrong. Even with values planted on the real LA entries, only J (the commonest letter) comes back. Any LA value it gives is a guess at C at best. |
| la9:LA-9.3b | Real LA vs null. Totals permuted across the 30 sections, 8 runs. Statistics: mean modal posterior mass over the 7 letters; log-likelihood at the posterior mode. | Real LA: modal mass 0.061 (prior 0.020), log-lik -143.8. Shuffled: modal mass 0.034-0.070 (P = 0.125 for >= real), log-lik -195.5 to -212.2 (P < 1/8 for >= real). Real LA modes: J 1/2, E 3/4, A 1/2, F 1/4, JE 1/3, H 1/360, L2 5/8 (chains agree 0.25 in cycle 1; posterior mass <= 0.16 each). Shuffled runs give J 1/2 in 3 of 8. | The real totals fit the error model much better than shuffled totals (log-lik), but only because real totals are near their sums. The value posterior is no more concentrated than under the null (P = 0.125). No LA fraction value is identified by the errors. |
| la9:LA-9.3c | Calculating-method fingerprint. Gibbs mechanism weights for real LA vs shuffled (8), planted LA (4) and Ur III LA-sized (8). Direct check: share of integer-only sections whose total misses the sum by 1-9 units (not a power of 10), LA vs LB vs Ur III. Fisher exact test. | Weights: LA tally 0.16, exact 0.24, skip 0.07, carry 0.05, slip 0.04, misread 0.05, round 0.05, bg 0.32. Ur III LA-sized: tally 0.04, skip 0.10, exact 0.46. Shuffled: tally 0.05, bg 0.72. Planted mixed habit: tally 0.085. Direct: small unit-level misses in integer-only sections LA 6/23, LB 6/15, Ur III 3/57 (LA vs Ur III P = 0.015; LB vs Ur III P = 0.002; Aegean 12/38 vs 3/57, P = 0.001). Decade-type misses (+-10, 100): LA 1/30, Ur III 12/871. Dropped carries: LA at most 1 clear case (HT 118, -20). | One real signal: Aegean totals (LA and LB alike) miss by a few units far more often than Ur III totals. It does not point to a carry-based method (no dropped-carry or decade-slip excess) and fits unit-level miscounting OR lost/extra unit strokes. Confound: my Ur III set excludes sections with broken numbers, LA and LB do not. Grade C: 'unit-stroke slips', not a calculating tool. |
| la9:LA-9.final | Three cycles (tools/la9_*.py). (1) Exact likelihood of each written KU-RO total under 9 error mechanisms (exact, skip line, double line, dropped written carry, counting-board slip +-10^k, tally +-j, rounding or ignoring fractions, misread fraction sign, background for lost lines or a wrong section cut), with a Gibbs sampler over hidden letter values (49-value grid) and the mechanism weights. (2) Rejection ABC: 3M simulated Linear A scribes plus 10.4M for controls and nulls, who add the real entries and write the total with fraction signs; 10 value-free summaries. (3) Controls: Ur III szunigin grain totals from CDLI (871 sections; barig 1/5, ban2 1/30, sila3 1/300 hidden), Linear B to-so sections (27; T, V, Z, S hidden), planted totals on the real LA entries, LA with totals shuffled. | The method works when there is a lot of data: from Ur III errors alone (334 sections that cannot balance) it recovers all three sub-unit values (posterior 0.99-1.00). At LA size it fails on known values: Ur III subsamples 5/24, LB 1/16 (bootstrap) and 1/4 (full), planted LA 5/28 (only J), often confidently wrong. LA: no letter value beats the shuffled-total null (modal mass 0.061 vs 0.034-0.070, P = 0.125); the ABC's J = 1/2 also appears with shuffled totals. Mechanisms: no carry, counting-board or rounding habit; at the planted 40% level the ABC would have seen carry, tally or rounding. The only signal: Aegean totals (LA 6/23 and LB 6/15 integer-only sections) miss by 1-9 units, against Ur III 3/57 (P = 0.001 pooled). | **The mistakes do not identify the Linear A fraction values or a calculating tool beyond the controls.** The bottleneck is size: about 20 erroneous LA sections, where Ur III needs hundreds. One lead at grade C: Aegean totals miss by a few unit strokes (miscounted or lost strokes), not by dropped carries. Confound: undamaged Ur III vs damaged LA/LB. Not cracked. |
| la8:LA-8.2a | Planted controls under v2 DL. | PFLAT: best K=6 = the true 6 classes (ARI 0.999), 1 open class (NAME, keyed by every last sign), fitness 19,812 vs 20,152 for the true genome; 3 seeds agree (ARI 0.95-1.0). PREC: K=5, 2 open (N keyed -A/-O; V keyed -TE/-SI/other), ARI 0.97, fitness 29,045 vs 29,053; DET merged with W1 and REL with P. | PASS. The GP recovers closed vs open slots and the suffix that keys each open class. |
| la8:LA-8.2b | Linear A admin, v2, 3 seeds. Shuffle control LAS (tokens permuted inside each document), same compute. | LA best fitness 30,972 (2 seeds identical, the third 30,999), K=4, 4 states, 2 open classes. Classes: C0 OPEN word class (W2 976, W1 437, L 67 tokens; KU-RO, TE, SI, SA-RA2, KA, PA, A; novel keys = all multi-sign last signs + W1), C2 OPEN commodity class (L 565, W1 219: NI, GRA, CYP, VIN, *304, OLIV, CYP+D, OLE+U; novel key L), C3 NUM, C1 FRAC. LAS: K=1-2 with the same tokens, fitness 32,103 (+1,131 bits, 0.29 bits/token worse). | Linear A order is real structure (LAS loses 1,131 bits). The evolved machine is a 4-slot loop: word or commodity -> NUM -> (FRAC) -> next word. Only two open classes, no closed function-word class. |
| la8:LA-8.2c | Linear B (DAMOS, size-matched), same compute; second independent draw LB2 (1 seed). | LB: K=4 (2 open: words / logograms; NUM; FRAC with metrological W1 adjuncts), seeds ARI 0.985-1.0. LB2: K=7, fitness lower per token, and it evolves a CLOSED class of 31 multi-sign formula words: o-na-to, to-so-de, ko-to-na, te-o-jo, e-ke, e-ke-qe, pa-ro, do-e-ra (300 tokens, P(next NUM) = 0.00), separate from the open name class. | Linear B can support a closed class of non-numbered formula words, but this showed up in one draw of two (it depends on the sample containing the Pylos E- land series). |
| la8:LA-8.2d | Proto-Elamite (sign-level), same compute; second draw PE2. | PE: K=3 in both draws: an OPEN sign class (M288, M054, M297, M066, M124...; P(next NUM) 0.54), a closed class that merges NUM with 13 signs (M388, M218, M371, M240, M377...), and a small class of 20 signs (M157 and variants, M305, M327) that sits at the start. | PE evolves into the flattest machine: item -> number with a header slot. |
| la8:LA-8.2e | Number attachment, counted directly (tools/la8_attach.py). For each kind, P(the next token is NUM), against 1,000 within-document shuffles. | LA: P(NUM after W2) 0.52 (shuffle 0.37, P<0.001), after W1 0.56, after L 0.62. LB (2 draws): after W2 0.07-0.08 (shuffle 0.16-0.20, P=1.0, i.e. words AVOID numbers), after L 0.71-0.76. PE: after a sign 0.48 (0.35-0.37). Before NUM: LA W2 38% / L 29% / W1 27%; LB L 77-83% / W2 11-17%. | Robust difference: in Linear B a number hangs on a logogram and words do not take numbers. In Linear A, as in Proto-Elamite, a number hangs directly on a word or sign: "word NUM" is the entry. Linear A writes counts the PE way, not the LB way. |
| la8:LA-8.2f | Search check: zoom runs that force K >= 8 (1,500 generations, 1 seed), compared with the free v2 optimum. | LA zoom 31,131, WORSE than LA's K=4 (30,972). LB zoom 36,381, BETTER than LB v2 (36,529). PE zoom 25,925, BETTER than PE v2 (25,956). LAS zoom 32,381, worse. | The GP stalled in a K=3-4 basin for LB and PE, but not for LA. Cycle 3 restarts every corpus from its K>=8 population without the constraint (v3), with equal compute. |
| la8:LA-8.3a | v3: each corpus is restarted from its forced K>=8 population, now with no constraint (1,500 generations, same for all). This tests whether the cycle-2 optima were local traps. | LA returns to exactly the cycle-2 optimum (30,971.9, K=4: the same 4 classes as 2 of the 3 v2 seeds). LB improves to 36,362 with K=7: open words; open logograms; a closed class of NUMBERED logograms (OVIS:m, VIR, *154, SA, CAP:f; 27 members, P(next NUM) 0.93); 2 closed classes of non-numbered formula words (to-so-de, o-na-to, ko-to-na, ka-ke-we / pe-mo, ke-ke-me-na, a-ta-ra-si-jo); NUM; FRAC. PE improves to 25,793 with K=4: open signs; a closed class of 71 numbered signs (M288, M054, M297...; P(next NUM) 0.89); a header class (M157 and variants); NUM. LAS stays K=1-2. | Linear A's 4-class machine is a real optimum, not a search failure. Linear B (both draws) and PE gain extra closed classes; Linear A does not. |
| la8:LA-8.3b | Recursion arm. Over each corpus's best classes, evolve slots (1-3 states per class) with or without a one-counter stack (push or pop on entering a state, depth <= 4, END needs an empty stack). Equal compute: 2 seeds x 300 generations x 16 offspring per arm. Planted: PREC with its true classes; PREC with its evolved classes; PFLAT as the false-positive control. | Stack gain (fsa minus pda fitness, bits): PREC true classes 737 (REL pushes, a V slot pops: the planted centre-embedding is found); PREC evolved classes 0 (DET and REL were merged into other classes, so the bracket cannot be seen); PFLAT 243 (FALSE POSITIVE: the counter is used as extra memory in a flat form); LA 63 (NUM pushes, a word slot pops: a counter, not an embedding); LB, LB2, PE, LAS 0. | The stack test cannot separate recursion from bounded memory: the flat planted form gains more (243) than Linear A (63). Linear A shows NO evidence of recursion above the flat-form level. Recursion is only detectable here when the closed function classes are already right, which no real corpus supplied. |
| la8:LA-8.3c | Evidence per assignment (tools/la8_margins.py): move each Linear A type with >= 3 tokens to the other open class and record the fitness loss. Positive = the evolved assignment wins. | Commodity class: 57 types, 48 with margin >= 2 bits, 0 misplaced. Strongest: CYP 66, GRA 32, CYP+D 30, NI 28, OLIV 22, MI+JA+RU 20, OLE+KI 19, *401+RU 18, OLE+MI 15, OLE+U 15, VIN 15, *304 12.5, *308 11.5. Weak (< 2 bits): JU, *131B, *560, *23M, E, *22M, KI. Word class: 98 types, 92 >= 2 bits, 0 misplaced. Strongest: A-DU 36, SA-RA2 36, TE 30, KU-RO 29, SI 22, KA-PA 20, RU 18. Damaged ligatures VIR+[?] and HIDE+[?] and *304+PA fall in the WORD class. | Two open functional classes are solid for Linear A: entry words (names and terms) and commodity markers. Single signs NI, *304 and *308 behave as commodity logograms, not as words. KU-RO, KI-RO, SA-RA2, A-DU and TE are not a separate closed formula class: they behave like ordinary numbered entry words. |
| la8:LA-8.final | GP over typed-slot probabilistic automata (word classes; open = admits novel words keyed by kind and last sign; 1-3 slots per class; Baum-Welch transitions). Fitness = 2-fold held-out bits + description length. Equal compute per corpus (240k evaluations, plus a 48k-evaluation escape restart). Corpora size-matched to Linear A (3,935 tokens). Controls: planted flat form (recovered: K=6/6, ARI 0.999), planted recursive language (recovered: open N/V keyed by their suffixes, ARI 0.97), within-document shuffle (LAS collapses to 1-2 classes and loses 1,131 bits), a second LB and PE draw, a forced K>=8 restart, and a stack arm with a flat-form false-positive control. | Best machines. LA: 4 classes, 4 states, 2 open (entry words; commodities), 0 closed word classes, one loop (word or commodity -> NUM -> (FRAC) -> next). LB: 7 classes, 2 open (words; logograms) plus closed numbered logograms and closed non-numbered formula words (to-so-de, o-na-to, ko-to-na, e-ke...). PE: 4 classes, 1 open sign class, a closed numbered-sign class, a header class. Recursion: no corpus beats the flat-form false positive (LA 63 bits vs PFLAT 243). Number attachment: in LA a number follows a word directly (P 0.52 after multi-sign words vs 0.37 shuffled), as in PE (0.48 vs 0.35-0.37); in LB words avoid numbers (0.07 vs 0.18) and numbers hang on logograms (0.71-0.76). | Linear A is closest to a SMALL FLAT FORM: a list template "entry-word or commodity + number (+ fraction)". It attaches numbers the Proto-Elamite way, and is smaller than Linear B's machine: it has no closed formula-word class and shows no recursion. At this size (about 4,000 tokens) Linear B's administrative tablets are also flat and also show no recursion, so this does NOT show that Linear A is not a language. It shows that the tablets are written as bare entries, without the separate counting logogram and formula-word layer seen in Linear B. Not cracked. |
| la11:LA-11.1a | Data. 39 languages from 18 families (UD treebanks, eBible.org, CDLI Sumerian ATF; data/la11/sources.json records URLs and licences). Raw texts were romanized, collapsed to 5 vowels and cut into open syllables. MIXED scheme: s or a sonorant before a consonant is dropped, other cluster consonants are written with the next vowel, final consonants are dropped. Only these are kept: syllable unigram and bigram counts over the word types of a training half, plus 1,500 held-out types recoded to opaque integers. Raw downloads were deleted. 35 languages have at least 1,000 training types and serve as targets; Hittite (143), Telugu, Amharic and Yoruba serve as inputs only. | Training types range from 1,207 (Akkadian) to 16,974 (Korean). Syllable inventories range from 72 to 174. | Corpus built. Hittite UD is too small (1,274 tokens) to be a target. |
| la11:LA-11.1b | Contest. Every input has 742 types, matched in length to the LA admin types. For each input and target, simulated annealing (C core, 6 restarts x 50k moves, then a greedy polish) finds the best injective map from the top 40 input symbols to the target's top 60 syllables, under the target's discounted syllable bigram model with word boundaries. Gap = bits/symbol of shuffled input minus bits/symbol of real input; shuffling permutes symbol tokens across types (8 shuffles for LA, 4 for LB, 2 for language inputs). z = gap standardized against the gaps of held-out real words from unrelated families scored under the same target. Inputs: LA; LB with identities hidden (3 length-matched subsamples); 3 random Markov strings; held-out words of all 38 languages. 5,145 anneals. | Power on a language's own words: self-identification top-1 in 34/35, top-3 in 35/35. With the language itself excluded, the best target falls in the input's own family 7/31 times (chance 4.3). **Linear B control fails:** Ancient Greek ranks 23, 21 and 32 of 35 by z (by raw gap, without standardization: 9, 5 and 8); Modern Greek ranks 34, 32 and 35 by z. LB's best targets are Sanskrit, Italian and Tamil (max z +0.6 to +1.0). LA: every z is negative. Top: Arabic -0.10, Coptic -0.12, Nahuatl -0.24, Sanskrit -0.28. Bottom: Finnish, Hungarian, Mandarin (-1.2 to -1.4). 32 of 38 real inputs, scored against unrelated families only, reach a higher maximum z (p = 0.85). Raw gap: LA 0.206 bits/symbol, LB 0.29-0.32, Markov 0.05-0.09. Convergence: restart spread 0.024 bits/symbol; the gap's s.d. over shuffles is 0.006. | **No language absorbs Linear A better than unrelated real words do.** The family-mean permutation (Afro-Asiatic p = 0.005) is driven by having 6 targets of mean -0.49, all below zero, so it carries no positive meaning. The method has power only when the input shares the model's orthography and corpus. It cannot recover Greek through Linear B spelling at LA size. A blind map-free contest therefore cannot name LA's language. LA's sequential structure sits between Markov noise and real words (gap 70% of LB's), which matches LA-5. |
| la11:LA-11.2a | The full contest again (5,145 anneals) with three changes. DROP scheme: only the consonant right before a vowel is written. M = K = 40: a bijection onto each language's top 40 syllables, so inventory size cannot help a language. Bigram counts are rescaled to 2,000 training types for every language, so corpus size cannot help either. Same inputs and controls: LA, 3 hidden-LB subsamples, 3 Markov strings, held-out words of 38 languages. | Self-identification top-1 in 34/35. Best relative (self excluded) is in the input's own family 9/31 times (chance 4.3). **LB control fails again:** Ancient Greek ranks 23, 17 and 25 of 35 by z and 15, 13 and 13 by raw gap; Modern Greek ranks last (35/35) in all three. LB's tops are Korean, Quechua and Tamil (z +1.0 to +1.4). LA top: Coptic +0.23, Arabic +0.06, Tagalog -0.17, Hausa -0.26, Swahili -0.29. Bottom: Japanese, Welsh, Indonesian (about -1.1 to -1.2). Orphan real inputs reach a higher maximum in 28/38 (p = 0.74). Markov maxima are -0.35 to -0.85. Raw gap: LA 0.189, LB 0.27-0.29, Markov 0.05-0.09. | Replicates cycle 1. **No language and no family absorbs LA better than unrelated real words do**, and the Greek control again shows no power through LB spelling. |
| la11:LA-11.2b | Is the recurring Afro-Asiatic lead (Arabic and Coptic in the top 2 in both cycles; family-mean permutation p = 0.005 in both) more than absorbency? Each target's affinity for random structure is measured by its mean Markov-control z, and its affinity for a real, foreign-spelled syllabary by its mean hidden-LB z. LA's z profile is regressed on both, and the Afro-Asiatic residual is tested by permutation (5,000). | LA's profile tracks the Markov profile: Spearman rho = 0.44 (c1) and 0.38 (c2). Markov inputs also rank Coptic, Akkadian, Hebrew and Tagalog at the top. Residual tops: c1 Sanskrit, Coptic, Arabic, Nahuatl, Hindi; c2 Arabic, Hausa, Coptic, Sanskrit, Hindi. Afro-Asiatic residual mean: +0.09 (p = 0.18) in c1 and +0.20 (p = 0.025) in c2. But Akkadian and Hebrew are among the 3 lowest residuals in c1, and Akkadian again in c2. | **Mostly absorbency.** About half of the Afro-Asiatic lead is the same bias the Markov noise shows. What remains is carried by Arabic, Coptic and Hausa, not by the ancient Semitic languages, and Sanskrit and Hindi sit beside them. Grade C at most: "LA bigram structure is closer to Arabic / Coptic / Indo-Aryan syllable sequences than to Uralic, Japonic or Austronesian ones." Would support: the same residual lead with LA non-HT sites and with ECHO spelling. Would kill: it vanishes under a third spelling scheme. Not a language identification: about 12 family comparisons were made over two cycles. |
| la11:LA-11.3a | Spelling-mismatch control. Held-out words of every language are spelled with DROP, while the targets' models are built with MIXED, so input and model follow different writing conventions for the same language. This mimics a script that spells its language differently from our syllabification. Same LA, LB and Markov inputs; 5,500 anneals. | Self-identification survives the mismatch: top-1 in 34/35, top-3 in 35/35. The best relative in own family drops to 6/31 (chance 4.3; median best-relative rank 5). LA: Coptic +0.19, Arabic +0.07, Nahuatl -0.02, Swahili -0.19, Sanskrit -0.24. 27/38 orphan inputs reach a higher maximum (p = 0.72). | A mismatch between our spelling schemes does not hide a language from its own model. The method recognizes **the same corpus**, not the same language family. With the language removed, relatives win little more often than chance (6-9 of 31 across cycles vs 4.3). |
| la11:LA-11.3b | Sample-size control for Linear B. LB is scored at 742 types (3 subsamples), 1,500 types and all 3,678 types, with identities hidden. If the failure in 11.1-11.2 were only a matter of size, Ancient Greek should rise with n. | Ancient Greek rank by z: 22, 23, 33 (742 types), 22 (1,500), 25 (3,678). By raw gap: 9, 5, 8, 6, 6. Modern Greek ranks 31-35 by z. At full size LB's tops are Georgian, Lithuanian, Maltese and Welsh. Larger inputs inflate every z (+1.5 to +3.3), because the reference rows have 742 types. | **The Greek control fails at every size, up to 5x LA's.** The method cannot identify Mycenaean Greek through Linear B spelling against a classical Greek literary corpus, even with all 3,678 LB types. What separates LB from literary Ancient Greek is register, era, the proportion of names and the spelling all at once, and the free map cannot bridge it. Linear A faces the same gaps (unknown era, register and spelling) with less data. So a null result for LA is expected whether or not its language is in the panel. |
| la11:LA-11.3 | Cycle verdict | Three cycles, 3 spelling schemes on the language side (MIXED, DROP, and DROP-on-MIXED), 2 inventories (M = 60, M = 40 bijection), raw and size-equalized models: 15,785 anneals in total. | The contest has power only to recognize the same corpus (34/35). It has almost no power for related languages (6-9/31 vs 4.3) and none for Greek through Linear B (rank 17-33/35). On LA it shows no language or family above unrelated real words (best z +0.07 to +0.23; p 0.72-0.85 vs orphans). Not cracked. |
| la11:LA-11.4a | ECHO scheme: every cluster consonant is written with the next vowel and final consonants are dropped. This is the closest of our schemes to Linear B's own spelling (ko-no-so). The full contest is rerun (5,460 anneals), adding LAn: the 337 LA admin types from sites other than Haghia Triada (held-out sites). | Self-identification top-1 in 35/35. Best relative in own family 11/31 (chance 4.3). **Greek control, partial:** Ancient Greek ranks 7, 11 and 27 of 35 by z, and 6, 5 and 10 by raw gap. Modern Greek ranks 22-33. LB's tops are Quechua, Georgian and Arabic (max z +0.17 to +0.39). LA: every z is negative. Top: Arabic -0.50, Sanskrit -0.58, Swahili -0.67, Nahuatl -0.72, Coptic -0.73. 33/38 orphan inputs reach a higher maximum (p = 0.87). LAn: top Akkadian -0.36, Coptic -0.61, Arabic -0.67; bottom Japanese, Mandarin, Indonesian. | LB-like spelling gives the best Greek result of the four cycles. Even so, Greek wins in none of 3 subsamples, and the best rank in the 14 LB runs over 4 cycles is 5 (raw gap) or 7 (z). LA again shows no language or family above unrelated real words, under the spelling built to suit an Aegean syllabary. |
| la11:LA-11.4b | The kill test for the grade-C Afro-Asiatic lead from LA-11.2b. The residual regression on the Markov and LB profiles is rerun under ECHO, for LA and for held-out-site LAn. | LA: Afro-Asiatic residual -0.00 (p = 0.51). Residual tops: Sanskrit, Coptic, Arabic, Hindi, Nahuatl. Bottom: Finnish, Akkadian, Georgian. LAn: Afro-Asiatic residual +0.10 (p = 0.15). Residual tops: Uyghur, Akkadian, Turkish, Coptic, Maltese. Bottom: Latin, Hebrew, Japanese. Correlation of LA's profile with Markov: rho 0.36; with LB: 0.58. | **The Afro-Asiatic lead is killed (demoted).** It does not survive a third spelling scheme, and it changes members at held-out sites (Akkadian rises, Arabic falls, Turkic joins). Only a weak, non-genealogical residue repeats across all 4 cycles: Arabic, Coptic, Sanskrit, Hindi and Nahuatl absorb LA's bigrams slightly better than Markov and LB absorbency predict. This residue does not hold for LAn. No reading can be built on it. |
| la11:LA-11.final | 39 languages from 18 families (UD, eBible.org, CDLI; sources and licences in data/la11/sources.json; only syllable count tables and opaque held-out sequences are kept). Words are cut into open CV syllables under three spelling conventions (MIXED, DROP, ECHO). For each input and each of 35 target languages, simulated annealing (C core; about 21,200 anneals in 4 cycles) finds the best one-to-one map from the input's top 40 signs to the language's syllables. The map is free, so no sound value is assumed; no Linear B value is used anywhere. Score: compression gain of the real input over its token-shuffled versions, standardized against held-out words of unrelated languages. Controls: (a) shuffled inputs (built into the score); (b) Linear B with sign identities hidden (742, 1,500 and 3,678 types); (c) random Markov strings; plus self-identification and relative-identification for every language, a spelling-mismatch test, and LA from held-out sites. | **Power:** a language's own held-out words pick out its own model in 34-35 of 35 (every cycle, even with mismatched spelling). With the language removed, a relative wins only 6-11 of 31 (chance 4.3). **Linear B control:** Ancient Greek never wins; its rank is 17-33 of 35 under MIXED and DROP (also at 3,678 types) and 7-27 under ECHO; Modern Greek ranks near the bottom. **Linear A:** no language rises above unrelated real words in any cycle. Best z: +0.23 (Coptic, c2), +0.19 (Coptic, c3), -0.10 (Arabic, c1), -0.50 (Arabic, c4). Real orphan inputs beat LA's maximum in 72-87% of cases. Markov strings stay below LA (raw gap 0.05-0.09 vs LA 0.19-0.21 vs LB 0.27-0.32 bits/symbol). A recurring Afro-Asiatic lead is explained about half by absorbency of random structure, vanishes under ECHO (p = 0.51) and changes members at non-HT sites. | **No language family absorbs Linear A better than chance or better than the controls.** The method is too weak to answer the question: it recognizes a corpus, not a language, and it cannot identify Greek from Linear B even at 5x LA's size, ranking it at best 5th and usually mid-table. A null for LA therefore says nothing about any family; it is not evidence against Greek, Semitic, Anatolian or any other candidate. A side result fits LA-5: LA's sequential structure is real (19x the shuffle s.d.) but weaker than LB's (about 70% of LB's gap) and absorbed less well than real words by every language. Not cracked. |
| la12:LA-12.final | Jigsaw: are HT tablets fragments of ledgers split across tablets? Exhaustive exact closure search (totals vs 1-3-block sums of other sides, completions, every quantity vs multi-item sums) + simulated-annealing ledger assembly; nulls N1 (numbers shuffled across tablets), N2 (iid), N3 (each entry redrawn from its own tablet); controls: real HT tablets cut and mixed, Linear B with planted totals cut, planted summary lines, planted word; attribute test (scribe, findspot, two sides, shared words). | 0 cross-tablet closures beat chance under N3; N1-only excess of 2-3-block sums vanishes under N3; joined tablets share scribe/findspot/words at chance; no word role (Holm P = 1.0). Cut real tablets are not recovered; planted summary lines are picked (recall 5-10/10) but barely lift the score. | NOT SUPPORTED / undecidable: exact closure is saturated at HT (10^2-10^4 chance matches per total for 2-3 tablets). Grade A: saturation; C: HT 116a GRA 109 = HT 1 KU-PA3-NU 109. loops/la12_*.txt. |
| la12:LA-12.1a | Data. 182 inscribed HT tablet sides (la6 entry parser). Blocks = every section sum, whole-side sum (all tags and per commodity tag) and written total; 580 (V) / 579 (C) blocks. 53 targets = KU-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO, KI-RO numbers. Two arithmetic modes: V = integer part and the COUNT of each fraction sign added separately (no values assumed); C = conventional lineara.xyz fraction values (data only), 1/480 units. Exact closures, compatible tags, all subsets of 1-3 blocks from distinct OTHER sides (k1,k2,k3: 1.7e8 triples per run, sparse exact index) and completions own-section + 1-2 blocks (c1,c2). | Saturation map: a typical total of 10-100 has 0-15 k1, 50-900 k2 and 7,000-60,000 k3 exact closures; c2 hundreds. Only totals >= 400 or with rare fraction vectors have < 10 k3 closures. | Any 3-tablet 'ledger' is reachable for almost every HT total by chance. Only k1/c1 can be specific. |
| la12:LA-12.1b | Real vs nulls (100 runs each): N1 entry numbers shuffled across sides within commodity tag, N2 iid draws per tag; written totals and each target's own-section sum kept real (an earlier draft that also shuffled own sums gave a false c-excess from near-miss sections; fixed). Neighbour-value null rejected as biased (decreasing density, round totals: real below it on every statistic). | V: targets with a k1 closure 32 (N1 30.4+-1.8, P=0.24; N2 P=0.11); with c1 6 (6.1, P=0.76); L_c2 P=0.22; L_k2 216.4 vs 209.3+-3.0 (P=0.02); L_k3 400.7 vs 395.5 (P=0.04). C: k1 34 (31.6, P=0.14); c1 8 (7.2, P=0.33); L_k2 P=0.01; L_k3 P=0.01-0.03. Per target (V, 139 non-zero tests): 17 at P<=0.01 (1.4 expected), all of them k2/k3 counts on large totals (HT 1, 11b, 100, 104, 110a, 118, 119, 122b PO-TO-KU-RO); BH(10%) keeps 4-6. | No excess of specific (k1, c1, c2) cross-tablet closures. A diffuse excess of 2-3-block combinations reaching large totals (~+3% log count) that cycle 3 must test against a null that keeps each tablet's magnitude. |
| la12:LA-12.1c | Positive control PL: every HT side whose total closes internally (7 in V, 8 in C) cut into 2-3 fragments (total keeps a random share), fragments mixed back; same search, same nulls (34 runs), 3 seeds x 2 modes. | Planted targets do NOT stand out on their own true statistic: per-target P vs N1 0.03-0.94 (median ~0.2); planted-only H_c1 P 0.27-0.74, L_c2 P 0.12-0.44. Planted-only L_k2 P=0.029 every seed, but uncut HT shows the same L_k2 excess, so that is background, not the plant. | Positive control FAILS at HT size: real cuts of real HT tablets are not recoverable by arithmetic closure alone. |
| la12:LA-12.1d | Positive control PB: 205 random Pylos + Knossos documents; 10 multi-entry documents get a planted exact total, then are cut the same way (3 seeds x 2 modes; LB sub-units in V, true values in C). | Mixed: planted H_k1 P=0.03 in 1 of 3 seeds; H_c1 P=0.03-0.85; L_c2 and L_k3 BELOW null (P>=0.94); per planted target P=0.03 for 3-4 of 10-16 in each seed (the large or fraction-rich ones). | Detection only for rare values. The method can find a join when the total is large or carries a rare fraction pattern, not otherwise. |
| la12:LA-12.1 | Cycle verdict | Cross-tablet closures that beat chance at HT: none specific (k1, c1, c2 all at null). Known same-tablet joins seen in passing: HT 116b KU-RO *304 15 = HT 116a *304 section (k1, P=0.01), HT 122b PO-TO-KU-RO 97 (known 31 on side a + 1 + 65) has k2/k3 counts above null (P=0.01), but so do 7 other large totals that have no known join.
