C: CYP+D is a non-tablet (nodule/roundel/vessel) form (P 0.0012; would kill: new CYP+D on tablets). C (demoted): fraction-like letters inside logograms mark small amounts (opposite sign observed). No meaning class can be assigned to any LA added part. Not cracked. |
| la36:LA-36.1 | tools/la36_common.py, la36_c1.py. Every pair of Linear A lists sharing >= 2 recipient words (60 units: SIDES 3 = a/b of one tablet, SCRIBE 3, SITE 12; plus COLUMNS 21 = two commodity columns of one multi-commodity list, ROWS 21 = two recipients' commodity vectors on one side), aligned by recipient. Families: RATIO y = R(r x) (r = p/q <= 16, R exact/floor/round/ceil/half), DIFF y = x + d, COMP x + y = T (identical entries excluded), AFF y = r x + d (needs 3); IDENT counted apart. Pair score s = best family's agreeing count minus its parameter count; the max over families and parameters is inside every null. Fractions at the conventional values. Nulls (1,000 each): N1 alignment shuffled (values drawn within each list), N2 list B replaced by a random list of the same site. Controls: PY Ma target columns (known *146 : RI : KE : *152 : O = 7:7:2:3:1.5), 111 Linear B series/site document pairs (DAMOS), planted RATIO/DIFF/COMP on the real 60 units (clean, and with one aligned entry left unchanged). | Controls: PY Ma S 93 vs 29 (P 0.001), 12/15 columns P <= 0.05; the search returns the known ratio exactly for 5 of 9 non-ME column pairs (KE = 2/7 *146 round, O = 1/2 *152, KE = 2/3 *152, RI = 7/3 *152, RI = 7/2 KE; RI = *146 as identity) and a near neighbour for the rest (3/7 -> 5/12 ceil). LB series pairs S 111 vs 38 (P 0.001). Planted: every class P 0.001 (clean and partial); per unit only 12/60 (4/60 partial) reach P <= 0.05, because most units align 2 entries. Linear A: SIDES S 7 vs 4.9 (N1 P 0.022; N2 P 0.002), all from HT 9a/b (RATIO, 5 of 6 entries under ceil(0.77 x); exact 4/5 for 3 entries as in la24); SCRIBE 1 vs 0.17 (P 0.17); SITE 10 vs 8.4 (P 0.22; N2 0.038); COLUMNS 7 vs 5.6 (P 0.28); ROWS 5 vs 4.0 (P 0.29). HT 86a -> HT 95b halves all five shared recipients (20 -> 10; N2 P 0.028) but the lists are constant, so it is one list-level halving, not a recipient-level rule. Corrected for 5 classes x 2 nulls the SIDES edge is P 0.11 (N1) / 0.02 (N2), one tablet. | NOT CRACKED. Grade A (method): the family search recovers a real Bronze Age ratio system (PY Ma) from the numbers alone, and planted transformations at class level. Grade B (calibrated negative): no transformation family links Linear A lists of one scribe, one site, or the commodity columns/rows of one list beyond the nulls. Grade C (unchanged, la24): HT 9a/b is the only before/after pair (would kill: J re-read on HT 9a). Grade C: HT 86a -> HT 95a/b is a halving of a constant allotment (would support: a third list of the same five at 5 or 40; would kill: none). |
| la36:LA-36.2 | tools/la36_c2.py. Units: every pair of lists sharing >= 1 recipient word in the same commodity (LA 91 units over 49 lists, 78 with a direction; LB 198 DAMOS units; PY Ma 21 target-vs-later-line units). Direction = sign of the median log(y/x) over aligned entries. Marker words = words on one list and not the other. Statistic 1 (units): best binomial P over words seen in >= 3 units. Statistic 2 (lists): each list carrying the word votes its majority direction; binomial over >= 3 lists. Nulls: unit-level direction flips; list-level random effects (each list a random level, direction = sign of the difference; keeps 'this list is just small'). Controls: PY Ma (o-u-di-do-si / o-pe-ro / a-pu-do-si lines are below the target), PY Es dossier, all LB, planted markers (a word forced to the smaller side). | PY Ma: o-da-a2 0/11, ka-ke-we 0/10, o-u-di-do-si 0/10, o-pe-ro 0/5 lists on the larger side; best-word P 0.002 under the list null, 3 words at P <= 0.05 vs 0.05 expected. LA, unit flips: 14 words at P <= 0.05 vs 1.6 (P 0.004; QA-*310-I 1/15, KI-KI-RA-JA, KI-RE-TA2, RE-DI-SE 0/9). Under the list null this falls to 14 vs 14.0 (P 0.53): each such word sits on one list that is simply small. List statistic: LA best P 0.95 (TA-I and TE 3/3 larger, KU-PA3-NU 1/5, KI-RO 1/4, KU-RO mixed over 12 lists). LB all: best P 0.18 (unit level P 0.0005, list level 0.07; do-so-mo 0/11 units). Planted markers in LA are found only for words on >= 6 lists (KU-RO P 0.012, PA 0.033; KU-NI-SU, DI with 3-5 lists not found); only KU-RO and PA qualify. | NOT CRACKED. Grade A (method): unit-level direction tests are fooled by list-level amount scale (LA P 0.004 -> 0.53; LB 0.0005 -> 0.07); a list-level null is required. Grade B (calibrated, low power): no Linear A word marks the direction of change; KU-RO (12 lists) does not, and a word that always marked the smaller list on >= 6 lists would have been seen. Words on 3-5 lists (KI-RO 1/4 larger, KU-PA3-NU 1/5) are below the power limit. Grade C: KI-RO and KU-PA3-NU on the smaller side (would support: 3 more pairs with these words on the smaller list; would kill: 2 on the larger). |

Grade B (calibrated negative): no global before/after rule (ratio or difference, any fraction values) holds across Linear A list pairs, dossier pairs or commodity columns; no fraction values are implied. Grade C (demoted): HT 9a/b supports J = 1/2 only under exact scaling; the equally good rule 'round up 4/5' fits 5 of 6 entries for any J <= 1/2, so the pair no longer favours J = 1/2 specifically (would support: a second pair with exact 4/5 on J-bearing amounts; would kill: none needed). |
| la36:LA-36.final | Three cycles (tools/la36_common.py, la36_c1.py, la36_c2.py, la36_c3.py; checkpoints data/la36_ckpt/). (2) Direction-marking words with unit-level and list-level nulls. Grade A (method): the family search and held-out random rules recover a real Bronze Age ratio system and its deficit words; unit-level direction tests need a list-level null. Grade B (calibrated negative): Linear A paired lists do not show a fixed transaction rule or a direction-marking word beyond chance; power covers words on >= 6 lists and rules in >= 40 % of units. Grade C: HT 9a/b as before/after (scaled by 4/5, rounded up); J = 1/2 no longer specifically implied (demoted). Grade C: HT 86a -> HT 95a/b as one halving of the same five allotments (would support: the same five at 5 or 40 elsewhere). Grade C: KI-RO and KU-PA3-NU on the smaller list (1/4, 1/5 lists; below power). |
| la38:LA-38.1a | tools/la38_common.py, la38_c1.py, la38_report.py. Prior work: Packard (1974) scored 9 fictitious LA decipherments by LB-like vowel alternations; Davis (2024) scored LA/LB/Cypriot syllabotactic overlap against homomorph relabelings. Neither scores the value map by language-independent phonotactics against grid-preserving relabelings; this does. A value map (sign -> C, V; LB-derived for LA) is scored by 9 measures: ocpC / ocpP (adjacent same consonant / same place class avoided, -log obs/exp), sameV (same vowel, reported only), vinit (pure vowels word-initial: onset principle), son (initial strengthening), freqC / freqV (Spearman of row / column token count vs cross-linguistic inventory frequency), comp (-bits/phoneme of the C/V phoneme stream, plug-in bigram), la21 (AUC of the blind la21 same-row probability for pairs sharing a consonant). Unvalued signs (logograms, *-signs, PA3, AU, cluster signs) break words into fragments; only true word edges count as edges. Nulls (10^5 each): R1 consonant labels permuted among rows and vowels among columns (grid kept); R2a vowels permuted inside each row; R2b consonants permuted inside each column; R3 all (C, V) values permuted among signs. | Full Linear B (KN+PY, 64 valued signs, 27,260 tokens). R3: ocpC z +4.2 (P 1e-5), ocpP +3.0 (0.002), vinit +1.9 (0.02), freqC +1.4, son +0.2, comp 0.0, freqV -1.0, sameV -3.3 (LB repeats vowels: the cluster-spelling vowel copy), la21 +11.6. R2b: ocpC +3.9, ocpP +2.9, vinit +1.9. R2a (vowels inside rows): comp +5.7 (P 1e-5), sameV -3.0, freqV -1.0. R1: freqC +2.4 (P 0.006), all others <= +1.3, comp -1.8. | Measure sets fixed here, before LA was run in full (SEL per tier = z >= 1.64 on full LB): R3 and R2b = ocpC + ocpP + vinit; R2a = comp; R1 = freqC. son, freqV and the a-priori 7-measure composite (ALL7) carry nothing in LB and are dropped from verdicts. (An earlier 2,000-relabeling R3 smoke test had already been run on LA; noted for honesty.) |
| la38:LA-38.1b | Positive control at LA size: LB drawn by document to LA's valued-token count (3,569), 10 draws x 4 tiers x 10^4 relabelings. | SEL beats its relabelings in 10/10 draws in every tier: R3 median z +4.3, R2b +4.2, R2a (comp) +5.7, R1 (freqC) +2.5. la21 +11.6 under R3. | Power at LA size is high for R3, R2b and R2a. R1: freqC P < 0.05 in 47/100 (median z +1.6). | R2a, R2b, R3 calibrated: wrong values sit in the middle (false-positive rate 5-11% at nominal 5%: verdicts below use P <= 0.01). R1 freqC +1.7 (P 0.045; tier killed in 1d). | The LB-derived values are far better than chance for Linear A: only 4 in 100,000 relabelings score as well on consonant phonotactics, 42 in 100,000 on vowel-sensitive compressibility. |
| la38:LA-38.2b | Where LA sits among true-value scripts at its size: the same statistics for the 10 LB draws at LA size (cycle 1b). | LA R3 SEL z 4.1 vs LB draws 3.7-5.8 (rank 5/11); R2b 4.3 vs 3.5-5.9 (rank 6/11); R2a 3.8 vs 3.2-6.6 (rank 2/11, i.e. second lowest). | Linear A under LB values is as phonotactically natural against relabelings as Linear B under its own true values, for consonants; slightly lower, but inside the LB range, for vowels. |
| la38:LA-38.2c | Negative control: 100 wrong-value maps for LA (random relabelings treated as the truth), 1,000 relabelings each per tier. | P < 0.05 in: R3 SEL 4/100 (single measures 3-9/100), R2b 2/100, R2a 8/100; median z -0.2 to +0.2. R1 freqC 74/100. | Wrong values sit in the middle: the LA result is not a property of the corpus or the measures. R1 killed again. |
| la38:LA-38.2d | Kill control: LA with signs shuffled inside each word (5 shuffles; word bags and sign frequencies kept, order destroyed), 10^4 relabelings; the same for LB drawn at LA size (5 draws). | LA shuffled: ocpC +2.6, ocpP +2.1 (5/5 P < 0.05), vinit -0.5 (0/5), comp under R2a +1.7 (2/5), SEL R3 +1.9. LB shuffled: ocpC +3.7, ocpP +2.2 (3/5), vinit -0.4 (0/5), comp R2a +1.6 (1/5), SEL R3 +2.8. | Same split in both scripts: the consonant-OCP signal lives at the level of the word (same-consonant signs avoid sharing a word, not only adjacency) and survives the shuffle in LB too, so its survival is not an artifact; the onset-principle signal (vinit) and about half of the vowel signal (comp) need the real order and vanish when it is destroyed, in LA as in LB. |
| la38:LA-38.2 | Cycle verdict | Against 10^5 grid-preserving and fully random relabelings, the LB-derived values make Linear A obey consonant OCP (adjacent and word-level), place OCP and the onset principle, and make its phoneme stream more compressible, to the same degree as Linear B's true values at the same corpus size; wrong values and order-destroyed corpora behave as they do in LB. | GRADE B (borrowed values carry real phonetic information for LA as a whole, consonants and vowels). Not A: Cypriot passed only the vowel test (1c), and the consonant measures share their basis with la21. NOT CRACKED (no reading). |
| la38:LA-38.3a | tools/la38_c3.py, la38_report3.py. Two scores fixed in cycle 1: SC = mean z of ocpC, ocpP, vinit (vs 5,000 R3 relabelings); SV = z of comp (vs 5,000 R2a relabelings). Row test: the k signs of one LB consonant row each exchange their full value with a random sign outside the row (2,000 draws), P = share scoring >= the hypothesis; column test the same with SV. Sign support: C-support = share of exchanges of the sign's consonant with each sign of its own vowel column that lower SC (e.g. RO<->KO); V-support = the same for vowel exchanges inside its own row with SV. (A first version exchanged with any sign, which creates homophones that the plug-in compressibility rewards; it was found on LB, discarded, and rerun before LA was read: data/la38_ckpt/c3_calib_v1_homophone_bug.json.) | Calibration, LB drawn at LA size, true values (4 draws): rows with P <= 0.05: 12-47 % (r and the pure-vowel row in 4/4, w 3/4, d, n, m, p 2-3/4; single-sign rows never). Columns: 0 / 20 pass (column test has no power). Mean sign support 0.72-0.79 (C) and 0.71-0.83 (V); 6-18 % of signs below 0.5. Planted errors (6 value swaps = 12 wrong signs, LB 6 draws and LA 4 plants): AUC right > wrong 0.53-0.85 (median ~0.68); a planted error drops the rows it touches (LA plant: 0-31 % rows pass). | Row tests: calibrated, moderate power (a true row of 4-5 signs passes about half the time at LA size). Column tests: no power; no verdicts on vowel columns from this test. Sign support: weak (AUC ~0.68); usable only to flag, grade C at best. |
| la38:LA-38.3b | The same on LA with LB-derived values. | Rows passing: pure vowels A E I O U (P < 0.0005), M- (MA ME MI MU; P 0.002), R- (RA RE RI RO RU; 0.010), T- (TA TE TI TO TU; 0.018), K- (KA KE KI KO KU; 0.033). Marginal: D- (DA DE DI DU; 0.051), S- (SA SE SI SU; 0.069). Not beating chance: J- (JA JE JU; 0.105), N- (NA NE NI NU; 0.107), P- (PA PI PO PU; 0.112), and the small rows Q- (QA QE; 0.26), Z- (ZA ZU; 0.27), W- (WA WI; 0.30) and single signs PU2 0.23, TA2 0.30, RA2 0.47. Rows passing: 31 % (38 % of multi-sign rows), inside the LB true-value range (12-47 %). Columns: none pass (as in LB). Sign support: mean C 0.82, V 0.80 (LB true values 0.72-0.79 / 0.71-0.83); only PI has C-support < 0.5 (0.44); V-support 0 for WA, WI (a two-sign row: WA<->WI is the only exchange), TO (16 tokens; every vowel exchange inside the T row fits better). | Rows that beat chance under the borrowed values: pure vowels, M, R, T, K (grade B: each passes a calibrated test, and the pattern - vowel row and R strongest - is the one LB itself shows); D and S marginal (C). J, N, P and the small rows Q, Z, W, *-2 signs are not supported, which at LA size is also what a true row often does (LB true rows fail 53-88 % of the time): absence of support is not evidence against them. Sign level: no sign is contradicted beyond what LB true values show; flags (grade C): TO (vowel), PI (consonant), WA/WI (order of the two W signs). |
| la38:LA-38.3 | Cycle verdict | The borrowed values beat chance row by row as often as true LB values do at the same size; the strongest rows are pure vowels, M, R, T, K. Vowel columns cannot be tested one by one at this size, in LB or LA. | GRADE B for rows (vowel, M, R, T, K); C for D, S; no verdict on columns or on J, N, P, Q, Z, W. NOT CRACKED. |
| la38:LA-38.4a | tools/la38_c4.py (stage whole). LA split by findspot: Hagia Triada (1,671 valued tokens) and all other sites (1,898). Whole-map tests R2a / R2b / R3, 10^4 relabelings each. Control: 6 LB draws at half LA size (~1,800 tokens). | Consonant set (SEL R3): HT z +2.8 (P 0.001), other sites +3.8 (P 1e-4); R2b: HT +2.9 (0.001), others +4.2 (1e-4). LB half draws: R3 2.4-5.2 (6/6 P < 0.05), R2b 2.1-5.0. Vowel set (comp, R2a): other sites +3.0 (P 0.004); HT +1.2 (P 0.12), below all 6 LB half draws (2.2-6.2, 6/6 pass). | The consonant result replicates in both halves at LB strength (B). The vowel result replicates outside Hagia Triada only; at HT the vowel fit is weaker than any true-value LB sample of that size (C: would support - the same deficit in the post-1985 HT documents or in HT personal-name lists alone; would kill - HT comp >= 2 on a larger HT sample). |
| la38:LA-38.4b | tools/la38_c4.py (stage rows). Row tests of cycle 3 in each half; control: 4 LB half-size draws. | Rows with P <= 0.05 in both halves: pure vowels (HT 0.010, others < 0.001), M- (0.023, 0.004), T- (0.041, 0.031). One half only: R- (HT 0.021, others 0.066), K- (0.133, 0.044), D- (0.26, 0.024). Neither: S, J, N, P, Q, Z, W, single signs. LB half draws pass 6-47 % of rows (r and the pure-vowel row most often: 4/4 and 3/4). | Rows replicated on held-out halves: pure vowels, M, T (B). R, K, D hold in one half and in the full corpus (C for D; R and K stay B on the full-corpus test of 3b, not replicated). |
| la38:LA-38.4 | Cycle verdict | Held-out halves keep the main result for consonants (both halves at LB-like strength) and the strongest rows (pure vowels, M, T); vowel values are supported outside Hagia Triada but weakly at Hagia Triada. | GRADE B (consonants, rows V/M/T); C (HT vowel deficit). NOT CRACKED. |
| la38:LA-38.final | tools/la38_common.py, la38_c1.py, la38_c2.py, la38_c3.py, la38_c4.py, la38_report.py, la38_report3.py; logs loops/la38_cycle1-4.txt. LB-derived values of 52 LA syllabic signs scored by language-independent phonotactics (consonant OCP, place OCP, onset principle, sonority, inventory-frequency fit, phoneme-stream compressibility, blind la21 rows) against 10^5 relabelings per tier: R1 rows/columns relabeled as wholes, R2a vowels permuted inside rows, R2b consonants permuted inside columns, R3 all values permuted. Measure sets fixed on full Linear B before LA was run. Controls: LB full and 10 LB draws at LA size (true values), Cypriot Idalion tablet (true values, 1,010 signs), 100 wrong-value maps each for LB and LA, within-word sign shuffles of LA and LB, held-out halves (Hagia Triada vs other sites) with LB half-size draws, planted value errors for the row and sign tests. | LA beats its relabelings: consonant set z +4.1 (P 4e-5, R3) and +4.3 (R2b); vowel set (compressibility) +3.8 (P 4e-4, R2a). LB at LA size: 3.7-5.8 / 3.5-5.9 / 3.2-6.6, so LA sits inside the true-value range (rank 5/11, 6/11, 2/11). Wrong values: P < 0.05 in 2-8/100. Cypriot passes the vowel test (z 3.9), consonant test only P 0.06. R1 tier killed (wrong values pass 47-74 %: it tests row sizes, not values). Shuffles: OCP survives in LA and LB alike (word-level OCP), onset and order-based vowel signal vanish in both. Rows: pure vowels, M, R, T, K beat chance (P 0.0005-0.033); D, S marginal; J, N, P, Q, Z, W and single signs not (true LB rows also fail half the time at this size); held out in both halves: pure vowels, M, T. Columns untestable one by one (LB too). Held-out halves: consonants replicate (HT z 2.8, others 3.8); vowels replicate outside HT (3.0) but not at HT (1.2, below all LB half draws). Sign flags: TO (vowel), PI (consonant), WA/WI order (support test AUC only ~0.68). | GRADE B: the Linear B values are not arbitrary for Linear A; they make it obey language-independent phonotactics as strongly as true values do in LB at the same size (consonant structure, pure-vowel row and vowel structure). Row level: B for pure vowels, M, T (replicated), R, K (full corpus); C for D, S; no verdict for J, N, P, Q, Z, W. C: weaker vowel fit at Hagia Triada; TO, PI, WA/WI flags. Would kill the B: a calibrated test on a larger LA corpus (post-GORILA documents) where the consonant set falls below LB's true-value range. NOT CRACKED: no reading, only support for the transliteration convention. |

| la41:LA-41.2 | Cycle verdict | Fixed vs variable. LA families: spelling kept 22/26, logogram 2/2, AMOUNT kept 8/15 = 0.53 (bootstrap 0.29-1.00) against 0.09 for the same word on unrelated lists (n 284) and 0.05 positional (binomial P < 1e-5): copies conserve amounts, and when amounts change the whole list changes (HT 9 by 4/5, HT 95 by 1/2). LB non-formula copies (En/Eo etc.): amount kept 0.78, logogram 1.00, spelling 0.92; the edit types are the same (drops, order swaps, rare spelling variants), so LA copying looks like LB copying, but LA rewrites amounts more often (0.53 vs 0.78; 15 entries, not significant). LA edit catalogue over 5 families: 22 words same, 4 spelling edits (3 are a final sign dropped: DI-DE-RU/DI-DE at a break on HT 86b; U-NA-KA-NA-SI/U-NA-KA-NA twice, the libation formula), 1 substitution (A-RU/A-SI), 4 dropped, 3 added, 11 order inversions (mostly HT 95a/b), 7 amounts changed. Number-only families (38 vs 5.8) are KILLED by the strict null that keeps each list's amount set (38 vs 33.7, P 0.16): lists share round amounts, not copied order. | Grade B: in LA near-copies, names and amounts are the fixed elements, order is free; amounts change only as a whole-list rescaling. Grade B (negative): no LA list re-uses another list's amount sequence with different names. |

Power at n=16: 100 % row-mates 1.00, 50 % 0.99, 25 % 0.69. | see verdict |
| la41:LA-41.3h | LA amount-ratio test on independent families: HT 86/95 trio counted once; then with HT 9a/b and HT 86/95 (known from la24, la36) removed. Null as 3c. | Independent: 14 families, 3 ratio families vs null 1.08, P 0.0546. Without the known pairs: 10 families, 1 vs 0.33, P 0.2967; HT62+73~HT97a hits 2: [(6.0, 4.0), (8.0, 6.0)]. | see verdict |
| la41:LA-41.3 | Cycle verdict | (2) Copy-made substitutions: the LB control passes; 15 distinct substitutions harvested blind from LB copy families follow the blind la21 LB rows (P 0.0008) and LB consonants (0.33 vs 0.10, P 0.0065): ra2/ra, qe/pe, e/i, wo/wa, si/pi/ne (case endings) are recovered. LA: robust families yield one substitution, RU~SI (A-RU 4E on HT 9a / A-SI 3 on HT 9b), not testable alone; the loose set yields 16 scorable substitutions that do not follow the blind LA rows (P 0.50) although the scorer has power 0.69-1.0 at this n if >= 25 % were row-mates, so the loose 'variants' are mostly different words. (3) Amount ratios: LA loose 5 vs 1.15 (P 0.0006) but this is HT 86/95 (counted three times) and HT 9a/b; independent families 3 vs 1.08 (P 0.055); without those known pairs 1 vs 0.33 (P 0.30; HT 62+73~HT 97a, 6->4, 8->6, floor 3/4). LB ratio families 61 vs 28.7 (P 0.0002; PY Es fixed proportions). | No new LA sign equivalence (calibrated: power adequate at n 16). Ratio edits in LA = the two known pairs only (grade C each, as la24/la36). RU~SI grade C. |
| la41:LA-41.final | Three cycles (tools/la41_common.py, la41_c1.py, la41_c2.py, la41_c2b.py, la41_c3.py, la41_c3b.py; checkpoints data/la41_ckpt/). Every document side as an item list (word, logogram, amount). (1) Near-copy families by 7 alignment schemes (5 Smith-Waterman variants with exact/one-sign-variant/logogram/number scores and gap costs, 2 order-free matchings), pair score = information of shared words, threshold per scheme at FDR 0.1 against a site-word shuffle (100 reps), robust = >= 4/7 schemes; (2) edit catalogue and element conservation vs same-word-unrelated and positional nulls; number-only families vs a within-list amount shuffle; (3) copy-made sign substitutions vs blind la21 rows (LB consonants only for the LB control); amount-edit ratios vs site-pool redraws. Controls: Linear B KN+PY blind (known PY Eo -> En draft/fair copies, Es, Jn), planted copy families (recovered 0.91), planted row-mate substitutions (10/10; power 0.69-1.0 at LA's n). | LA has 5 robust near-copy families against 0.33 by chance: HT 9a~9b, HT 95a~95b, HT 86a~86b and two libation-formula pairs; HT 86a~HT 95a/b only order-free (re-ordered copy). In the families, names and amounts are fixed (amounts kept 8/15 vs 0.09 for the same word on unrelated lists), order is free (11 inversions), and amounts change only as whole-list rescalings (HT 9 x4/5, HT 86->95 x1/2). LB copies show the same edit profile (amounts 0.78, logograms 1.00). Number-only 'families' are killed by the within-list null (P 0.16). Copy-made substitutions: LB 15 distinct follow blind rows (P 0.0008) and consonants (P 0.0065); LA robust gives only RU~SI (A-RU/A-SI, HT 9a/b); LA loose 16 do not follow the blind rows (P 0.50). New ratio families beyond HT 9 and HT 86/95: 1 vs 0.33 (P 0.30). | NOT CRACKED. Grade A (method): blind family search plus copy-substitution harvest recovers Linear B draft/copy pairs and sound-related spelling variants. Grade B: LA near-copies are rare (5; 3 are recto/verso), keep names and amounts, re-order freely. Grade B (calibrated negative): no LA sign equivalence from copying; no number template reused with other names. Grade C: A-RU = A-SI as a copy variant (would support: A-RU and A-SI in the same slot elsewhere at HT; would kill: both on one list as separate entries). Grade C (unchanged): HT 9a/b x4/5 and HT 86->95 x1/2; HT 62+73 ~ HT 97a (floor 3/4) too weak to claim. |

| la40:LA-40.1a | Linear A tablets (435, 752 word types, 1,531 tokens), 24 chains x 1e6 updates. | 164 of 181 types with >= 2 tokens forced; 137 of them to L (a catch-all role), H 17, C 6, T 2, P 2. | Most 'forcing' is the default role. |
| la40:LA-40.1b | Null: words shuffled across all word slots within site, same search, 6 runs x 12 chains. | Forced per role (mean [max]): L 165.5 [172], H 4.2 [9], P 2.3 [4], C 0, T 0, M 0. Real: L 137, H 17, C 6, T 2. Non-default forced roles the null never reproduces for the same word: 27 (KU-RO T, PO-TO-KU-RO T; TE, SA, *307, *326, *334, U-MI-NA-SI C; A, A-DU, MA, JA, DA, MI, U, ME, A-RA, A-RI, I-SE, KA-NA, KU-RE, RA2, A-PA, U-*34-SI H; A-SI-JA-KA, I-DA P). | Real > null for T, C, H; but see 1c. |
| la40:LA-40.1c | Positive control: Linear B KN+PY at Linear A size (6 draws, 1,531 word tokens each), known classes (persons from personnel series, ~110 place names, ~50 transaction terms, TO-SO/TO-SA totals, ~50 commodity words) scored only. Null: LB with words shuffled (3 draws). Ceiling: all 2,335 KN+PY documents. | Strict accuracy of forced labelled types 0.05-0.40 (all types 0.25-0.36) vs shuffled LB 0.18-0.48; best-mapping accuracy 0.50-0.73 vs majority class 0.50-0.65 (no gain). TO-SO never T (L or P); A-PU-DO-SI H in 3/6; O-PE-RO always L. Full LB: strict 0.09, best-map = majority (0.84). | FAILED: the rules do not recover LB word classes from structure, at LA size or at full size. |
| la40:LA-40.1d | Planted roles in Linear A (2 runs): KU-RO renamed in half its tablets (T), new tablet-first heading on 10 tablets (H), new word before a logogram on 8 lines (C), new word replacing one counted entry head on 8 list tablets (E). | T recovered 2/2 (0.99, 0.92); C 2/2; H 0/2 (goes to P); E takes its neighbours' role 2/2, but that role is the catch-all L. | Recovers only what the rules spell out (closure, logogram contact). |
| la40:LA-40.1 | Cycle verdict | The pre-registered type system fails its positive control: Linear B classes are not recovered and TO-SO, the clearest LB total, is not typed T. The 27 'forced' non-default roles in LA are rule echoes: KU-RO/PO-TO-KU-RO as T (already A from la1 arithmetic), C = signs written next to logograms (TE, SA, *307 ...), H = single signs and short words written first on a tablet without a number. Not evidence for word classes. Next: learn the rules from the control instead of writing them (cycle 2). | Negative (method fails control). |
| la40:LA-40.2a | 1,200 random type systems on Knossos (LA size), macro recall. | Top 5 0.39-0.42; median 0.19. Label-permuted search top 5 0.41-0.50 (higher than with the true labels). | Training fit is search noise. |
| la40:LA-40.2b | Held-out: top 20 systems on Pylos (3 samples, 2 chains). | Macro recall 0.176 (0.08-0.28) vs label-permuted top 5 0.174 vs median-ranked systems 0.154; chance with 6 roles ~0.17-0.20. The best systems put almost everything into one role (e.g. P 36/65, L 0/13, H 0/12, T 0/3). | FAILED: no structural type system transfers from Knossos to Pylos. |
| la40:LA-40.2 | Cycle verdict | Even inside Linear B, with the rules fitted on known classes, structure alone does not type words at Linear A size: role signatures do not transfer across archives, and a random-label search fits as well as the real one. Therefore no learned system is applied to Linear A as evidence; cycle 3 asks only whether role assignments are stable on held-out tablets. | Negative (calibrated: control fails). |
| la40:LA-40.3a | Linear A halves (128 shared types). | Excess +0.03 to +0.09 by system (prereg +0.052, learned +0.032/+0.053/+0.094); shuffled +0.000 to +0.037; real beats shuffle in 4-6 of 6 splits. ARI 0.10-0.14. | Weak but real held-out consistency. |
| la40:LA-40.3b | Pylos halves, size-matched (84 shared types). | Excess +0.16 to +0.27 (prereg +0.161 vs shuffled +0.123; learned +0.08 to +0.27 vs ~0.00); ARI 0.22-0.33. PY at 2x size: excess +0.08 to +0.25, ARI 0.14-0.25. | Control: structure gives LB words stable (if unnamed) roles. |
| la40:LA-40.3c | Per-word held-out stability, Linear A, pre-registered system. | Same role in both halves: KU-RE H 5/5, U H 5/5, A-SI-JA-KA P 4/4, A H 5/6, DA H 3/4, KA-NA H 3/4, A-DU H 3/6. KU-RO is T in only 3 of 12 halves (L otherwise): at half size the T role collapses. TE alternates C/H. Learned systems' role names are arbitrary (they put most single signs into 'T'). | Named roles do not survive halving except heading-like H for a few short words. |
| la40:LA-40.3 | Cycle verdict | Role partitions inferred from structure are reproducible across disjoint halves in Linear B (ARI ~0.3 at matched size) and much less in Linear A (ARI ~0.12, about 0.4x; excess over marginals about 1/3 of Pylos), while shuffled words give ~0. So LA word types do carry some position/role identity, but much weaker than LB's, as la8's flat two-open-class grammar implies; none of it can be named, because the naming step fails its LB control (cycles 1-2). | Grade B (calibrated, relative): weak role consistency. |
| la40:LA-40.final | Every word type gets one role (person/group, place, commodity/qualifier, heading/transaction, total, number-word); tablets must type-check under structural rules (closure of totals, headings before lists, logogram contact, one entry role per list). (1) Pre-registered rules + DM clustering, tempered Gibbs, 24 chains x 1e6 updates on LA; word-shuffle null (6 x 12 chains); LB KN+PY at LA size with known classes (6 draws), shuffled LB, full LB; 4 planted roles. (2) 1,200 random learned type systems scored on Knossos, re-tested on Pylos, against a label-permuted search (600). (3) Disjoint-halves stability on LA vs size-matched Pylos, shuffled-word null, ARI. About 1e9 sampler updates in all. | (1) LA: 164/181 types 'forced', 137 to the catch-all L; 27 non-default forced roles unseen in the null (KU-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO T; TE, SA, *307 ... C; A, A-DU, MA, JA, KU-RE, U ... H), but LB control fails: strict accuracy 0.05-0.40 vs shuffled 0.18-0.48, best-map = majority, TO-SO never T, even at full LB size. Plants: T and C recovered (rule-spelled), H not. (2) Learned systems: Pylos macro recall 0.176 vs permuted-label search 0.174 vs chance ~0.17-0.20. (3) Held-out ARI LA 0.10-0.14 vs Pylos 0.22-0.33 (shuffled ~0); KU-RO keeps T in only 3/12 halves. | Grade A (method, calibrated negative): structural type inference cannot name word roles even in Linear B at Linear A size; no Linear A word class is established by it. Grade B: LA word types carry reproducible role identity at ~0.4x LB strength (fits la8's flat grammar). Grade C: KU-RE, U, A, DA, KA-NA as heading-type words written first without a number. Would support: these words opening new tablets without a count; would kill: them as counted entry heads on new tablets. |

| la45:LA-45.3e | Search-failure check: hand-made partitions, no game (logogram vs word; + total words KU-RO/PO-TO-KU-RO, to-so/to-sa, szu-nigin2), scored on the same final tablets | LA: 2-meaning +0.13, 3-meaning +0.19 bits (S2 +0.01 / -0.01); LB +0.95 / +0.94 (S2 +0.10); Ur III +0.00 / +0.09 | The game misses a small real LA signal (~0.2 bits, order-borne, carried by logograms and KU-RO) that a fixed partition finds; in LB the game finds 3x more than the hand partition. LA's order-borne role signal is at most ~0.2 bits per word occurrence, against >= 1 bit in every control |

| la45:LA-45.4b | LINEAR B, KN+PY -> Thebes (50 scorable occurrences) | gain -0.41..+0.45 (mean -0.05), contradictions 6-8% | Real controls do NOT transfer to a different archive at this tiny size (power floor) |
| la45:LA-45.4c | UR III, Umma/Puzrish-Dagan/Girsu -> Ur + Nippur (196 occurrences) | gain -0.14..+0.11 (mean +0.01), contradictions 3-9% | Same: about zero |
| la45:LA-45.4d | LINEAR A, GORILA 1-3 -> later publications (73 scorable occurrences) | gain -2.22..-2.79 bits/occ in 6/6 populations (mean -2.57); one-meaning model -0.01 | Meanings fitted on the early (mostly Hagia Triada) corpus actively mispredict the new finds, ~2.5 bits below both real controls |
| la45:LA-45.4e | LA_TIME_S1 (type-shuffled LA, same split) | gain -0.26..+0.29 | As the real controls |
| la45:LA-45.final | Four cycles (tools/la45_common.py, la45_c1.py, la45_c2.py, la45_c3.py, la45_c4.py, la45_report.py, la45_compare.py, la45_c1_tablets.py, la45_c2_support.py, la45_c3_pairs.py, la45_c3_oracle.py; checkpoints data/la45_ckpt/, git-ignored). Adversarial self-play: Proposers give every value-free word/sign type one of <= 12 anonymous meanings (class bigram + emitted types + occurrence frames incl. section-sum arithmetic), fitted by incremental collapsed Gibbs with an MDL cost per meaning; Critics evolve the subset of a vault of unseen documents that maximises the Proposers' loss (held-out bits minus contradictions = held-out events the meanings call impossible); population-based training 12 x 6, 120 rounds; 6 independent populations per corpus; abstract names (TOTAL, COMMODITY, ENTRY, HEADER, TERM, RARE) by fixed profile rules. ~1.4 billion candidate evaluations over 28 corpora x 6 populations. Controls: planted meaning system, Ur III and Linear B reduced to opaque ids (truth used only to score), type-shuffled (S1) and within-document-shuffled (S2) twins, site and time splits. | Planted: recovered every time (NMI 0.69-0.80 vs null 0.05-0.14; TOTAL 1/1; COMMODITY 9/9; persons ENTRY; order-borne 58-82%). Ur III / Linear B: roles above chance (NMI z 3-7), Knossos -> Pylos and Umma -> Puzrish-Dagan transfer (+0.1..+2.4 bits), word order worth 1.0-1.3 bits on tablets (6/6); total words missed in both. Linear A, random split: 54 types settle (agreement >= 0.8), gain 1.7-2.7 bits, but 39/54 settle identically with order destroyed; on tablets the meanings give +0.01 bits and order adds nothing (drop -0.06, 3/6; controls 1.0-2.4, 6/6); a hand partition logogram/word/KU-RO finds only +0.19 bits. Hagia Triada meanings mispredict other sites (-0.9 bits, 6/6) and later finds (-2.6 bits, 6/6) where real controls stay >= 0. Shuffled LA gives no stable meanings beyond frequency (gain ~0.1). | NOT CRACKED. GRADE A (method): calibrated self-play recovers planted, Ur III and Linear B roles from value-free tokens. GRADE B: the main logograms (VIN, OLE, OLIV, VIR 1.0; GRA, NI 0.83) plus single signs KI, DI, RE, TI, MA form one counted-commodity meaning (stable across populations, survives held-out criticism, but document-level, not order-borne). GRADE B: single-sign nodule/roundel words (*301, KA, KU, SI, RO, ZE) form a stable heading meaning (document-level). GRADE B (calibrated negative): Linear A tablets carry little order-borne role structure that generalizes (<= 0.2 bits vs >= 1 bit in controls), and HT usage does not carry to other sites or newer finds. GRADE C: ligatured OLE+U/+MI/+DI behave as counted entries, not commodities, by order (would support: same split in a new HT find; would kill: vanishes without HT 9/86/95 copies). No total meaning recovered for KU-RO (controls miss totals too, so no evidence against it). |
| la47:LA-47.final | THE LAYOUT IS THE GRAMMAR (la47; tools/la47_layout.py, la47_common.py, la47_engine.py, la47_c1.py, la47_c1b.py, la47_c2.py, la47_c2b.py, la47_c2c.py, la47_c3.py, la47_c3b.py, la47_show.py; logs loops/la47_cycle1-3.txt; checkpoints data/la47_ckpt/, git-ignored). Tablets as 2-D pages: physical lines from lineara.xyz/GORILA transcriptions aligned to corpus tokens (287 LA sides), sign boxes from SigLA (118 sides), LB pages from DAMOS. ~100,000 random spatial grammars (depth <= 3 trees over layout features only) chosen on half the pages and scored on held-out pages, against equal searches over word order; nulls: identities permuted within page, and reading order re-flowed onto a donor page's line breaks (shape fixed, contents swapped); controls: LB, a planted line-start rule, a planted size rule. | (1) Layout carries ~0.045 bits/word about which word sits there, the same as order (0.049) and ~0.002 bits beyond it; nulls -0.01 to +0.01. LB: order 0.32-0.38, layout 0.06-0.10. Planted line-start rule found at 0.13-0.21 bits (order: 0). (2) LA writes run-on lines: 14 % of line breaks split a word or number (0 in LB); breaks fall at entry boundaries 1.4x chance (LB 2.2x; HT 1.55x; Khania 0.9x, P 0.72). (3) No class is written larger (calibrated to a 5 % shift); gaps mark nothing robust. (4) Layout cells agree with la45: totals bottom-of-tablet on their own short line (z 5-6), header signs line-initial (z 2.9), commodities inside lines next to their numbers (z 4.4; LB z 22.6). (5) Layout profiles sort la45 classes (balanced accuracy 0.44 vs null 0.33, P 0.035) where order profiles do not (0.28); in LB order wins. (6) Nothing learned at HT transfers to other sites. | Hypothesis killed for line-level and size layout (B): Linear A does not hide its structure in space. Its tablets are run-on text with weak entry alignment, so layout and order are both thin, and the missing role signal is in neither. Kept: run-on writing (A, data), totals / headers / commodities placement (B), layout > order for classes (C: would support P < 0.01 when more SigLA-aligned tablets are added; would kill P > 0.2). Fine-geometry grammar search was underpowered (907 words), no verdict. |
| la47:LA-47.1a | Data: physical lines from lineara.xyz 'transcription' (GORILA line layout; checked by hand against SigLA box y-coordinates on HT 1, HT 9a, HT 13), logical tokens from corpus.json, aligned by character class (difflib). 287 LA tablet sides with alignment >= 0.9 (3,478 items). LB: DAMOS KN+PY, 2,970 pages, physical lines and recto/verso from the edition's line labels. tools/la47_layout.py, la47_common.py. | LA scribes run text over line ends: 14 % of LA line breaks fall inside an item (93 words and 14 numbers split across two lines, e.g. HT 1 DI-DI / ZA-KE); 0 in DAMOS LB (DAMOS hyphen-fragments not counted, so LB is an underestimate). | Layout data built (A). |
| la47:LA-47.1b | Where do scribes break lines? Breaks not inside an item classed as entry boundary (number -> word), word -> word, or before a number. Null: same text re-flowed onto a donor page's line breaks (same line count), snapped to item boundaries, 200 reps. Control: LB. tools/la47_c1b.py | Entry-boundary share: LB 0.561 vs null 0.252 (2.2x). LA 0.497 vs 0.355 +- 0.016 (1.4x, P < 0.005). By site: HT 0.543 vs 0.349 (P < 0.005), Phaistos 0.48 vs 0.34 (P 0.01), Zakros 0.45 vs 0.36 (P 0.06), Khania 0.30 vs 0.33 (P 0.72: Khania scribes ignore entries when breaking lines). Share of entries that begin a line: LA 0.31, LB 0.47. | LA lines are weakly entry-aligned at HT, not at Khania; LA layout is run-on text, LB layout is a ruled list (B). |
| la47:LA-47.1c | Random spatial grammars: depth <= 3 trees over 9 layout features only (top/middle/last physical line, line-initial, line-final, wraps a line, side, number later on the same line, line count, vertical quarter, items on the line), 2,000 per split, chosen by 2-fold CV on half the pages, top 10 scored on the held-out half (4 splits); same search over 6 order features (rank, next/previous is number, after divider, rank quarter, length). Targets: word identity, la45 class, number magnitude. tools/la47_engine.py, la47_c1.py | Held-out bits/word (top-10 mean), word identity: LA spatial 0.045, order 0.049, spatial added to best order grammar +0.002. LB (3 subsamples matched to LA size): spatial 0.06-0.10, order 0.32-0.38, increment -0.01 to +0.06. la45 class: LA spatial 0.008, order -0.002; LB 0.06-0.11 / 0.38-0.44. Number magnitude: LA -0.04 / -0.01; LB spatial 0.01-0.07, order -0.05-0.03. Best LA spatial grammar uses vertical quarter + line-initial + line-final. | LA layout carries about as little as LA order (both ~0.05 bits on identity); in LB order dominates and layout adds a little. |
| la47:LA-47.1d | Nulls for 1c: (P) identities permuted within page, 3 reps; (R) order kept, page re-flowed on a donor's line breaks, raw (3) and snapped to item boundaries (2). | Identity, spatial top-10: P -0.009 to 0.003; R snapped -0.003 to 0.009; R raw -0.02 to -0.03. Order under R keeps 0.04-0.07 (as it should). Increment of spatial over order: real +0.002 vs snapped-R -0.012 to -0.025. | The 0.045 bits of LA layout are real (above P and R), but are almost all redundant with order (B). |
| la47:LA-47.1e | Planted control: real identities shuffled within page, then 20 % of word types forced to start a physical line (pages re-flowed); 2 reps. | Recovered: planted-class target spatial 0.13 / 0.21 bits vs order -0.01; word identity spatial 0.03 / 0.11 vs order 0.01 / -0.01; best grammar is "line-initial" both times. | Engine finds a planted spatial rule that order cannot see (A, control). |
| la47:LA-47.1 | Cycle verdict | Coarse layout (lines, sides, line starts and ends) carries ~0.045 bits/word about which LA word sits there, the same as word order, and adds ~0 beyond order; planted spatial rules of this kind are found at 0.1-0.2 bits. No hidden spatial grammar at line level. | Negative for line-level layout (B). |
| la47:LA-47.2a | Fine 2-D geometry from SigLA boxes: 118 tablet sides where every sign box aligns to a corpus token (907 words). Per word: size (log height over page median, minus the mean for that sign code or logogram), indentation of line-initial words, gap before the word, vertical drift from the line. Direct test: mean residual size by la45 class, within-page permutation null (2,000). tools/la47_c2.py | Size difference vs rest (log units): commodity -0.003 (n 131, P 0.81), header -0.004 (25, P 0.92), total -0.007 (19, P 0.82), line-initial words -0.002 (331, P 0.86). Indentation shares are the same in every class (flush 63-77 %). | No class is written larger or smaller (B, calibrated by 2c). |
| la47:LA-47.2b | Gap between two words on one line (SigLA boxes, 205 pairs): is a commodity sign glued to the word before it? Null: gaps permuted within page (2,000). Width control: gap over the pair's own mean width. tools/la47_c2b.py | Median gap before a commodity 0.26 vs 0.37 sign-widths (P 0.50; pair-width 0.23 vs 0.36, P 0.21); logograms vs syllabic words 0.25 vs 0.45 (P 0.15). Wider gap after a header word 0.54 vs 0.34 (n 8, P 0.02, one of 7 tests). | No spatial binding of commodity to word. "Gap after header" is C: would support: P < 0.01 when SigLA adds documents; would kill: P > 0.1 at n >= 20. |
| la47:LA-47.2c | Power of 2a: plant a size shift on commodity tokens (or a random 15 %), same test. tools/la47_c2c.py | Residual-size sd 0.147; a 5 % shift on commodity tokens gives P 0.004, on a random 15 % P < 0.001. | 2a would have seen a 5 % size marking (A, control). |

| la47:LA-47.2 | Cycle verdict | Size marks nothing (calibrated to 5 %); gaps mark nothing robustly; the grammar search on fine geometry is underpowered and says nothing either way. | Negative for size (B); geometry grammar untested (power). |

Same with order profiles (rank x next/previous is number). Null: identities permuted within page (200). LB control (600 pages, logogram / word / to-so). tools/la47_c3.py | LA (96 types): layout 0.436 vs null 0.328 +- 0.056 (P 0.035); order 0.281 vs 0.290 (P 0.48). LB (188 types): order 0.713 vs 0.550 (P < 0.01); layout 0.524 vs 0.531 (P 0.71). | In LA the layout profile sorts la45 classes a little and order does not; in LB it is the other way round. Grade C: would support P < 0.01 with the 3-line-or-more tablets of new finds; would kill: P > 0.2 on a corpus with SigLA-aligned lines. |
| la47:LA-47.3b | Which layout cells carry la45 classes: for every spatial feature value, class share vs within-page permutation (2,000), |z| >= 2.5 reported. tools/la47_c3b.py | LA totals (KU-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO): bottom quarter 6.6 % vs 1.9 % (z 5.7), last line z 5.0, line-initial z 3.7, lines with two items z 5.9 (KU-RO and its number on a line of their own), first line 1/416 (z -2.6). LA header single signs line-initial z 2.9. LA commodities avoid line starts (11.9 % vs 18.7 %, z 4.4) and sit on lines that carry a number (z 3.3). LB commodity line-start avoidance z 22.6, numline z 10.5. | Spatial roles agree with la45: total = own short line at the bottom, header = line start, commodity = inside a line next to its number (B). The commodity layout signal is ~5x weaker than LB's. Words split over lines are never commodities, but commodities are mostly one sign, so that is trivial. |

| la48:LA-48.1 | Planted conserved economy at LA size (393 documents): best labelling vs 20 quantity-shuffled copies | survival 1.0: 178 balance points vs 18.2 +- 5.8 (P 0.048, the minimum); 0.5: 29 vs 15.2 (P 0.048); 0.2: 21 vs 13.7 (P 0.095). Aggregating economy: 1.0: AGG 47 vs 3.5 (P 0.048); 0.3: score 25 vs 16.8 (P 0.095), AGG 6 vs 3.1 (P 0.14) | A (method): the search finds conservation when most of the archive survives; detection fails below about 30-50 % document survival. Genuine sums (AGG) are seen only in a near-complete archive. |
| la48:LA-48.1b | Linear B KN and PY, same search and nulls | KN 62 vs 34.6 +- 6.8 (P 0.048); PY 120 vs 40.1 +- 6.0 (P 0.048). All excess is PAIR (KN 35, PY 75 two-sided repeats); AGG KN 7 vs 7.95 (P 0.81), PY 9 vs 9.1 (P 0.62) | B: Linear B nodes repeat their own amounts on several documents (assessment and delivery, or copies); no cross-document sums are seen even where they are known to exist. |
| la48:LA-48.1c | Ur III Puzrish-Dagan livestock accounts (central ledger with known deliveries, transfers and expenditures) | 393 documents: real minus null +120, +1, -13, +25, +58 over 5 draws (P 0.17-1.0); 2,000 documents: 1,350 vs 1,198 +- 24 (P 0.17); AGG 310 vs 266 (P 0.17) | B (control fails): even a central ledger of conserved animals shows no cross-document balance beyond chance at these sizes, because the surviving documents are a tiny, unaligned sample of the flows. |
| la48:LA-48.1d | Linear A, all sites (393 documents with entries, 1,557 entries, 152 nodes on >= 2 documents) | 16 balance points vs 10.1 +- 4.4 (P 0.14); POIS 12.7; random labels 5.1. PAIR 10, AGG 2 vs 1.35 (P 0.48). Balanced nodes: HT MU-RU, PA, *305, KA/VIR, DI/VIR, SA-RU/GRA, DI-KI-SE, PA-TA-NE; KH TU/CYP, TA; PE *307/OLE; ZA TI | B (calibrated negative): Linear A amounts do not conserve across documents beyond chance. With the planted power curve, this says that either the surviving tablets are under about a third of one closed account, or the tablets do not record a single closed flow system. No conservation network can be read. |
| la48:LA-48.2 | Planted conserved economy (complete, 100 %): can the best labelling tell IN from OUT documents? | KIRCH finds 76 balanced nodes, 79 % of them the true pass-through officials (null 0-7 %); COMB 60 nodes, 100 % officials. Yet direction |phi| = 0.00-0.08 (P 0.26-1.0); the aggregating economy gives 0.165 (P 0.018) under KIRCH and 0.00 under COMB. GRAPH alone gives phi 0.13-0.30 on real AND shuffled amounts (the gain is from the holder / counterparty layout, not from quantities) | A (method): conservation fixes only RELATIVE direction inside each chain of balanced documents. Flipping every document of a chain leaves every balance intact, so absolute direction (who gives, who receives) cannot come from Kirchhoff balance alone; it needs a broken symmetry (a known store, a known stock sign). Pass-through nodes ARE recovered when the archive is complete. |
| la48:LA-48.2b | Ur III Puzrish-Dagan at LA size (2 draws): directions and roles against the known verbs (ba-zi / zi-ga out, mu-kux in) and known officials | direction |phi| 0.03-0.12 (P 0.28-1.0), QSHUF 0.05-0.12; SOURCE vs RECEIVER net-sign AUC 0.55-0.62 vs QSHUF 0.52-0.58. Top-throughput hubs: ugula, i3-dab5, ensi2, szabra, mu, iti, kiszib3, zi-ga, sza3-bi-ta (overseer title, the verb 'took over', governor, temple administrator, date words, 'sealed document', 'expended'); none of the known central officials | B (control fails): in a real conserved archive the method names grammar words and titles as hubs and cannot orient flows. Throughput hubs are frequent words, not stores. |
| la48:LA-48.2c | Linear B KN and PY: hubs | KN hubs are places (a-mi-ni-si-ja, da-wo/GRA, ru-ki-to, pa-i-to, su-ri-mo, ka-ta, re-ko-no); PY hubs include pa-ro (a preposition, 'from/at'), to-sa / to-sa-de (the TOTAL words), pi-*82, ma-ro-pi, ro-u-so | B: in Linear B the hubs are districts that deliver goods (KN) or function words (PY); totals that are not caught by arithmetic become hubs. Not the known collectors or the palace stores. |

Balanced nodes (12): 1 = 1 pairs (HT MU-RU, PA, DI-KI-SE, PA-TA-NE), the two sides of one tablet (HT 86a/b SA-RU GRA 20; ZA 12a/b TI 13), HT KA/VIR 4 (HT 62+73 vs HT 93a), KH TU/CYP E alone (KH 2 vs KH 57), KH TA 2 (KH 8 vs KH 50), PE *307/OLE 1 + J (tablet PE Wy 5 vs stone object PE Zg 5), and two 3+-term sums (HT *305 across 6 tablets, HT DI/VIR 2 + 2 = 4) that occur at the shuffled rate | B (negative): the Linear A 'network' is the control's network: hubs are the biggest numbers and the total word (KU-RO), as in Ur III and Linear B. No store, palace or household can be named by flow. C: PE Wy 5 / PE Zg 5 (*307 OLE 1 + J on a tablet and on a stone object) record one transfer twice (would support: another Petras document with *307 and the same amount; would kill: a re-reading of either amount). |
| la48:LA-48.3 | Planted economies: held-out amounts | Complete, forwarding exact sums: COPY 355 of 898 vs QSHUF 90-123; PHYS 24 vs 2-7. Complete, aggregating: BEYOND 24 of 1,104 vs QSHUF 2-4 (RANDLAB 19). 50 % survival: COPY 197 vs 90-119, PHYS 1 vs 0-3. 20-30 % survival: COPY 113-120 vs 85-145, PHYS 0-2 vs 1-4 | A (method): conservation predicts unseen amounts only in a near-complete archive, and then mostly as repeats. Fitted labels add little over random ones (BEYOND 24 vs 19), because the residual is tested in both directions. Node-specific repeats (COPY above QSHUF) are still visible at 50 % survival. |
| la48:LA-48.3b | Controls: Linear B KN, PY; Ur III Puzrish-Dagan 393 and 2,000 documents | COPY above every shuffled run: KN 232 vs 180-217, PY 299 vs 250-256, Ur III 8,446 vs 8,043-8,225 and 55,255 vs 51,577-52,151. PHYS: PY 15 vs 2-7, Ur III 303 vs 167-234 and 742 vs 527-595, KN 5 vs 2-3, but RANDLAB is higher everywhere (PY 23.9, Ur III 361 and 852) | B: in real administrations the same entity carries the same amount on several documents. The fitted flow labels never predict better than random labels, so the 'flow direction' adds nothing to the repeats. |
| la48:LA-48.3c | Linear A, all sites | COPY 62 of 302 vs QSHUF 51-78 (mean 62.9; P about 0.55). PHYS 2 vs 0-1 (RANDLAB 3.9). BEYOND 2 (HT 3 KU-PA3-NU 3; KH 88 NI 10) vs 0-1 in 10 shuffles (P about 0.09) | B (negative; power limited, see 3d): in Linear A a word's amount on one document predicts its amount on another no better than a random amount of the same site and commodity. Linear B (+18 %), Ur III (+4-7 %) and a planted archive with 50 % survival all show this effect; Linear A does not (-1 %). So Linear A words in entries do not behave like standing accounts (a person or store that keeps its quota from record to record), or the surviving records of each account are too few. Killed: the two BEYOND hits (at the shuffled rate). |
| la48:LA-48.3d | Power check: Linear B KN and PY cut to Linear A size (393 documents, 3 draws each; tools/la48_c3b.py, 2 x 80,000 proposals per fold, 6 shuffles per draw) | COPY excess over shuffled: KN +31, +31, +41 % (only 33-52 eligible amounts), PY +3, +21, +47 %; per draw P 0.14-0.71 (0.14 is the floor with 6 shuffles). Linear A: -1 % on 302 eligible amounts | C: Linear A sits below all 6 Linear B draws of its size, but Linear B draws of this size reach the shuffle floor in only 3 of 6, so the Linear A result is an upper bound, not proof. Reading: Linear A entry words are not standing accounts with fixed quotas. Would support 'no standing accounts': the same deficit on the new SigLA/Khania material. Would kill: an excess >= 20 % with P < 0.05 once more Linear A entries exist. |
| la46:LA-46.1a | Strict rule (beat every forger group), p < 1e-4, all 11 corpora. | LA: 1 residue of 404 tested: TOT KU-RO (7 held-out documents end a section with KU-RO + exact running sum; forger expectation 0.33). UR2: 1 (a running sum). All other corpora 0, INCLUDING the planted worlds (0/12 planted relations; best planted CO pair p 2e-4) and LB (0 of 365). | The strict inversion has no power at LA size: every relation a real writer repeats >= 3 times is also produced by at least one forger group at a comparable rate. Only the KU-RO total survives (grade A, already known: la1/la14). |
| la46:LA-46.1b | Liberal rule (beat the weakest group), p < 1e-3, per-family pass rate vs the two forger worlds (Fisher, one-sided). | CO (tablet co-occurrence): LA 23/46 = 0.50 vs worlds 35/176 = 0.20, P = 7e-5; LB1 41/51 = 0.80 (P 3e-15), LB2 39/95 = 0.41 (P 2e-4); PL1 31/87 (P 0.005), PL2 20/88 (P 0.35); SH 6/67 (0.09; P 0.99); UR1 35/764 (0.05; FAILS). Every adjacent family (WN, WL, LN, XS, NN, PF) is at or below the world rate in LA, LB and the planted worlds; planted word->number scales are NOT found (1/6, 2/6): an adjacent constraint is a surface statistic and any forger copies it. | The only family where real tablets beat the forgers is long-range: which words share a tablet. LB positive control passes on both draws; planted control 1 of 2; Ur III fails (its LA-sized sample is ~100 long formulaic documents that cluster forgers reproduce). |
| la46:LA-46.1c | What the LB CO residues are (truth used for scoring only). | LB top CO/ES residues: a-ta-ra-si-jo + e-ko-te + ka-ko (bronze-smiths with / without bronze), ka-ko + ta-ra-si-ja (bronze allotment), e-ke-qe + ko-to-na and da-mo + o-na-to (land tenure), ko-wa + ko-wo (girls + boys in personnel lists), ke-ke-me-na + te-o-jo, ko-ri-ja-do-no + po-ni-ki-jo (spices), pu-ro -> MUL. TOTAL words 8/63 of LB1 residues vs 12/163 tested. | Positive control: the residues land on real-world schemata (a work-group, a land-tenure transaction, a personnel census, a spice list), i.e. on sets of entities that recur together because the world bundles them. |
| la46:LA-46.1d | The LA CO / ES residues (liberal), and the documents behind them. | (i) DA-ME, DI-DE-RU, SA-RU, KU-NI-SU, MI-NU-TE all pairwise: HT 86a/b, HT 95a/b (two tablets, both sides); (ii) *306-TU, PA-DE, *324-DI-RA: HT 9a/b, HT 122a; (iii) PA + QA-*310-I: HT 8a, 85b, 122a; (iv) A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA with I-PI-NA-MA, U-NA-KA-NA-SI, SI-RU-TE (the libation formula on stone vessels); (v) *28B-NU-MA-RE + SI-PI-KI: ZA 4a, 5b, 15a; (vi) SA-RA2 -> NI entry succession, 11 HT tablets (E 4.0 strict); TE -> SA-RO, 4 HT tablets. | Item-level residues are recurring CASTS: the same 3-5 entries listed together on separate tablets (two sides of one tablet count twice here: cycle 3 merges sides). The libation formula is a text, not the world, and is reported as a formula residue. |
| la46:LA-46.1 | Cycle verdict | Strict inversion: powerless (0/12 planted, 0 LB), only KU-RO = total survives. Liberal + world calibration: one constraint type survives in LA and in LB: recurring casts (which entries share a tablet). Adjacent pairings (word-number, word-logogram, logogram-number, sign pairs) are never residues: forgers copy them. | B (calibrated, family level): LA tablets bundle entries into recurring casts beyond every forger world (0.50 vs 0.20). C: the specific casts (HT 86/95 group; HT 9/122 group; ZA *28B-NU-MA-RE + SI-PI-KI) (would support: held-out prediction in cycle 2; would kill: the excess vanishing when sides are merged and CV is grouped by tablet). |
| la46:LA-46.2a | Held-out AUC and firing ratio, 6 splits each. | AUC (residue set / random set / t of the difference): LA 0.522 / 0.501 / 7.4; W1 0.499 / 0.499 / -1.0; W2 0.512 / 0.493 / 2.7; SH1 0.500 (no residues); PL1 0.524 / 0.499 / 6.3; LB1 0.532 / 0.502 / 5.3; LB2 0.520 / 0.506 / 4.3; UR1 0.680 / 0.521 / 9.3. Firing ratio real/forged on B: LA 3.02 (81 vs 26.5); W1 0.19; W2 1.36; SH1 -; PL1 4.84; LB1 2.41; LB2 2.34; UR1 1.84. Relations with >= 10 held-out real instances and >= 3x the forgers: LA 3, W1 0, W2 0, PL1 1, LB 1 + 1, UR1 1. | AUC alone does not separate LA from an n-gram world (W2 also gains, t 2.7: re-estimation artefacts), but the firing ratio does: worlds 0.19-1.36, real corpora 1.8-3.0, planted 4.8. LA's residues transfer to unseen tablets at a ratio above both LB draws. |
| la46:LA-46.2b | What transfers (summed over splits, real B / forged B). | LA: ES SA-RA2 -> NI 27 / 3.8; WL NI -> VIN 29 / 6.3; TOT KU-RO = running sum 14 / 0.4. LB: ES + CO ko-wa / ko-wo (girls / boys in personnel lists) 28 / 6.5 and 38 / 9.0; CO e-ke-qe / pe-mo (holds seed-land) 9 / 2.8; da-mo / o-na-to (lease from the damos). UR1: CO iti / mu (month + year names on one tablet) 210 / 119; PF ba-hul LAST (year formula 'destroyed' closes the tablet) 107 / 49; CO ki / mu. PL1: the planted partner pair KI-RO / PA-I-TO 53 / 8.9 (1-2 of 12 planted relations per split pass at A size). | Controls land on real-world frames: LB personnel censuses and land tenure; Ur III dating (month + year, year name at the end). The planted cast is recovered on held-out data. In LA three constraints transfer: the running total, and one commodity BASKET: SA-RA2 + (GRA or CYP) + NI + VIN (+ *23M) in that order on 11 HT tablets (HT 18, 28a, 28b, 30, 90, 94a, 99a, 100, 114a, 121, 130). |
| la46:LA-46.2c | The SA-RA2 basket in the data (direct listing). | HT 28b: SA-RA2 GRA 20 OLE+DI 5 NI 2 VIN 4; HT 30: SA-RA2 CYP 14 NI 7 VIN 8; HT 99a: SA-RA2 CYP 4 NI 4 VIN 1; HT 100: KU-RO 97 SA-RA2 CYP 5 NI 2 VIN 2 OLE...; HT 121: SA-RA2 GRA 5 OLE 4 NI 2 VIN 3 *23M 3. The basket is always GRA/CYP first, NI before VIN, NI <= the first amount. | Forgers miss it because the order spans 3-6 tokens through logograms and numbers; any order-3 exact forger could copy it, but the ensemble mostly backs off. A fixed multi-commodity bundle following one word: what a real ration or delivery basket looks like. |
| la46:LA-46.2 | Cycle verdict | Residues found on half the tablets predict the other half; the effect is above forger worlds (firing 3.0 vs 0.2-1.4) and comparable to LB. What transfers in LA is accounting structure: the running total (A, known) and one fixed commodity basket headed by SA-RA2 (B). No reading. | B: SA-RA2 heads a fixed commodity basket (GRA/CYP, NI, VIN, sometimes OLE, *23M) on HT tablets (would support: a new HT tablet with SA-RA2 followed by the same order; would kill: SA-RA2 found with a different commodity order on 3+ tablets). C: SA-RA2 names the basket or its recipient category (not testable here). The basket pattern itself is visible to any reader of HT tablets, so this is a validation of the method, not a discovery. |
| la46:LA-46.3a | Casts (CO) with sides merged and topic forgers, ensemble-mean rule, p < 1e-3. | LA 0/39; W1 0/134; W2 0/166; SH1 0/63; LB1 0/120; LB2 0/91; PL1 1/69 (planted 1/12), PL2 0/103 (0/12). Weakest-group rule: LA 10/39 = 0.26 vs worlds 88/300 = 0.29 (P 0.74); LB1 0.30, LB2 0.70. | The cycle-1 cast excess is KILLED: it came from sides of one tablet counted as two documents and from forgers that could not cluster tablets. Once forgers may memorise tablet vocabularies, co-occurrence is forgeable in Linear B as well (the LB casts also vanish), so 'which words share a tablet' is not a residue at this size (method blind, not a negative about LA). |
| la46:LA-46.3b | Ordered long-range residues, ensemble-mean rule, p < 1e-3, all families. | LA 3 of 324 tested: TOT KU-RO = running sum (4 tablets, E 0.19, P 5e-5); ES SA-RA2 -> NI (9, E 1.70, P 7e-5); WL NI -> VIN (12, E 3.06, P 8e-5). Worlds W1, W2: 0; SH1: 0. LB1 and LB2: ES ko-wa -> ko-wo (girls then boys in personnel lists; 13 / 14 tablets vs E 3.6 / 4.0, P 1e-4 / 7e-5), nothing else. PB1, PB2 (planted ordered constraints): 3/6 and 3/6 found, 0 false passes. Strict rule: 0 everywhere (PB 0/6), so it is powerless. | The ensemble-mean rule is calibrated (0 in three nulls, 0 false passes in two planted worlds, recall 0.5 for planted rate-0.8 constraints) and lands on a known real-world schema in Linear B both times (the census order girls/boys). In Linear A it keeps exactly the running total and the SA-RA2 basket (SA-RA2, then GRA or CYP, then NI, then VIN). |
| la46:LA-46.3c | Adjacent pairings under every rule and cycle (WN word->scale, LN logogram->scale, WL beyond the basket, XS sign pairs, NN, PF, SW). | Never above the worlds in LA, LB, Ur III or planted corpora; the planted word->scale constraints (cycle 1) were found 0-2/6. | Constraint types a forger reads off the surface (commodity-unit pairings, word-number scales, which sign ends a word before which) are invisible to the inversion by construction. The user's control expectation 'commodity-unit pairings' fails for this reason, not because the method misses LB structure; LB's residue is an ordered personnel schema instead. |
| la46:LA-46.3 | Cycle verdict | With every artefact removed, real Linear A beats ~160,000 forged tablets per corpus in exactly two places, both accounting: the running total under KU-RO and one ordered commodity basket after SA-RA2. Casts, word-number pairings, site choices and sign pairs are all forgeable. | A (known): KU-RO totals. B: the SA-RA2 basket is the only non-arithmetic world constraint the forgers cannot fake. NOT CRACKED. |
| la46:LA-46.final | Novelty: la14 ran a document-level forger contest; LA-46 inverts it at item level: which specific relation (sign pair across a word edge, word -> number scale, word -> logogram, logogram -> scale, entry -> next entry, two words on one tablet, site x word, number -> number, word = running total, first/last word) do real held-out tablets show more often than every forger? Forgers: 200-300 random architectures per corpus (back-off n-grams of order 1-4 over randomly abstracted contexts, optional site, line index, copied layouts, tablet clusters up to K 32), 5-fold CV, 2-3 forgeries per held-out document per architecture (~93,000-160,000 forged documents per corpus; ~3 M in all). Rules: strict (beat the strongest of 16 forger groups), ensemble mean, weakest group. Held-out test: residues found on one half scored on the other half against fresh forgers that saw everything else (6 splits). Controls: two forger-written worlds and a within-kind shuffle (negative); planted casts and word->scale constraints, and planted ordered constraints (positive); Linear B KN+PY and Ur III at LA size, words as opaque ids, known structure used only to score. | Strict rule: powerless at LA size (0/12 and 0/6 planted, 0 in LB), only KU-RO = total survives in LA. Liberal rule + world calibration (cycle 1): co-occurring casts in LA 0.50 vs 0.20 (P 7e-5) and LB 0.80/0.41, but this was KILLED in cycle 3 (sides of one tablet counted twice; topic forgers fake casts in LB too). Held-out (cycle 2): residues found on half A fire on unseen half-B tablets at 3.0x the forgers in LA, 2.3-2.4x in LB, 1.8x in Ur III, 4.8x planted, 0.2-1.4x in forger worlds. Final calibrated rule (cycle 3, ensemble mean, p < 1e-3): worlds 0, shuffle 0, planted ordered constraints 3/6 + 3/6 with 0 false passes, LB 1 + 1 (ko-wa -> ko-wo, the girls-then-boys order of personnel censuses). LA: 3 residues: KU-RO + exact running sum; SA-RA2 -> NI; NI -> VIN (one basket: SA-RA2, then GRA or CYP, then NI, then VIN, sometimes OLE, *23M, on 11 HT tablets). Adjacent pairings (commodity-unit, word-number scale, sign pairs, site choices) are never residues anywhere: a forger copies any adjacent statistic. Ur III residues are the dating frame (month + year on one tablet, the year formula last). | NOT CRACKED. A (known, la1/la14): KU-RO totals are the one place every forger fails. B: the only other constraint a surface forger cannot fake is an ordered commodity basket headed by SA-RA2 (GRA/CYP, NI, VIN); it predicts held-out tablets (27 real vs 3.8 forged instances) and matches la20's commodity order (would support: a new HT tablet with SA-RA2 in this order; would kill: SA-RA2 with another order on 3+ tablets). B (method): item-level inversion works only with an ensemble-mean rule and only for ordered long-range constraints; it is blind to adjacent pairings and, once forgers can cluster tablets, to co-occurrence (also in LB). Killed: LA 'casts' (cycle 1). C: SA-RA2 names the basket or its recipient class (untestable here). |

C: figs and olives share fewer LA tablets than chance (4 vs 9.2). |

Massive random guessing: ~700 random context predicates x all terms per run (LA 85,176 links, LB 558,885), support-stratified z, 40 held-out-deposit splits; nulls: within-site room shuffle (N1), cross-site context shuffle (N2); planted links; pre-registered commodity probes; inversion (read the room from the tablet, leave-one-deposit-out); family-wise max-z test. | LA: 39-100 surviving links vs N1 65 (P 0.48-0.57) and N2 112 (P 0.81-0.90); room reading AUC 0.60 (N1 P 0.71, N2 P 0.14); no single link survives family-wise correction. LB: 6,595 links vs N1 2,610 and N2 2,028 (P 0.048, 0/20); OLE~storerooms z 8.7, ROTA~Arsenal z 10.0 (N1 P 0.048), but they replicate on held-out rooms only 46 % and 12 % (one or two deposits each); VIN, GRA and livestock texts are NOT near their goods; cloth (TELA, pa-we-a)~magazines survives family-wise vs N1 (fw P 0.01-0.02); room reading AUC 0.78 (N2 P 0.03, N1 P 0.10). LB at LA size keeps 2,400-3,100 links (30-50x LA). Planted links recovered: LA STOR 15/15, RIT 15/15, METAL 12/15; LB METAL 3/3, STOR 3/3 (ANIM untestable: one deposit). LA sanctuaries are read from their words (RIT AUC 0.91, N2 P 0.01) via A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA, I-DA, ME. | NOT CRACKED. LINEAR A NULL (calibrated: plants recovered, LB control passes, not a size effect). The rooms of Linear B carry word information; the recorded rooms of Linear A do not, beyond site membership. Cause not separable: the LA room table is thin (6 room labels, all HT), and/or LA tablets were kept in offices away from the goods - even in LB only oil, wheels and cloth sit with their goods. B (not new): libation-vessel words follow sanctuary deposits across sites. C: QA2 and TE with the HT Villa magazine (unadjusted p 0.002, 0.008; would kill: QA2 on tablets from a new LM I house with no magazine); C: NI, CYP, O, *86, A-DU with houses (HT Casa rooms, KH Kastelli, TY) rather than the Villa (would kill: these terms on new tablets from a palatial magazine); C: OLE with storage sites (N2 p 0.013, N1 p 0.36). |

| la44:LA-44.final | Resurrect the ancestor by simulating history (loops/la44_cycle1-3.txt, la44_final.txt): 190,000 random proto-languages (6 morphology classes, 3 syllable classes, inventories, roots, Zipf use) evolved through random sound-change sequences and borrowing, written in a 13x5 CV syllabary under random spelling conventions; ABC over a 44-77 statistic panel (raw, value-free, shuffle-referenced, control-pruned); unconditional and conditional ('given affixation') questions. Controls: Linear B subsamples at LA size (must give FUS_SUF), Cypriot Idalion (must give FUS_SUF), globally and positionally shuffled LA (must be flat), in-model calibration, 60-shuffle null for the LA call. | In-model: morphology class 68-78 % (chance 17 %), calibrated; syllable class 44-47 % (chance 33 %); borrowing, coda, cluster rates and sound-change kinds not identifiable (R^2 <= 0.05; lifts 0.8-1.6 for LA and LB alike). LB: FUS_SUF mode 4/5 (raw), 5/5 (control-pruned), 5/5 conditional; fails on the shuffle-referenced panel (2/5) and at 107 types (1-2/5). Cypriot (107 types): AGG_BOTH 0.79-0.86, FAIL. Shuffled LA: never flat (ISOL 0.67-0.99 unconditional; TEMPL 48/60 conditional). LA: ISOL 0.79-0.98 in every unconditional panel (= its shuffles; driven by word length: 39.5 % of LA types are 2 signs vs 8.9 % in LB); conditional on affixation FUS_SUF 0.571, AGG_SUF 0.231, TEMPL 0.128 (shuffle null P = 6/60 = 0.10). Diagnostic: real syllabic texts (LA and LB alike) avoid same-series neighbours and doubled signs far beyond any simulated history and have lower sign-to-sign predictability; LA's Kober-type final alternation (+0.45 over shuffle) is a third of Greek's (+1.29) and sits at the 44th percentile of simulated inflecting languages. | NOT CRACKED, NOT SUPPORTED. C: given that LA has affixation, a suffixing language with inflection-class-like final alternations (Greek-type) is the ABC's lean, then agglutinating suffixes (would support: the lean survives a 100-type larger sample or a second LA site set with P < 0.05 against shuffles; would kill: P >= 0.2 on more shuffles or a non-HT hold-out going TEMPL). B (method finding): the posterior over typology from a written type list of LA's size is driven by shuffle-invariant statistics (word length, sign inventory) and is NOT informative about LA; any ABC typology claim needs a shuffle-referenced null, and at ~100 types (Cypriot, single LA sites) the method is blind. B (diagnostic): the generative gap between simulated and real syllabic writing (series OCP, doubled-sign avoidance, low adjacent MI) is shared by LA and LB, i.e. LA's sign-sequence constraints are those of a real spelled language, not specific to any typology. Void: syllable structure, inventory size, history kinds. |
| la44:LA-44.1a | In-model check: RF OOB accuracy and calibration of the 1 % rejection posterior on 400 held-out simulations. Raw panel (44 stats). | Morphology OOB accuracy 0.772 (chance 0.167); confusions AGG_SUF <-> FUS_SUF (2,708 / 2,433) and AGG_PRE <-> AGG_BOTH. Calibration: posterior max 0.45-0.6 -> 0.45 correct, 0.6-0.8 -> 0.71, >= 0.8 -> 0.96 (C4 PASS). Syllable class OOB 0.467 (chance 0.33): weakly identifiable. Continuous parameters: R^2 consonant inventory 0.64, vowel count 0.34, root length 0.19, number of sound changes 0.18, borrowing 0.02, coda rate 0.04, cluster rate 0.04. | The machinery reads morphology type from a written type list; syllable structure and history weakly or not at all. |
| la44:LA-44.1b | Positive control: Linear B (Greek) at LA size, 5 random type subsamples of 583. | FUS_SUF mode in 4/5 (0.725, 0.530, 0.597, 0.615; sub2 TEMPL 0.389 > FUS_SUF 0.313); AGG_SUF second in 4/5. Syllable class CCVC mode 5/5 (0.42-0.52). | C1 PASS (4/5, the minimum). |
| la44:LA-44.1c | Negative control: LA with signs shuffled across all types (5) and within position classes (3). | Global shuffles: ISOL 0.849-0.889 (normalized entropy 0.29-0.34); position shuffles: ISOL 0.79-0.85. | C3 FAIL: shuffled LA is NOT flat; the machinery calls structureless text 'isolating'. |
| la44:LA-44.1d | Linear A, raw panel. | ISOL 0.961, every other class <= 0.013; syllable CV 0.32 / CVC 0.29 / CCVC 0.39 (flat); consonants 15-25 (median 20; prior 16), vowels 4-8 (prior), sound-change count, borrowing, coda and cluster rates = prior. Sim-cloud position: 8.9 % of simulations are farther from the cloud centre (same as the shuffles; LB 17-19 %). | VOID: LA's call equals the shuffled-LA call. What drives it is shuffle-invariant: 39.5 % of LA types are 2 signs (LB 8.9 %), mean 2.88 signs; top RF features are initial/final entropy and top-3 initial/final shares. |
| la44:LA-44.1e | Same on the value-free sub-panel (27 statistics; no Linear B sign values used). | LB: FUS_SUF mode 4/5 (0.50, 0.38, 0.42, 0.45; sub1 TEMPL 0.48). Shuffled LA: ISOL 0.90-0.94. LA: ISOL 0.865, FUS_SUF 0.074; LA cloud position 2.3 % (shuffles 0.3-1 %). | Same verdict: C1 passes narrowly, C3 fails, LA call void. |
| la44:LA-44.1 | Cycle verdict | The simulator + ABC recovers Greek inflection from Linear B spelling (4/5 at 583 types, both panels) and is calibrated in-model, but a sign-shuffled Linear A gets the same confident 'isolating' posterior as Linear A itself. | NOT SUPPORTED as a reading of LA's typology. Kept (B, outside the ABC): the raw-panel posterior is dominated by word length; LA's shuffle-invariant profile (short words, 49 LB-valued signs) is matched best by short-rooted isolating ancestors. Next (cycle 2): reference every statistic to its own shuffle (target minus shuffled target, simulation minus shuffled simulation), so only internal structure can drive the posterior and a shuffled target is pushed to the centre. |
| la44:LA-44.2a | Delta panel (33 shuffle-referenced statistics), in-model. | Morphology OOB accuracy 0.754 (raw 0.772): internal structure alone carries nearly all the class information in the simulations. Calibration: 0.45-0.6 -> 0.52, 0.6-0.8 -> 0.67, >= 0.8 -> 0.95 (C4 PASS). Syllable class barely identifiable. | Structure-only inference works in-model. |
| la44:LA-44.2b | LB control, delta panel. | FUS_SUF mode in 1-2/5 (0.661; tie 0.362 vs ISOL 0.360); ISOL mode in 3/5 (0.49, 0.44, 0.68). CCVC mode 5/5 (0.46-0.60). | C1 FAIL: stripped of length/inventory information, Greek's structure is read as isolating. |
| la44:LA-44.2c | Shuffled LA, delta panel; C5 cloud test. | Global shuffles ISOL 0.67-0.87 (entropy 0.31-0.64), position shuffles ISOL 0.75-0.90; all inside the cloud (32-72 % of simulations farther out). | C3 and C5 FAIL: ISOL is the class for 'little structure', and a shuffled target is not out-of-model. |
| la44:LA-44.2d | LA, delta panel. | ISOL 0.79, FUS_SUF 0.115, AGG_SUF 0.048; cloud position 0.90 (central). | VOID (same call as its shuffles). |
| la44:LA-44.2e | 'Both' panel (raw + delta, 77 stats). | OOB 0.782. LB: FUS_SUF mode 5/5 (0.62, 0.44, 0.27, 0.56, 0.53; sub2 beats ISOL 0.251 by 0.02). Shuffles: ISOL 0.69-0.88. LA: ISOL 0.951. | C1 PASS, C3 FAIL, LA VOID. |
| la44:LA-44.2f | Diagnostic (no ABC): LA and LB structure deltas (mean of 5 own shuffles) placed in each class's simulated distribution. | Both real scripts sit outside the simulator on three statistics: same-consonant-series adjacency (cecho delta LA -0.33, LB -0.69; class medians -0.03 to -0.07, LA at the 1-6th percentile), doubled signs (LA -0.36, LB -0.61; sims +0.07 to +0.21, i.e. sims DOUBLE more than their shuffles) and adjacent MI (LA +0.13, LB +0.27; sims +0.32 to +0.41). Where the simulator does cover the data: Kober alternation (same stem, final sign alternating within one consonant series) LA +0.45, LB +1.29; FUS_SUF median +0.57, others -0.25 to +0.13 (LA at the 44th percentile of FUS_SUF, 67-89th of the rest); 2-sign prefix/suffix extensions LA +0.031/+0.027 lie at the 8-14th percentile of every suffixing class; final-alternation richness (alt) LA +0.068 = ISOL median (+0.077), below every affixing class (6-20th pct). | The simulator is misspecified for real syllabic scripts: real writing systems (LB and LA alike) avoid same-series neighbours and doubled signs far more than any simulated history, and have lower sign-to-sign predictability. This misspecification, shared by the positive control, is why the shuffle-flat test cannot pass. On the statistics the simulator does cover, LA looks like a weakly affixing language with Greek-like (Kober-type) final alternation at about one third of Greek's strength (C). |
| la44:LA-44.2 | Cycle verdict | Referencing every statistic to its own shuffle removes the length artefact but loses the LB control; adding raw statistics back restores LB but not the shuffle control. LA is called ISOL under every panel, as are its shuffles. | NOT SUPPORTED. Next (cycle 3): fix the diagnosed misspecification (OCP priors on consonant identity and place, larger lexicons), drop the statistics on which the known control LB lies outside the central 95 % of all simulations (rule fixed before LA is scored), remove the ISOL attractor by asking the conditional question 'given affixation, which kind?', and add the Cypriot control at n = 107. |

All six classes. | OOB 0.768, calibrated (0.45-0.6 -> 0.61, 0.6-0.8 -> 0.73, >= 0.8 -> 0.95). LB FUS_SUF mode 5/5 (0.84, 0.55, 0.36, 0.76, 0.76). Shuffled LA: ISOL 0.89-0.99 (8/8). LA: ISOL 0.98. | C1 PASS (5/5, best so far); C3 FAIL; LA VOID (= shuffles). |

LA: FUS_SUF 0.571, AGG_SUF 0.231, TEMPL 0.128, AGG_BOTH 0.058, AGG_PRE 0.013 (normalized entropy 0.71). Shuffled LA (8): TEMPL mode 6/8, FUS_SUF 2/8 (shufG0 0.585). | C1 PASS; C3 FAIL (shuffles not flat: TEMPL is the new attractor), but LA's call differs from most shuffles. |

LA posteriors: consonants 15-25 (median 20-21, prior 16), vowels 4-8 (median 6 = prior), everything else = prior; no sound-change kind lifted above 1.6x (most 0.8-1.2) and LB gives the same picture. | Histories are not identifiable from a written type list of this size: the 'kind of history that produces Linear A' cannot be read off (void). LA typology: C at best. |
| la53:LA-53.final | Attention per word occurrence (own line, lines spanned, heads the document, extra words, earliness, entry bulk, word length) measured against what quantity, commodity, document size, site and word length predict; 2,000-6,000 random specs; whole-document, within-document and between-document variants. Nulls: attention permuted within commodity x size strata (N1); quantities shuffled (N2); frequency-matched label permutation for the transfer test. Controls: Linear B (DAMOS, 37 status types: deities, wanax, lawagetas, priestesses, sanctuaries, officials) and Ur III (CDLI, 51 status types: gods, deified kings, lugal, ensi2, temples) as opaque ids; planted hierarchies in the real Linear A rows. Behaviour tests: site spread, non-administrative objects, round and small quantities. | Planted hierarchies are recovered (AUC 0.65-0.81, p 0.01). Real status is not: LB AUC 0.56 (p 0.24), Ur III 0.46 (p 0.78); within-document 0.45 / 0.45; best specs from one administration do not transfer to the other (p 0.64 and 0.31; spec AUC correlation -0.07). In both controls status words come LATE, not first (head AUC 0.50 LB, 0.39 Ur III). Known LB status words spread over more sites and take smaller amounts (p 0.05, 0.045); high-surplus LB words are site-local instead. Linear A: 2 / 1 types at p < 0.01 (1.8 expected), none at q < 0.1; split-half rho 0.21 (p 0.075). Its high-surplus words (A-PA-RA-NE, PA-DE, MA-KA-RI-TE, A-DU, *516, KU-NI-SU, SA-RA2) are tablet-only, mostly HT-only, sit first with extra words, and carry LARGER amounts than usual for the commodity (rho +0.15, p 0.004). | NOT CRACKED. A (method, calibrated negative): in two real administrations, scribes do not spend measurably more clay or layout on gods, kings or sanctuaries once quantity and commodity are allowed for, and no layout recipe for status carries from one to the other. So layout surplus cannot rank Linear A referents by status. B: Linear A surplus attention marks list structure and big entries (headings and transaction words), not rank. C: PA-DE (top in both rankings, p 0.01-0.02, n 3; HT 9a/b, HT 122a, small amounts 1-5.5) as a specially marked entry. Would support: PA-DE on a new tablet again with its own lines and a small amount; would kill: PA-DE as an ordinary crowded entry. Sign size could not be tested (no SigLA sizes in our parse). |

But they differ in distance as well as in meaning (long-range vs adjacent); the test cannot tell the two apart. |

Each learner writes 3x the documents and the 255 kept are chosen greedily to hold 40 random real features (any family) at their starting counts. 100 chains vs plain chains. | Targets AUC 0.29 vs 0.08 in plain chains (one copy: 0.55 vs 0.24; 85 % of targets gain). The other 51 features are unchanged (0.06 vs 0.05; 55 % gain). Long-range targets (13) gain only 0.04 vs 0.015: selecting among learner outputs cannot rebuild structure that no learner writes. | PASS: an outside force that holds structure in place shows up as retention, so the measure tracks preservation. It is weak for long-range structure. |
