| la52:LA-52.1d | Control: Linear B KN+PY at LA size (sign identities only), 100 + 100 chains. Known meaning-bearing structures: totals (to-so), commodity-unit pairs (logogram->scale), person-ration links (word->logogram). | Totals: excess < 3 at this size (untestable). Commodity->scale AUC 0.31 and word->logogram 0.38 are the MOST persistent families (co-occurrence 0.18, entry->entry 0.03, logogram order 0.05). Fast-decaying words: personal names that recur as a group (a-pe-te-u, ri-ja-ko, ke-ki, po-ti-na-jo ~0.005-0.01); persistent: the land-tenure formula (ko-to-no-o-ko 0.61, e-ke 0.34, pa-ro 0.33, ko-to-na 0.26, pe-mo 0.24). | CONTROL FAILS for adjacent meaning (commodity-unit, person-ration): learners copy any adjacent pairing, meaningful or not (as in la46). Partial pass: the recurring groups of named men (a world fact) decay fastest, and formulae (learnable templates) refuse to die. |
| la52:LA-52.1e | Control: Ur III at LA size (4,000-document pool, sample 3,600 tokens), 100 + 100 chains. | Families: number->number 0.01, co-occurrence 0.03, entry->entry 0.03, site 0.03 | sign pair 0.14, first/last 0.25. Running totals: excess < 3. Fastest words: ba-zal, ga, us2-sa-bi, diri, 8(disz)-kam, month/festival names (ezem-me-ki-gal2), dumu (dating frame and kin terms); most persistent: 5(disz)-kam, saga, tug2, ninda, unu{ki}, i3-gesz (goods). | Mixed. The dating frame (calendar = world) decays fastest, as wanted; goods persist (they are frequent and topic-clustered), against the hope. 8-kam fast and 5-kam slow show the word-level noise floor (~0.1 AUC). |
| la52:LA-52.1 | Cycle verdict | Planted world vs meaningless rules separate (P), and the preservation null works. But the separation is by distance: long-range structure dies in all three corpora whatever it means, and adjacent structure survives whatever it means (LB commodity-unit and person-ration links are the most persistent features). | The instrument measures how hard a structure is to learn (distance, rarity), not meaning. Cycle 2: single-learner-type chains in a gentle regime, so that 'dies under EVERY learner, including the topic and neural learners that can hold long-range structure' becomes the criterion; frequency-matched word scores. |
| la52:LA-52.2a | Planted control, LA + 4 rules, 390 chains. | World rules: best 0.08 (latent official, by the topic learner) and 0.00 (exact sum); percentile 0.06 and 0.00 of 108 features. Meaningless adjacent rules: best 1.05 and 0.77 (percentile 0.94 and 0.74). Learner types disagree on what they keep (feature AUC Spearman n-gram vs template 0.07, topic vs neural 0.24; n-gram vs topic 0.73). | PASS, again by distance as well as meaning. The GRU (4-12 epochs on 3,600 tokens) keeps almost nothing (family medians <= 0.13), so 'best' comes from the n-gram and topic learners. |
| la52:LA-52.2b | Linear A, 390 + 390 chains, 91 features. | Best-learner family medians: TOT 0.04, entry->entry 0.06, co-occurrence 0.15, site 0.16, logogram order 0.17, word->far logogram 0.21, number->number 0.26 | sign pair 0.60, word->logogram 0.63, first/last 0.71, logogram->scale 0.89, word->scale 1.00. Lowest single features: KU-RO = running sum 0.04; SA-RA2 -> NI 0.06; *28B-NU-MA-RE / SI-PI-KI as successive entries 0.06; NI -> VIN 0.07; OLE+U before OLE+MI 0.11; KU-RO and SA-RA2 at Haghia Triada 0.13-0.14. Words (frequency-matched z): only OLE+RI < -2 (z -3.4); next QA2+[?]+PU -1.9, *28B-NU-MA-RE -1.7, SI-PI-KI -1.7, *305, A-DU; most persistent GRA+PA +8.0, OLE+DI +6.7, GRA +4.8, I +4.2, JA-SA-SA-RA-ME +3.1. The same chain-replicated names (SI-PI-KI, *28B-NU-MA-RE, KA-PA, KA-DI, MI-NU-TE, DA-SI-*118) head the list in the planted corpus run (separate chains). | The lowest-retention LA structures are the known ones: the KU-RO total and the SA-RA2 basket order (SA-RA2 -> NI -> VIN, la46/la20). But inside their families they are typical (entry->entry median 0.06), so this is 'long-range and ordered', not a separate meaning signal. Word z beyond +-2: 1 low, 5 high of 84 (the high tail is real: topic-learnable words). |
| la52:LA-52.2c | Control: Linear B KN+PY at LA size, 390 + 390 chains, 92 features. | Lowest best-learner retention: do-so-mo lists (entry->entry 0.03-0.04), ko-wa -> ko-wo (girls then boys, 0.06; co-occurrence 0.11), the logogram order *150 -> *142 -> CORN (0.07-0.08), me-u-jo-a2 -> ko-ru-to (0.06), site x personal names at KN (0.11-0.17). Highest: the land-tenure formula (ko-to-na / pe-mo 1.51, pe-mo -> GRA 1.66, te-o-jo z +36, do-e-ro +28, ko-to-no-o-ko +18, e-ke +12, ta-ra-si-ja +10); to-so -> GRA (total -> commodity, adjacent) 2.23; GRA -> fraction 2.05. | PARTIAL PASS. Known meaning-bearing ORDERED or GROUPED structures die under every learner (census order ko-wa -> ko-wo, the KN commodity order, contribution lists), and formulae refuse to die. FAIL for adjacent meaning: commodity-unit (logogram->scale) and total->commodity are among the most persistent features. The method sees meaning only where it lives at a distance. |
| la52:LA-52.2 | Cycle verdict | Requiring loss under every learner type does not change the cycle-1 picture: retention is set by the relation's distance and by how template-like the documents are. In LB this separates formula vocabulary (persistent) from census/order structure (anchored). In LA the anchored end is KU-RO and the SA-RA2 basket order, already known (la1, la46); no new LA word stands out (one of 84 below z -2). | Cycle 3: replicate on disjoint halves of the documents (LA, LB, Ur III). Word z must predict the other half. |
| la52:LA-52.3a | Ur III control (halves of the LA-size sample). | Family order replicates (rho 0.90). Features beyond family: rho 0.93 vs within-family shuffle 0.52 +- 0.08, P 0.0005 (40 shared). Words: rho 0.28, P 0.009 (70 shared). Anchored in both halves: ba-ti and i3-dab5 ('received'), ba-usz2 ('died'), ba-zi ('withdrawn'), u4 (day), 3(disz)-kam (dating), maszkim (commissioner), ab2, masz2, ud5 (animals), ma-na. Persistent in both: sila3, gur, dug, ninda (measures and containers), ba-du3. | PASS. In Ur III the instrument replicates and splits the vocabulary meaningfully: transaction verbs, dates and the goods counted decay; measures and fixed phrases persist. |
| la52:LA-52.3b | Linear B control. | Family order rho 0.96. Features beyond family: 0.66 vs 0.63 +- 0.09, P 0.39 (13 shared, low power). Words: rho 0.68, P 0.005 (14 shared). Anchored in both: ko-wa, *150, *142, CORN (ko-wo and to-so-de in one half). Persistent in both: pe-mo, ke-ke-me-na, pa-ro, e-ke-qe, o-na-to, GRA. | PASS at word level. Census and commodity-series vocabulary decays, the land-tenure formula persists, in both halves. Most of the correlation comes from the formula end. |
| la52:LA-52.3c | Linear A halves. | Family order rho 1.00 (ES 0.08-0.10, SW 0.15-0.17 ... WN 0.87-1.00). Features beyond family: rho 0.84 vs 0.50 +- 0.39, P 0.47 (10 shared). Words: rho 0.03, P 0.46 (23 shared). Same sign in both halves only for KA (-0.6/-1.1), KU-RO (-0.3/-0.8), TU, CYP (anchored side, all |z| < 1.2) and TE, SI, GRA, *304, GRA+PA, OLE+U (persistent side). SA-RA2 -0.9/+0.6, VIN -1.4/+1.2, RE -1.9/+1.2, VIR+[?] -1.4/+1.1: these flip. | FAIL: Linear A word anchoring does not replicate across halves (LB passes at 14 words, Ur III at 70). Only the family ordering (a property of the learners) replicates. Cycle-2 word candidates (OLE+RI, SI-PI-KI, *28B-NU-MA-RE) are not testable in halves (too rare) and stay unsupported. |
| la52:LA-52.3 | Cycle verdict | Calibrated: the held-out test passes in both known corpora (Ur III P 0.009, LB P 0.005) and fails in Linear A (P 0.46). | No Linear A word is shown to carry world-anchored structure by this method. What remains for LA is the cycle-1/2 family-level statement and the known long-range items (KU-RO total, SA-RA2 basket order). |
| la52:LA-52.final | Novelty: iterated learning (a language-evolution method) used as a meaning detector on an undeciphered corpus. A chain of simulated learners each learns the corpus from the previous generation's output and writes a new corpus of the same size. Structure the writing system can carry by itself should survive or grow; structure that dies under every learner must have been held by something outside the writing (the world being recorded). About 7,000 chains (~30,000 learned-and-rewritten corpora): random mixtures of back-off n-gram, slot-template, topic-clustered and GRU learners with random bottleneck, copy noise and memory limits (cycle 1, harsh); single-type chains (cycle 2, gentle); disjoint document halves (cycle 3). Measure: excess of every relation instance over a kind-preserving shuffle, and its retention relative to chains started from a shuffled corpus (the learners' own attractor). Nulls: shuffled-start chains; an objective that preserves 40 arbitrary features. Controls: planted world-anchored rules (latent official, exact sum) vs planted learnable-meaningless rules (word before GRA, word -> fixed scale); Linear B KN+PY and Ur III at LA size, words as opaque ids; held-out halves. | Planted: world rules retain 0.00-0.08 (percentile 0.00-0.14), meaningless rules 0.21-1.05 (percentile 0.74-0.94). The preservation objective lifts its targets (0.29 vs 0.08). In all three corpora retention is ordered by relation DISTANCE: running totals, entry succession, co-occurrence, logogram order and site decay in about one copy; word->scale, logogram->scale and first/last word survive. Linear B: ko-wa -> ko-wo (census order), the *150 -> *142 -> CORN order and contribution lists die under every learner; the land-tenure formula (te-o-jo, do-e-ro, ko-to-no-o-ko, e-ke, pe-mo) refuses to die; commodity->unit and to-so -> GRA are among the MOST persistent (the control fails for adjacent meaning). Ur III: transaction verbs, dates and counted animals decay, measures persist; word anchoring replicates across halves (rho 0.28, P 0.009). LB: replicates (rho 0.68, P 0.005). Linear A: lowest retention = KU-RO running total (0.04) and the SA-RA2 basket order (SA-RA2 -> NI 0.06, NI -> VIN 0.07), but both are typical of their families; word anchoring does NOT replicate across halves (rho 0.03, P 0.46). | NOT CRACKED. A (method, calibrated): iterated-learning decay ranks structures by how far apart their parts are, not by whether they mean something. Meaning held between neighbours (commodity-unit, total->commodity, person->ration) is copied as easily as meaningless adjacent rules, so it can never look world-anchored. B (calibrated negative): no Linear A word or sign carries replicable world-anchored structure by this measure (it passes in LB and Ur III at the same size). B (convergent, known): KU-RO totals and the SA-RA2 -> NI -> VIN basket are the Linear A structures no learner keeps, the same items la1 and la46 found by other routes. They add no new meaning. C: OLE+RI (z -3.4, full corpus only) is the one LA sign whose long-range links die faster than its frequency predicts. Would support: z < -2 in both halves once more tablets with OLE+RI exist. Would kill: z > -1 in a re-run with a new seed. B: the commodity signs GRA+PA, OLE+DI, GRA and the word JA-SA-SA-RA-ME are 'learnable' (they keep their long-range links under topic learners), i.e. they behave like the LB formula words. They sit in repeated document templates and are not world-anchored. |

Would support: HT95b joined to, or re-read with, a GRA sign. Would kill: a non-grain commodity sign on HT95b. |

ONLY com=SAME: -9.1. | Linear A KU-RO sometimes adds across logograms and sometimes keeps one per commodity. Post hoc reading (C): a logogram written after a name (TA-I AROM, VIR+*313a in a list of names) is counted like any entry; a commodity column (OLIV vs *308, CAPm vs *21M/*21F) gets its own total. |

| la56:LA-56 | Model each total that does not add up as an inclusion habit (entries of some word / sign / commodity / role left out, subtracted, doubled; sub-totals, after-total lines or the other side added; fraction signs swapped, ignored or read as a whole unit; total = number of lines) and as a misplaced section cut. Nulls: totals reassigned, entries dealt across tablets, noise added to balanced totals. Controls: planted exclusions and shifted cuts in real LA; Ur III szunigin count lists (values hidden); LB to-so (too few). | No rule beats the nulls (real best 3.0 nats vs N1 4.6, N2 6.1, N3 4.7; 0 replicated rules). Planted excluded words rank 1st of ~560 single rules in 21/22 runs. Ur III gives back a real excluded category (kin-gur-ra / udu-gal lines with their own szunigin). LA mismatches are as explicable as noise on balanced totals (<= 2 changes: 0.48 vs 0.50), Ur III's are more (0.60 vs 0.43). No word, sign or commodity takes the blame (best family-wise P 0.08). Re-cut documents: 5 vs 4.7 under noise (planted 19.5). | Negative, calibrated: the Linear A arithmetic errors do not carry an exclusion habit or a hidden grouping at this corpus size. |
| la55:LA-55.final | WORDS GROW LIKE CITIES (tools/la55_*.py; loops/la55_final.txt). Per-word and per-sign urban-scaling fits across archives (35 LA deposits, 103 LB find areas, 277 Ur III site-years): Poisson exponent beta, threshold gain and occupancy, each z-scored against documents re-dealt to archives (N1 free, N3 within genre, N4 within genre x length; 300-1,000 each) plus a bootstrap (N2). Fixed class rule (saturating / linear / superlinear / threshold). Controls: LB full and at LA size, Ur III, with known function words, goods and names; a planted class. Further tests: Heaps per independent class, held-out presence prediction, inverse 'census' (archive size from 8 surviving documents), settlement hectares (Whitelaw; gtp; Wikipedia). | Controls: function words do not saturate relative to names in LB, LBS or Ur III (beta AUC 0.39-0.59, occupancy 0.27-0.39, p >= 0.18). Names-linear holds only in full LB under N3 (p 0.037); it is lost under N4 (0.056), at LA size (0.23-0.60) and in Ur III (0.70-0.87). Held-out saturating predictions do worse than proportional (LB -0.032, LBS -0.056 bits). The census estimate gives rho -0.08 (LB, LBS). Planted: saturating 15/15, linear 11-14/15, superlinear 1-5/15. LA: robust saturating = GRA, OLIV, HIDE, KI, TI, *188, *131B, *86-RO, TE-RI; threshold = CYP, MI, QA2, *411-VS, *164, *306, O, DI-NA-U (the nodule signs occur only at KH and HT R13); 115-117/141 linear. KU-RO, VIN and SA-RA2 change class with the null. LA sign saturation (37/98) was document length (7/98 under N4). LA held-out -0.008 bits (N3 +0.013); census rho 0.06 (p 0.30); no lexical closure (Heaps 0.788 vs 0.797). la45 x la55 p 0.018 (N3) / 0.10 (N4). Hectares vs logogram types rho 0.81 (p 0.042, n 6), slope ~0.4-0.5; richness given documents rho -0.17. | NOT CRACKED. KILLED by its controls: archives do not grow like cities in LB or Ur III either (A, method negative). LA scaling exponents predict nothing held-out (B). Descriptive classes (B): staples and hides saturate; Khania/HT-R13 nodule signs are 'threshold' = locality. C: la45 commodities lean to saturation; bigger towns left more documents and logogram types sublinearly. |
| la55:LA-55.1a | Planted control: 60 words planted in the LA documents, 15 each saturating (a random archive regardless of size), linear (random document), superlinear (archive weight N^1.6), threshold (archives N >= 30 only); 6-25 documents each | N1: SAT 15/15, LIN 13/15, THR 14/15, SUP 5/15 (9 read as THR). N3: SAT 15/15, LIN 14/15, THR 7/15, SUP 2/15 | Saturation and linearity are recoverable at LA size; threshold only under N1; superlinear is NOT separable from threshold in LA (only two archives above 100 documents: HT R13 and Khania). |
| la55:LA-55.1b | Linear B control, known classes (FUNC = totals, deficits, transaction verbs; COMM = logograms; PERSON = opener of person-headed series; PLACE = la45 list). Prediction: FUNC saturates (lower beta, higher occupancy than PERSON). AUC vs 2,000 label permutations | Full LB (771 types): FUNC<PERSON beta AUC 0.59 (N1, perm p 0.18) / 0.39 (N3, p 0.89); FUNC>PERSON occupancy 0.33 / 0.27 (p 0.97 / 0.99). LBS: 0.27 / 0.34 (p 0.98 / 0.93). COMM>PERSON threshold gain: LB 0.54 (p 0.27) / 0.57 (p 0.16); LBS 0.66 (p 0.04) / 0.65 (p 0.07) | KILLED in the control: Greek administrative function words do NOT saturate; they are series-bound and concentrate in the archives that keep those series (threshold/superlinear as often as saturating). Commodities lean to threshold only weakly. |

| la55:LA-55.1d | 'Names grow in proportion' (tools/la55_c2b.py): share of types deviating from LINEAR, PERSON vs FUNC+COMM, labels permuted within frequency quartiles (rarer names must not win by low power), 20,000 permutations | LB N3: PERSON 0.05 (n 44) vs FUNC+COMM 0.50 (n 46), p 0.037; PLACE 0.12 vs 0.50, p 0.0075. LB N1: p 0.93. LBS (LA size) N3: 0.00 (n 15) vs 0.49, p 0.23. LA (la45 ENTRY+RARE vs HEADER+COMMODITY) N3: 0.11 vs 0.32, p 0.53 | At full LB size and within-genre null, names and places grow in proportion while function words and goods deviate (weak, p 0.04 / 0.008). At Linear A size the control loses it (p 0.23), so LA cannot be read this way. |
| la55:LA-55.1e | Linear A words (141 types), classes under N3 (fair null; N1 is dominated by the HT R13 nodule dump, which makes 93/141 'saturating') | N3: LINEAR 117, SATURATING 13 (GRA, VIN, KU-RO, SA-RA2, OLIV, KI, TI, *188, *22F, HIDE, *131B, *86-RO, TE-RI), THRESHOLD 9 (CYP, MI, QA2, *301 logogram, *411-VS, *164, *306, O, DI-NA-U), SUPERLINEAR 2 (*401, JE). Threshold T: *301, *164, *306, *411-VS only at Khania/HT R13 (T 219); MI, QA2 T 72; CYP T 9 | Staple goods and the total word KU-RO are 'infrastructure': every small archive has them at a higher per-document rate than the big ones. The 'threshold' words are the Khania / HT R13 nodule signs: with two big archives a size threshold cannot be told from a regional or deposit habit. |

Suspected confound: documents in small LA archives (libation vessels, tablets) are longer. Re-tested with a length-stratified null in cycle 2. |

Saturating in BOTH N3 and N4: GRA, OLIV, HIDE, KI, TI, *188, *131B, *86-RO, TE-RI. Threshold in both: CYP, MI, QA2, *164, *306, O, *411-VS, DI-NA-U. Unstable: VIN, SA-RA2 (saturating N3 only), KU-RO (saturating N3, threshold N4) | Robust LA 'infrastructure' = grain, olives, hides and a few single signs; KU-RO's class depends on the null, so it is not graded. |

LA (la45 ENTRY+RARE vs HEADER+COMMODITY) p 0.95 | The only control-supported pattern (names linear, LB p 0.037 under N3) is fragile (p 0.056 under N4) and absent at LA size. |
| la55:LA-55.2f | la45 classes x la55 classes, chi-square vs 5,000 permutations of la45 labels (37 typed words) | N1 p 0.005 (genre-driven), N3 p 0.018 (COMMODITY 6/14 saturating, HEADER 0/14 saturating, 3/14 threshold), N4 p 0.10 (COMMODITY 4/14 saturating) | Weak link: la45 commodities lean to saturation and headers to threshold, but it does not survive the length-stratified null at p < 0.05. |
| la55:LA-55.2g | Held-out presence (tools/la55_c2.py): leave out each small archive (N <= 100), predict presence of each word (>= 3 docs elsewhere) from shrunk scaling vs proportional; real vs 200 N1 and N3 re-dealings | LA shrunk -0.0076 bits/word-archive (n 4,555), N1 -0.0072 (p 0.59), N3 +0.013 (p 1.00); raw -0.017 (N1 -0.036, p 0.02; N3 +0.006, p 1.00) | Scaling exponents do not predict which words a held-out Linear A archive contains better than proportional sampling; on within-genre shuffles they do better than on the real data. |

| la55:LA-55.3u | Ur III control (277 site x year archives, 10,988 docs, 3,730 types; 300 re-dealings each N1/N3/N4) | FUNC<PERSON beta AUC 0.51 / 0.42 / 0.44 (perm p 0.42 / 0.94 / 0.88); FUNC>PERSON occupancy 0.34-0.39 (p >= 0.99); FUNC classes N3: SUPERLINEAR 15, LINEAR 11, SATURATING 8 (n 34). Names-linear (frequency-binned): p 0.70 / 0.87 / 0.79 | KILLED in the second control: Sumerian function words (verbs, totals, units, offices, dates) are as often SUPERLINEAR as saturating; names are not more linear than other words once frequency is matched. |

LBS -0.08 (17; p 0.48 / 0.69). LB -0.08 (25; N1 -0.01 p 0.64; N3 0.05 p 0.92) | KILLED at the control: scaling words do not tell how big an archive was, in Linear B either. | la49:LA-49.1a | F3 scale-raise running-sum circuit; drivers = consensus z of words before the most reactive numbers; null = the same on 3 shuffled corpora (99th pct 1.13). | Cross-seed consistency of driver vectors LA 0.38 vs shuffled 0.26-0.42 (no gap). Planted TOTAL words rank 8, 16, 19, 24, 60 of 92 (none above the null). Linear B 4x: to-so 27/190, to-sa 122/190; Ur III: szunigin (after its number) 13/40. Linear A: KU-RO 49/90, KI-RO 40/90. | la49:LA-49.1b | F2/F1 number head: in each transformer the head with the largest number-specific held-out damage (> 0.05 nats and > 2x word / logogram damage); its attention attractor words. | Specific number heads in 0/5 LA seeds vs 4/5 in each shuffled corpus, 1/5 LB, 1/4 LB4, 2/5 UR, 1/5 PLANT. Planted BIND words rank 6, 81, 140, 144, 149 of 167; LB logogram attraction AUC 0.54 (LB4 0.66), Ur III commodity/unit 0.53. | KILLED: no head is found consistently in any corpus and the attractor read-off names neither the planted words nor the control logograms. Attention patterns of these models are not a usable readout. |
| la49:LA-49.1c | F4 representation geometry: mean pairwise correlation of word-type similarity matrices across seeds (RSA); consensus clusters scored against control truth (ARI vs 500 label shuffles); PC1 of consensus geometry vs commodity/person. | RSA LA 0.30 vs shuffled 0.09-0.11 (PLANT 0.35, LB 0.31, LB4 0.22, UR 0.53). Clusters match truth in LB (ARI 0.11, P < 0.002, 85 labelled types) and UR (0.25, P < 0.002); PC1 separates commodity from person: LB 0.97, LB4 0.95, UR 0.81 (3 persons only). | PASS as a method (controls recover classes). LA geometry is reproducible across seeds 3x above shuffled: models are forced into a shared word geometry by the data. What that geometry encodes is read in cycle 2 with function vectors instead of raw states. Grade B (method), no LA reading yet. |
| la49:LA-49.2a | Entry-duplication running-sum probe (replaces 1a): mask a number, insert a copy of the latest earlier (word, number) entry; a running total must rise, a scale-tracker need not. Drivers = consensus z of words before / after the reacting numbers. | Cross-seed consistency LA 0.11 (PRE), shuffled 0.12-0.17, PLANT 0.05. Planted TOTAL words rank 52-77 of 92; LB 4x to-so 127/173, to-sa 128/173; Ur III 4x szunigin 35/79; LA KU-RO 78/90. | KILLED: no tiny model of any corpus learns to add, not even Ur III at 4x. The absence of a sum circuit in Linear A models is therefore uninformative. |
| la49:LA-49.2b | Function vectors by transplant: put every word type into 120 fixed entry slots of real documents and read what the model expects of the neighbouring number (13-bucket distribution, scale and duplication sensitivity, expected log value). Cross-seed RSA of the vectors; drivers per component. | RSA of function vectors ~0 everywhere (LA 0.02, shuffled 0.00-0.01, PLANT 0.01, LB4 0.01, UR4 0.01): what a word 'does' to a foreign slot is seed-specific in every corpus. Only the expected-size component is consistent: LA 0.18 (shuffled 0.03-0.05; LB4 0.18; UR4 0.04). Its top LA word is KU-RO (z 1.60 > shuffled max 1.54), then HIDE+[?], GRA+F, DA-RI-DA, A-JA. But planted totals rank 46-165 of 188, to-so 78/792, to-sa 126/792, szunigin 230/758. | KILLED as a method (all three controls fail to name their total words). KU-RO at the top only restates that big numbers follow it (la1, A). |
| la49:LA-49.3a | Convergent features: FastICA (12 components) of final-layer states per model; a component is universal if a partner with abs r >= 0.5 exists in >= 50 % of other seeds over the same token positions; firing words; causal projection. | Mean best-match r: LA 0.31, shuffled 0.29 / 0.33, PLANT 0.29, LB4 0.32, UR4 0.18. Universal share LA 2.8 % = shuffled 2.8 % (0 % in the other shuffle); PLANT 0 universal (no feature fires on the planted totals); LB4 17 % (3 models), its features are place/textile groups, none fires on to-so numbers (1-7 of 56). | KILLED: independently trained models of Linear A do not converge on token-level features any more than models of shuffled Linear A. |
| la49:LA-49.3b | Word geometry, replicated across populations: consensus similarity of word-type states after regressing out profiles (count, logogram flag, P(number next / before), P(first)) and document co-occurrence; clusters from the cycle-1 population tested for cohesion in the cycle-2 population (Bonferroni over clusters). | Residual cross-seed RSA LA 0.22 (shuffled 0.07-0.09; PLANT 0.26; LB 0.28; UR 0.44). Cross-population agreement LA r 0.70, shuffled 0.35-0.37, PLANT 0.54, LB4 0.51. Controls: LB COMMODITY, PERSON, PLACE groups cohesive (P <= 0.0005; TOTAL, 3 types, not); UR COMMODITY, PLACE, OFFICE, VERB cohesive; the 5 planted TOTAL words fall in one population-1 cluster (cohesion 0.34, P < 0.0005) but only P 0.04 in population 2. Replicated clusters: LA 3 of 6, shuffled 1 of 9 and 0 of 11, PLANT 2 of 7, LB4 2 of 7. | PASS as a method (controls, shuffle gap, planted role partly). LA groups that replicate: (i) single signs of sealings (*301, KU, KA, SI, RO, TE, ZE, I, TA, A, O, JA, DA, *164, *411-VS; cohesion 0.34); (ii) counted commodities with NI, *304, *306, *308, E, SU (CYP, OLE, VIN); (iii) libation-formula words with signs PA, NA, TU, NE, QE, DI (A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA, JA-SA-SA-RA-ME, SI-RU-TE, I-PI-NA-MA, KU-PA3-NU, DI-NA-U). |
| la49:LA-49.3c | Causal test of the replicated groups: in each saved model project the group direction out of the final state at masked held-out tokens; loss change on the group's tokens vs other tokens, against 20 random word groups per model. | Sealing single signs: +1.31 nats on own tokens, +0.00 elsewhere, 12/12 models, P 0.008 vs random groups (PLANT copy: +0.96 vs +0.03, 8/8). Commodity and libation groups: no specific damage (-0.08, -0.11; P 0.73, 0.76). Planted totals: +0.55 vs +0.19, 7/8 models, P 0.06. Shuffled LA's one replicated group: +0.07, P 0.18. | One circuit survives every filter: a 'sealing-vocabulary' direction, the same class la45 found by a different route (B). Commodity and libation groups are consistent geometry but not circuits (C). |
| la49:LA-49.final | Train-and-dissect 115 tiny models, 6 circuit families, each with shuffled, planted and Linear B / Ur III controls (loops/la49_cycle1-3.txt) | Killed (controls fail): running-sum units (scale and duplication probes; no model of any corpus learns to add, not Ur III at 4x); number heads and attention attractors (no head recurs across seeds; planted bindings and control logograms not named); function vectors (seed-specific everywhere); convergent ICA features (LA = shuffled). Survives: word geometry beyond profiles and co-occurrence is reproducible across seeds (residual RSA 0.22 vs shuffled 0.07-0.09) and across two independent populations (r 0.70 vs 0.35-0.37), recovers LB and Ur III classes and partly the planted totals. 3 LA groups replicate; only one is a circuit: the sealing single-sign direction (*301, KU, KA, SI, RO, TE, ZE, I, TA, A, O, JA, DA, *164, *411-VS), whose ablation costs +1.31 nats on its own held-out tokens and 0.00 elsewhere in 12/12 models (P 0.008). | B: the sealing single signs form one class that the data force on every model (independent replication of la45's heading class; it is a vocabulary of nodules and roundels, its function unknown). C: counted-commodity group (NI, *304, *306, *308, E, SU with CYP, OLE, VIN) and libation-formula group with the signs PA, NA, TU, NE, QE, DI: stable geometry, but ablation is not specific. Would support: the same groups in models trained on new finds; would kill: those groups dissolving when HT 86/95-type duplicates and the stone vessels are held out. A (method, negative): tiny models trained on 5,000-20,000 tokens build no arithmetic, number-binding or word-function circuits even for Linear B and Ur III, so the absence of such circuits in Linear A models says nothing about Linear A. Not cracked. |

| la60:LA-60.1a | Simpler models vs the grammar skeleton, held-out LA (10 splits, ~192 test documents, G0 ~23,190 bits per half) | B0 +2,346 bits, B1 +1,486 bits worse than G0 (G0 better in 10/10). Linear B at LA size: B0 +2,211, B1 +367. | The role skeleton (kind classes + number-in-context) is a strong document model, much stronger relative to a trigram in LA than in LB (A, method). It needs no reading. |
| la60:LA-60.1b | PRIOR reading in the grammar vs G0 and vs the same grammar with its roles shuffled | PRIOR vs G0 +98 bits (worse in 9/10). PRIOR vs shuffled roles -159 bits (better than all 20 shuffles in 10/10 splits); vs shuffles keeping TOT fixed, P 0.00 median. Mixture with B1: +197 vs G0. | B (calibrated, two-sided): the reading's role assignment is far from arbitrary (shuffles lose 160 bits), but on held-out likelihood it adds NOTHING over the bare kind grammar: what it says is largely already visible in whether a token is a logogram, a single sign or a word. |
| la60:LA-60.1c | INDUCED reading (train-only rules) | vs G0 +82 (worse 9/10); vs shuffles -59 (better than all shuffles in 5/10, median P 0.03). | B: the blind rules recover part of PRIOR (KU-RO, NI, KI, DI, RE, *304, *308, *306, E; A-DU, KA-PA, KU-NI-SU as list openers) and with weaker effect. |
| la60:LA-60.1d | Arithmetic component (number after TOT = running sum, theta fitted on train) | Train: 4-8 of 12-18 KU-RO sections close (theta 0.28-0.47). Held-out saving 36 bits per half (PRIOR), 25 (INDUCED). Linear B at LA size: to-so present but 0-2 closing sections per half, saving 0-2 bits; INDUCED never finds to-so. | A (known, replicated as a generative gain): KU-RO arithmetic is the one component of the reading that pays on unseen documents. LA is MORE arithmetic-friendly than LB at the same size. |
| la60:LA-60.1e | Commodity order component (beta fitted on train) | beta = 0 for PRIOR in 10/10 splits; saving -1 bit; INDUCED +1 bit. LB -4 to -7. | B (negative for likelihood): the order (la20, A by pair accuracy) does not make commodity identities more predictable once the tablet's other commodities and context are modelled; it is a rank effect on few pairs. |
| la60:LA-60.1f | Calibration: a TRUE partial Linear B reading (LBPRIOR) through the same pipeline at LA size | LBPRIOR vs G0 -211 bits (better 9/10); vs shuffled roles -45 (better than every shuffle in 0/10; median P 0.23); mixture with B1 -726. | The calibration does not show the same pattern: a correct partial LB reading of this kind DOES beat the empty grammar, but beats its own shuffles less. LA's reading is better than shuffles yet not better than nothing. So 'beats shuffled roles' is necessary, not sufficient, and LA's reading carries less held-out information than a true LB partial reading of similar kind and size (B). |
| la60:LA-60.2a | Primary frozen split, PRIOR / INDUCED, 194 held-out documents | PRIOR: FULL 49, STRICT 7, totals tested 16 / closed 7, order pairs agree 75 / against 19. INDUCED: FULL 38, STRICT 11, closed 7/16, agree 108 / against 45. EMPTY: FULL 35. | Only a quarter of held-out documents are explained in full, and the EMPTY reading already 'explains' 35 trivially (one entry, one number). |
| la60:LA-60.2b | 10 splits, INDUCED (clean) vs shuffled roles and random readings | FULL 43.0 vs shuffled 41.0 (P~0.35) vs random 38.1 vs EMPTY 40.1. STRICT 10.9 vs 5.8 (P~0.06) vs 2.6. Closing totals 5.2 vs 0.3 (P~0.04). Order agreements 155 vs 75; violations 66 vs 75 (agree share 0.70 vs 0.50). | B: a clean, train-frozen reading explains held-out documents better than its shuffles only through KU-RO arithmetic and the commodity order. The gain is 5.1 STRICT documents per ~191, 2.7 % of held-out documents. |
| la60:LA-60.2c | 10 splits, PRIOR (contaminated) vs controls | FULL 49.9 vs 41.3 (P~0.03) vs random 42.7; STRICT 9.2 vs 3.7 (P~0.03); closing totals 5.8 vs 0.4; order agree 90.7 / against 27.9 (0.76) vs shuffled 17.9 / 18.3. Coverage 107.9 vs 100.9. | The hand-assembled reading does better on FULL (+8.6 documents, 4.5 %), but it was built from loops that saw these documents, so the extra over INDUCED is an upper bound. |
| la60:LA-60.2d | Calibration: Linear B at Linear A size, same pipeline | LBPRIOR (true partial reading): FULL 35.5 vs 31.6 (P~0.04); STRICT 8.4 vs 4.3 (P~0.03); gain 4.1 documents per 141 = 2.9 %; closing totals 0.7 (to-so rarely closes at this size); order agree 64.8 / against 10.7 (0.86) vs 37.5 / 37.6. INDUCED on LB: STRICT 5.2 vs 3.2 (P~0.12), never finds to-so. | A correct partial reading of the same KIND as LA's gains the same fraction of documents (2.9 % vs 2.7-2.9 %). The pipeline cannot tell a correct partial reading from LA's reading at this size: LA's reading is about as good as a true LB partial reading of roles, and the gain is small in both. This calibration says the role layer is plausibly right, and that a role layer is far from a reading. |
| la60:LA-60.2e | What the FULL glosses look like (PRIOR, held-out) | e.g. 'HT85a: header A-DU [heading/transaction]; *307+*387 VIR+[?] DA-RI-DA 12 PA3-NI 12 U-*325-ZA 6 DA-SI-*118 24 KU-ZU-NI 5 TE-KE 3 DA-RE 4 / KU-RO = TOTAL 66 (sum 66: closes) / commodities *307 > VIR'; 'HT127b: ... KU-RO = TOTAL 292 (running sum 292: closes) / *307 > VIR > KI'; 'HT105: header KA-PA; VIR+[?] 234 SA-RA2 VIR+[?] 235'. Typical failures: HT9a (closes, but heading SA-RO unknown), HT122a/b (KU-RO 31 vs 22, 65 vs 15), HT94a (110 vs 111), HT125a (OLE before GRA against the order). | Every gloss is a ROLE gloss: who-what-how-many with an unknown 'who'. 1,057 of 1,526 administrative word tokens (69 %) and every entry name are unread. |
| la60:LA-60.3a | Pseudo-outside test: reading induced on documents published in GORILA (376 administrative), frozen (INDUCED-GORILA hash in c3.json), used to decode the 11 administrative documents published after 1985 (KH 6, Thera 4, Zominthos 1) | INDUCED: FULL 2 of 11 (shuffled 2.2, EMPTY 3), STRICT 0 (0.16), no KU-RO, order agree 3 / against 2. PRIOR: same FULL 2, agree 3 / against 3. KH103 writes OLE > NI > CYP > VIN: three of six pairs against the frozen order (CYP should come first). | No outside gain (n = 11, upper bound only). The one new multi-commodity list goes half against the order. Would support the order: the next 3 new multi-commodity lists agreeing >= 80 %; would kill: <= 50 % on 5 new lists. |
| la60:LA-60.3b | Word -> commodity (scope commodity: same line, else the last one above), fitted on one half, scored on the other half; control = training commodities shuffled within site (100 per split); 10 splits | LA: 53 / 113 held-out uses right (0.47) vs site default 18 (0.16) vs shuffled 11.3; P <= 0.02 in 9/10 splits, 0.06 in one. Without the repeated HT86/95 group (DA-ME, DI-DE-RU, MI-NU-TE, KU-NI-SU, SA-RU): 40 / 98 (0.41). Linear B at LA size: 302 / 428 (0.71) vs 60 vs 68. | C: some entry words return with the same commodity on other tablets (carried by ~6 words: KU-PA3-NU, MA-DI, SA-RU, SA-RO, DA-RE, TA-I). This is not la54's unwritten-commodity task, which failed. Would support: >= 3 of P6's words with their commodity on new tablets; would kill: < 1 in 3 right on 10+ new uses. |
| la60:LA-60.3c | Gaps under the PRIOR reading, all 387 administrative documents (in sample) | FULL 104 / 387. Failures: cover 167 (a word outside an entry slot that the reading does not know: RA 7, PA 5, PA3 4, *318 3, TU 3, ME 3, A-SE 3, U-*34-SI 3, plus a long tail), substantive 150 (nothing checkable), order 35, totals 15 (HT 11a, 27a, 39, 94a, 100, 102, 109, 110a, 118, 119, 122a, 122b, 123+124b, 131b, PH 31a). Lexicon covers 469 of 1,526 word tokens; the rest are entry words, commonest KU-PA3-NU 8, DA-RE 7, MA-DI 6, DI-NA-U 6, SA-RU 6. | Biggest gaps, in order: (1) no word has a referent: entry names are 69 % of word tokens and none is read; (2) single signs between lines (RA, PA, PA3, TU, ME, *318) that are neither headings nor counted; (3) 15 non-closing totals (known causes, FINDINGS 2a: two commodity columns HT 27a, 94a, 100; slips of 1 or 10 HT 94a, 102, 119; damage); (4) fractions unvalued (la-1, la27). |
| la60:LA-60.3d | How far from 'cracked' (criteria from CLAUDE.md) | Calibrated controls: passed for the role layer only (2.7 % held-out documents over shuffles, the same as a true LB role layer). Outside-corpus prediction: frozen now (P1-P7); the only post-1985 test (n = 11) gives no gain. | Not cracked. The administrative skeleton is read (B); the language is not (no word meaning, no sound-to-meaning link). |
| la60:LA-60.final | Assemble every B (and best C) result into one machine-checkable reading; (1) a generative grammar of whole documents scored on held-out documents vs unigram/trigram/empty-reading grammar and vs the same grammar with roles shuffled; (2) freeze on training documents (sha256), decode the held-out half into glosses, count documents explained in full vs shuffled/random/empty readings; calibrate with Linear B KN+PY at Linear A size and a true partial LB reading of the same kind; (3) freeze outside-corpus predictions (sha256 538ad6fa...), pseudo-outside test on post-1985 documents, gap list | (1) The bare kind grammar (logogram / single sign / word / number) beats a trigram by 1,486 bits per held-out half. The reading beats its own shuffles by 159 bits (10/10 splits) but loses 98 bits to the empty grammar (9/10). Only the KU-RO arithmetic pays (36 bits/half). A true LB partial reading beats the empty grammar by 211 bits. (2) Frozen INDUCED: FULL 43 / 191 held-out documents (EMPTY 40, shuffled 41); STRICT 10.9 vs 5.8 shuffled (P~0.06), closing totals 5.2 vs 0.3, order agreement 0.70 vs 0.50. The gain is 2.7 % of documents; the true LB partial reading gains 2.9 %. (3) Post-1985 documents (n = 11): no gain; KH103 goes half against the order. Word -> commodity on held-out tablets 0.47 vs site default 0.16 (C). 69 % of administrative word tokens and every entry name stay unread. | NOT CRACKED. The role layer is real and about as good as a true Linear B role layer of the same size (B), but it is a parse of the ledger, not a reading of the language: no word has a meaning, the grammar adds nothing beyond surface kinds except arithmetic, and the outside test gives no gain. Distance to 'cracked': the skeleton is in place; everything that would make it a decipherment (referents for entry words, values for fractions, a sound-to-meaning link) is missing. |

| la59:LA-59.3 | Cycle verdict | Changing the ear changes nothing. In LA all four keys (two Miller-Nicely normalisations, Index Diachronica sound changes, identity-only 'flat') give in-sample gains 0.074-0.077 and shuffled diachronica keys 0.075 (z -0.03); held-out gains are all negative (-0.17 to -0.22; the diach held z -8.9 is one bad split, -0.30, against a null with SD ~0.005, not a signal). In LBs graded keys beat flat a little (0.187-0.193 vs 0.180; held -0.29 to -0.31 vs -0.35) but not shuffled keys (z -0.4 / +0.26); LB consonant co-assignment P 0.02 is one of 6 metrics. Ceiling: started from the true planted states the search keeps C labels at 0.49-0.56 (1x) and 0.71-0.88 (10x) and scores ABOVE the truth at 1x (1.15 vs 1.01: overfit), so at LA size the truth is not the optimum; from random starts it finds nothing (C 0.04-0.10) and at 10x it freezes (fit 0.72 vs true 1.19). | REJECTED (B, calibrated): at LA's alternation count the human-confusion kernel cannot identify sign features, and no key (heard or historical) explains LA or LB alternations better than its own shuffles. |

| la59:LA-59.4 | Cycle verdict | At 1x the fit beats the truth (overfit: the truth is not the optimum); from 3x up the truth scores higher than anything the search finds (fit 0.85-0.95 vs true 1.1-1.2 at share 1.0), and labels stay at chance (C 0.04-0.13, V 0.01-0.28) even at 30x. The free (C x V) labelling is too large a space for single-sign Gibbs moves; more data alone does not rescue the method. | Method REJECTED (B): the human-confusion decoder fails its own planted control at every data size tried; its LA and LB outputs carry no information. A future version would need block moves (whole consonant rows / vowel columns) or a two-stage fit (grid first, then rows to Miller-Nicely features). |
| la59:LA-59.final | HUMAN EARS AS THE ANSWER KEY (la59; tools/la59_common.py, la59_c1.py, la59_c1b.py, la59_c2.py, la59_c3.py, la59_c4.py, la59_diachronica.py, la59_report1.py, la59_report23.py; logs loops/la59_cycle1-4.txt). One-sign spelling alternations pooled over 1,000 random extraction settings (min length, position, grouping, frequency filter, 80 % document subsample) into a sign alternation graph (LA 76 signs; LB at LA size 68). Each sign gets a consonant (16 Miller-Nicely 1955 consonants + no-onset + other) and a vowel (5, formant key from Greek/Spanish/Hebrew five-vowel F1/F2, Bark); P(alternation a-b) = mixture of degree-only and degree x human confusability. Fits: Gibbs annealing, unconstrained and with the syllabary constraint (distinct states); 200,000 + 3 x 100,000 random assignments scored, top guesses re-scored on held-out sign pairs. Second keys: Miller-Nicely (Hubert normalisation), Index Diachronica 10.2 (1,433 one-to-one sound changes; correlates with Miller-Nicely rho 0.29 and the formant key rho 0.85), identity-only flat key. Nulls: shuffled keys (same values), double-edge-swap rewired graphs. Controls: LB with values hidden; planted kernel syllabaries at 1-30x LA's data. | LA: human key = shuffled keys (z -1.1 unconstrained, +2.0 constrained P 0.11, -0.03 for diachronica); real graph fits WORSE than rewired graphs (z -2.1, -4.6); held-out gains negative for every key (-0.16 to -0.22), random-guess survivors ~0 on held-out. LB control: no key beats its shuffles (z -0.55 to +2.0), LB values not recovered (vowel, place, manner, voice at chance; consonant co-assignment P 0.02-0.10 in 1 of 6 metrics per fit). Planted: labels at chance at every size (C 0.02-0.13, V 0.01-0.28); at 1x the fit beats the true states (non-identifiable), from 3x the search cannot reach them. LA outside-check place agreement 0.66 (P 0.004) in one fit is restart noise (0.31-0.69 over 12 fits). | REJECTED (B, calibrated negative). The decoder fails its planted and Linear B controls, so it yields no phonetic feature map. No Linear A feature assignment is graded above 'killed'. Side result (B): Linear A's one-sign alternations are not organised by human consonant or vowel confusability beyond what rewired graphs give (upper bound at this method's power, which is low). |
| la62:LA-62.final | CRACK ATTEMPT, OUTSIDE DATA: test the frozen la60 reading (sha256 538ad6fa...) and the la46/la51/la53/la54 items on Linear A outside the snapshot. Inventory (lineara.xyz live vs snapshot, SigLA 802 list, Crossref, open articles; positive control 18 known post-1985 items); blind transcription of 10 random known documents from drawings (control: random other document); kill/survive per prediction; decoding with PRIOR vs EMPTY, 200 shuffled and 100 random readings | lineara.xyz unchanged (identical bytes); positive control 18/18. Open new material = only the Knossos Anetaki ivory pair (KN Zg 58 accounting; KN Zg 57 B-D), prose and one illegible photo; RILA-S1 (107 items) closed. Blind transcription 35 % of numbers (control 15 %), 5-10 % of signs. Testable 3 of ~17 items: KN no KU-RO survives (trivial), KN commodity profile fails (1 of 6 classes vs 3 for 'other'), GRA before OLIV survives (+0.77 bits). PRIOR explains 46/65 tokens = EMPTY = shuffled = random. | Not cracked; the open outside evidence cannot separate the reading from an empty one. The Knossos profile (C) is demoted. Real test needs RILA-S1 and Anetaki II (human items in loops/la62_final.txt). |
| la62:LA-62.1 | Inventory of Linear A outside the snapshot: lineara.xyz live and GitHub files (byte compare), SigLA's 802-document list (name-normalised match), Crossref title search 2015-26, open articles (Ariadne Suppl. 5, PDIA V, Kadmos 2018 abstract, Del Freo 'Panorama'), CNR/ISPC notices, IULM IRIS, news. Positive control: the matcher must find 18 known post-1985 items in the snapshot; negative control: every unmatched SigLA name inspected by hand | Live lineara.xyz = snapshot (identical bytes). Positive control 18/18 found. SigLA: 783/802 matched by rule; the other 19 are naming variants (THE 7-12 = THEtab/THEfr, PH(?)31, PHWg45, PHWy42, HTWeWc3020 ...) except KN Wc <24>, <25> (GORILA-era roundels, single signs, not new). Genuinely new and open: only the Knossos ivory pair, KN Zg 58 (accounting, ~10-20 signs plus numbers) and KN Zg 57 B-D (~80 signs, no numbers), both known only from prose and one small photo. RILA-S1 (107 items) is closed. | The outside test set that is open on 6 Oct 2026 is about 2 documents, both from one cult deposit at Knossos and neither from Hagia Triada, where most frozen predictions apply. No open drawing or legible photo exists for either. Every other post-1985 text that we can find is already in the corpus (most of them were in la60's pseudo-outside test). Power will be very low; that is the main result of this cycle. |
| la62:LA-62.2a | Blind transcription of 10 known documents from facsimile drawings, scored against the corpus; control = same readings against random other documents (200 per document) | Numbers: 20 of 57 true number tokens recovered with the right value (35 %; control 7.2 = 15 %); 13 of 33 read numbers were wrong or spurious. Signs: 20 signs named, 18 right (HT88 11/11, suspect; others 7/9), but only 18 of 182 true signs were named at all (10 %; 7/152 = 5 % without HT88); control 3.5. Token-kind counts: total absolute error 58 vs control 92. Fraction signs: none recovered. | Our image transcription is NOT usable for sign-level work. It gives the layout and about a third of the numbers (above chance), and almost no syllabic readings (5 % of signs without HT88). Any transcription of a new document from images alone would be grade C at best, and the sign-group (word) layer, which the frozen predictions P6 and P7 need, would be missing. |
| la62:LA-62.2b | Transcription of the new documents | Skeletons only, from prose: logogram classes, number presence, group counts; 0 word identities, 0 number values beyond 'ten', 0 fraction identities. | Grade C skeleton. It can test predictions about commodity CLASSES, commodity ORDER and the presence or absence of KU-RO, but none about words, totals or fractions. |
| la62:LA-62.3 | Score every frozen la60 prediction (hash re-checked) and the earlier frozen items (la46, la51, la53, la54) on the outside documents (cycle-2 skeletons of KN Zg 58 and KN Zg 57 A-D); decode them with the frozen PRIOR. Controls: EMPTY reading, 200 role-shuffled, 100 random readings (explained tokens, FULL); for the site profile, the other five frozen site profiles | Testable: 3 of ~17 items. KN no-KU-RO survives (trivial); KN commodity profile fails (1 of 6 classes; 'other' 3, KH 2, ZA 2); GRA before OLIV survives (1 pair, +0.77 bits). Explained tokens: PRIOR 46/65, EMPTY 46/65, shuffled 46.0, random 46.0. Every explained token is a logogram; the reading's lexicon (KU-RO, KI-RO, NI, heading signs, transaction words) does not fire once. FULL: 0/5 for all readings (unknown groups; no numbers on the ring). Under the rules as frozen, all five are out of scope (ivory = non-administrative). | No outside gain, and no outside kill of anything central. The frozen reading explains exactly what the empty reading explains (0 extra tokens). The one site-specific profile tested (Knossos, built on 11 documents) fails against controls. The one order pair agrees, worth less than one bit. The decisive predictions (HT totals, KH/ZA profiles, word -> commodity, the SA-RA2 basket) have no open outside documents to be tested on. |
| la57:LA-57.1a | EVERY BUREAUCRACY ON EARTH VOTES, corpora (tools/la57_common.py, la57_build.py). Seven known record-keeping systems were reduced to one value-free form: opaque token ids, numbers (value, fraction flag), line or cord-group breaks. The systems are PC proto-cuneiform (Uruk IV-III, 3,827 docs), UR3 Ur III admin (5,000), OB Old Babylonian admin (4,081), EB Ebla admin (1,283), OA Old Assyrian admin (290; all four from the CDLI dump), LB Knossos+Pylos (DAMOS, 3,838) and KH Inca khipus (Open Khipu Repository: 605 khipus, cords = colour token + knot value, cord groups = lines). Each known system gets 4 draws at Linear A size (~5,200 tokens). 42 features per word occurrence: 24 local (position, line edge, adjacency to a number, magnitude, fraction, number = sum of others / max / rank, document and line size) and 18 type-level aggregates, rank-normalised within each draw. Role truth (controls only) comes from conventional meanings: COM, TOT, PER, PLA, TRA, HDR (office/header/date), UNI. For KH, TOT = top cords (a physical attachment class, not arithmetic; 98 cords on 12 khipus). Egyptian hieratic: no open structured transcription of accounts was found, so it was not used. Neo-Babylonian: only 18 transliterated admin texts in the dump, so it was dropped. | Eligible roles (>= 30 pos/neg per system): COM 6 systems (PC UR3 OB EB OA LB), TOT 5 (UR3 OB OA LB KH), PER 5, PLA 5 (UR3 OB EB OA LB), TRA 5 (PC UR3 OB EB LB), HDR 5 and UNI 5, both cuneiform-only (no outside system carries them in this reduction). Planted administration (la45 generator) is test-only. | Setup. UNI and HDR cannot test cross-civilisation transfer (all their systems are cuneiform). |
| la57:LA-57.1b | Massive random guessing (tools/la57_c1.py): 20,000 random sparse linear scorers per role (1-5 features, Gaussian weights, no training) = 140,000 hypotheses, scored by occurrence AUC on every system. Leave-one-system-out (LOSO): the 200 scorers with the best minimum AUC on the other systems form an ensemble, which is scored on the held-out system. Leave-one-civilisation-out (LOCO): cuneiform / Aegean / Andes. NULL: role labels permuted within each training corpus (type level; khipu occurrence level), 30 replicates of the whole selection, scored on the TRUE held-out labels (P floor 0.032). | LOSO ensemble AUC (null median, P): TOT UR3 0.98 (0.34, .03), OB 0.91 (.03), OA 0.96 (.03), LB 0.22 (0.42, P .81), KH 0.20 (0.54, P 1.0). COM UR3 0.71 (.03), OB 0.71 (.10), LB 0.81 (0.35, .10), PC 0.49, EB 0.40, OA 0.25 (fail). PER UR3 0.93, OB 0.90, OA 0.96 but the null is as high (P .16-.26); EB 0.54, LB 0.42 (fail). PLA UR3 0.93 (.03), LB 0.84 (0.51, .065), OB/EB/OA P .13-.32. TRA LB 0.84 (0.63, .10), UR3 0.90 (.065), OB 0.84 (.065), PC 0.61 (.10), EB 0.66 (.42). HDR 5/5 cuneiform systems P .03 (AUC 0.77-1.00). UNI 5/5 P .03 (0.82-0.96). LOCO (train on the other civilisations only): no role beats its null except TRA LB->EB (0.80, P .03); TOT trained on LB+KH fails on all cuneiform (0.22-0.47). | Totals, units and headers transfer only INSIDE the cuneiform family (B). No role transfers to every held-out civilisation at P < 0.05. Closest: PLA and TRA cuneiform->LB (P .065-.10, C). TOT is anti-transferable: cuneiform-learned 'total' scorers rank LB to-so and khipu top cords BELOW random (AUC 0.2). Each civilisation marks totals in its own way (A, method-level negative). |
| la57:LA-57.1c | Universal scorers = AUC >= 0.6 on EVERY known system carrying the role (selected on all systems), compared with the permuted-label count; then run on PLANT (never seen), Linear A, and 20 S1 (types shuffled over slots) + 20 S2 (order shuffled in documents) copies (tools/la57_c1_read.py, la57_read.py). Outside check: Linear A logograms as COM (their commodity status comes from picture readings, used only to score). | Universal count (null median, max): COM 19 (0, 5), TOT 169 (0, 0), PER 1,296 (0, 61), PLA 2,210 (0, 5), TRA 538 (12, 323), HDR 1,207 (48, 923), UNI 2,981 (133, 2,631). PLANT AUC: COM 0.90-0.93, TRA 0.97-0.98, PLA 0.84-0.85, TOT 0.69-0.71, PER 0.67-0.69. LA: logograms-as-COM AUC 0.723 vs S1 median 0.543 (q95 0.563). LA type-score spread beats S1 and S2 for COM, TOT, PER, PLA, TRA (P 0.05, the floor), but the share of types with majority agreement is never above the shuffles (P 0.14-1.0). Top LA COM: L:HIDE+B, *131B, L:GRA+K+L, PA-DE (rank 4/188), *118, *22M. TOT: TE-RI, QA-QA-RU, DA-RI-DA ...; KU-RO only 23/188, KI-RO 99/188. PER: A-DU 6/188. TRA: SI-RU-TE, JA-SA-SA-RA-ME, I-PI-NA-MA. | Universal scorers exist (well above null for TOT, PER, PLA), but LOSO shows they cannot be FOUND without seeing the held-out system, so they do not transfer. Only COM passes an outside check on Linear A (logograms recovered, B). The TOT readout is killed by its own anchor (KU-RO, the arithmetically proven total, is not singled out). Word-level LA role assignments are C at best: no type is assigned more often than in shuffles. |
| la57:LA-57.2a | Thousands of random TRAINED taggers (tools/la57_c2.py; run per role after a restart, data/la57_ckpt/c2_<role>/). Per role, 300 random specs: 2-15 of 42 features x {logistic regression with random C, tree with depth 1-4 and leaf 20-200, Gaussian NB}. Each spec is fitted on all systems but the held-out one (every system weighted 1, classes balanced) and scored by held-out occurrence AUC. LOSO over every system carrying the role, plus LOCO (cuneiform / Aegean / Andes). SURVIVOR = held-out AUC >= 0.6 on EVERY held-out system. NULL: role labels permuted within each training corpus, same 300 specs, 5 replicates. 6 x 300 x 7 = 12,600 spec-fits of ~8-12 folds each. | Survivors / 300 (null replicates); civ-out survivors (null): TOT 0 (8, 3, 2, 1, 0); 0 (21, 21, 9, 1, 0). Specs trained without LB give LB to-so median AUC 0.32 (null 0.53), without KH give top cords 0.48 (null 0.54). COM 8 (0 x5); 14 (0 x5), with LB held out median 0.60 (null 0.36) and OA failing (0.42). PLA 168 (24, 0, 27, 115, 0); 137 (42, 3, 35, 92, 4), LB 0.66 vs null 0.54. TRA 72 (2, 33, 0, 44, 3); 34 (1, 5, 6, 13, 4), LB 0.70 vs 0.55, PC weak (0.57). PER 72 (20, 0, 8, 9, 53); 17 (2, 3, 4, 8, 67): one null beats the real run. HDR 142 (0, 4, 0, 0, 0) and UNI 176 (0 x5) are cuneiform-only, so no civ-out test is possible. PLANT (never trained on): COM 0.90-0.96, TRA 0.93-0.96, PLA 0.82-0.84, PER 0.69-0.72. | TOT does NOT transfer between civilisations. Trained models do worse than label-permuted ones on the outside systems (A, calibrated negative: 'total' has no universal layout). COM transfers across all 6 written systems with no null survivor (B). TRA and PLA transfer above the null, but the null is strong (C+/B-). PER is not robust (one null replicate exceeds it): C. |
| la57:LA-57.2b | Readout of the refitted survivors (tools/la57_c2b_read.py) on Linear A. (i) Whole corpus. (ii) Administrative documents only (LA_ADM: 371 documents with at least one number, 3,693 tokens), because every known system is administrative. Each is set against 20 S1 (type shuffle) and 20 S2 (order shuffle) copies. Outside check: logograms as COM. Anchor ranks: KU-RO (arithmetic total, la1/la46), SA-RA2, A-DU, logograms. | Whole corpus: logograms-as-COM AUC 0.774 (S1 median 0.541, q95 0.609). PLA/TRA/HDR tops are libation-formula words (SI-RU-TE, U-NA-KA-NA-SI, JA-SA-SA-RA-ME, I-PI-NA-MA, A-TA-I-*301-WA-JA), an artefact of number-free documents, so the admin-only run was used. LA_ADM: logograms-as-COM 0.633 (S1 0.476, q95 0.550). PER majority-agreement share 0.060 vs S1 0.020 / S2 0.034 (P 0.05 both, the floor); PLA 0.081 vs 0.050 / 0.060 (P 0.14 / 0.05); TRA, COM and HDR not above the shuffles. Top PER = PLA = TRA sets overlap: A-DU (PER 8, PLA 10, TRA 6 of 149), SA-RA2 (19, 20, 8), *516, *318, *326, L:*307+*387, A-SE, A-KA-RU. Top COM words: DI-DE-RU, DA-SI-*118, *28B-NU-MA-RE, U-*325-ZA, TE-TU (with L:GRA+K+L, L:CAP, L:HIDE+B). KU-RO: COM 35, PER 142, TRA 125, HDR 51; UNI 3/149 (cuneiform-only model, 'word welded to a number'). | Linear A splits along two axes only: 'counted thing' (COM, the one role that transfers to every written system and finds LA logograms, B) and one undifferentiated 'named/heading entity' axis. On that axis the person, place and transaction models pick the SAME words (A-DU, SA-RA2, *516), so outside bureaucracies cannot tell LA persons from places from transactions (B, negative). C: DI-DE-RU, DA-SI-*118, *28B-NU-MA-RE, U-*325-ZA and TE-TU are words used as commodities (agreement 0.5-0.88). Would support: a new tablet with one of them directly before a number in a logogram slot. Would kill: one of them heading a list of logograms. |
| la57:LA-57.3a | A PARLIAMENT OF BUREAUCRACIES (tools/la57_c3.py, data/la57_ckpt/c3/). No pooling: each known system trains its OWN 60 random taggers (feature subset x LR / tree / NB) and votes on every other system. NULL: the voter's labels permuted (type level; khipu occurrence level), same specs, 5 replicates; * = above every null. 216 voter-trainings. | Kinship (voter -> target AUC). TOT: cuneiform <-> cuneiform 0.91-1.00*; LB votes cuneiform 0.11-0.28 and KH 0.23, and cuneiform votes LB 0.18-0.41; KH top-cord voters recognise UR3 and OB totals 0.77-0.79* but cuneiform voters do not see khipu top cords (0.50-0.54). COM: LB <-> EB 0.75-0.76*, UR3 -> LB 0.88*, OA is the outlier (0.23-0.51 from most). PLA: every voter > 0.74 on every target (UR3/OB/EB -> LB 0.77-0.80*). TRA: UR3 -> LB 0.83*, LB -> cuneiform 0.55-0.82*. Parliament (other systems' mean vote) on the held-out system vs null max: COM LB 0.857 (0.841), EB 0.751 (0.581), OA 0.316 (fail); TOT LB 0.173, KH 0.283 (both below the null median); PLA LB 0.795 (0.707), OA 0.946 (0.843); TRA PC 0.594 (0.582), OB 0.878 (0.798), EB 0.828 (0.742), LB 0.795 (0.847, fail); PER LB 0.644 (0.709, fail), EB 0.633 (fail). PLANT: COM 0.95-0.98, TOT 0.99, TRA 0.92-0.95, PLA 0.73-0.75, PER 0.69-0.72. | Record-keeping kinship is ROLE-SPECIFIC. Commodities and places look alike in every written bureaucracy (B). Totals form two incompatible conventions, cuneiform and Aegean, and the khipu top cord resembles the cuneiform convention (one direction only, C). Linear B and the khipus are each other's and everyone's opposite on totals (A, calibrated). |
| la57:LA-57.3b | The parliament on Linear A administrative documents (LA_ADM, 371 documents) against 10 S1 + 10 S2 shuffles. Statistics: (i) voter agreement (mean pairwise Spearman between systems' votes; on known systems agreement tracks accuracy for COM, PLA, HDR, UNI); (ii) number of LA types with a majority of systems voting them into the top decile; (iii) the verdict list. | Voter agreement LA vs S1 max / S2 max: COM 0.218 vs 0.185 / 0.204 (above both); UNI 0.823 vs 0.805 / 0.789 (above both); PLA 0.686 vs 0.667 / 0.683 (marginal); TOT 0.108 vs 0.105 / 0.014 (marginal over S1); PER 0.427 (below both, 0.494 / 0.584); TRA 0.259 (S1-level, below S2); HDR 0.546 (below both). Majority-vote type counts are never above the shuffles (COM 3 vs S1 median 4; PER 14 vs 11-15; PLA 13 vs 12-15; TRA 10 vs 10-13). Verdicts: COM *904 5/6, L:QI+SI 4/6, L:OLE+U 4/6, *316, L:GRA+K+L, L:GRA 3/6; UNI L:OLE+U 5/5, L:CAP, KU-RO 4/5, *23M, *28B-NU-MA-RE, U-*325-ZA, *904; TOT SA-RU, DI-DE-RU, DA-SI-*118, PA-JA-RE, QA-*310-I 3/5, KU-RO 2/5; HDR KI, SA-RA2 4/5; PER/PLA/TRA all *516, *326, *318, *560, DA-QE-RA, U, ZA, L:SI+SE. | Linear A gets a commodity layer the world's bureaucracies agree on more than on shuffled Linear A (B, with 2.2 and 2.3 already). The world also agrees, above shuffles, on a 'word welded to a quantity' (UNI-type) layer: oil-ligature logograms, *23M, *904, KU-RO (C). The world does NOT agree on who or where. Its person, place and transaction votes all land on the same few signs (*516, *318, *326, *560, U, ZA, DA-QE-RA) and are no more consistent than for shuffled text (B, negative). No Linear A total is voted in: KU-RO only 2/5. |
| la57:LA-57.final | EVERY BUREAUCRACY ON EARTH VOTES (tools/la57_common.py, la57_build.py, la57_build_adm.py, la57_c1.py, la57_c1_read.py, la57_c2.py, la57_c2b_read.py, la57_c3.py, la57_read.py; checkpoints data/la57_ckpt/). Seven unrelated or distant record-keeping systems were reduced to one value-free form: opaque tokens, numbers, line or cord-group breaks; 42 position, number-adjacency, arithmetic and type-level features. The systems are proto-cuneiform, Ur III, Old Babylonian, Ebla and Old Assyrian (CDLI); Linear B (DAMOS); and Inca khipus (Open Khipu Repository; cord colour = token, knot value = number, TOT = top cords). Egyptian hieratic: no open structured transcription found. Three loops: (1) 140,000 random untrained scorers; (2) 2,100 random trained taggers (LR / tree / NB); (3) a 'parliament' where each system trains its own taggers and votes. Each is run leave-one-system-out and leave-one-civilisation-out, against label-permuted nulls inside each training corpus (30 / 5 / 5 replicates), with a planted administration (test only), on Linear A (whole and admin-only), and against 20 type-shuffled + 20 order-shuffled Linear A copies. | Transfers between civilisations: COMMODITY (B). 8/300 trained survivors on all 6 written systems vs 0 in every null; parliament on LB 0.857 > null max 0.841; Linear A logograms recovered as commodities: AUC 0.774 whole / 0.633 admin, vs shuffle q95 0.61 / 0.55. TRANSACTION and PLACE transfer above the null but the null is strong (C+). PERSON is not robust. UNIT and HEADER transfer within cuneiform only (no outside system carries them). TOTAL is anti-universal: 0/300 survivors (nulls up to 8); cuneiform-trained totals score LB to-so 0.17-0.32 and khipu top cords 0.28-0.48, below the label-permuted null; khipu voters do recognise cuneiform totals (0.77-0.79, one direction). Linear A: the world agrees above shuffles on a commodity layer and a 'word welded to a quantity' layer, but not on person / place / transaction. Those votes collapse onto the same signs (*516, *318, *326, *560, U, ZA, DA-QE-RA; A-DU and SA-RA2 in the pooled models). KU-RO is not voted a total (2/5). | NOT CRACKED. A (calibrated negative): there is no universal layout of a total. Cuneiform, Linear B and the khipus mark totals in mutually opposite ways, so outside bureaucracies cannot find Linear A totals; KU-RO stays known only from arithmetic. B: commodity is the one role every written bureaucracy shares, and it recovers Linear A's logograms. B (negative): outside administrations cannot separate Linear A persons, places and transactions; LA has one 'named/heading entity' axis. C: DI-DE-RU, DA-SI-*118, *28B-NU-MA-RE, U-*325-ZA and TE-TU are syllabic words used as commodities (would kill: one heading a logogram list). C: khipu top cords follow the cuneiform total convention, not the Linear B one (would kill: the same test on more top-cord khipus giving AUC < 0.6). |

| la58:LA-58.1 | SIMULATE MINOAN GOVERNMENT AND LET THE PAPERWORK CHOOSE. C agent-based simulator (tools/la58_sim.c): random worlds of 6 kinds (single palace, capital + satellite offices, peer centres, temple centre, merchant houses, no institutions) x 30 nuisance parameters; paperwork (tablets, roundels, nodules, objects; opaque words, numbers, totals) thinned to each real site's surviving count; 224-statistic panel; ABC-RF on 100k LA, 60k LB, 60k Ur III worlds. Controls: 4,000 held-out worlds, 6 planted worlds x 6, LB (separate kingdoms), Ur III (one state). Null: 20 site shuffles. | Type accuracy 0.59 (no-institution worlds 99 %); centres found faintly (planted KN 0.23 vs 0.12); parent links at chance. Real LA: no institutions 0.44 (shuffles 0.017). Centre location fails in LB and Ur III (follows size). | Political map not readable (B, calibrated negative). |

Controls Ur III and LB titles; frequency-matched nulls; shuffles. | LA no-institution 0.75 (shuffles 0.23-0.45; LB 0.62; Ur III S 0.42, N 0.05). Titles: Ur III AUC 0.77 P 0.155, LB 0.53; LA list = sealing-device signs, survives shuffling. | B: LA sites behave as separate administrations. Titles killed. |

Deposits: N 0.28 (shuffles 0.018). Within-HT overlap 0.099 vs null 0.040 (P < 0.001); cross-site 0.027-0.029 vs 0.031-0.037; planted capital+offices 0.046 difference vs real 0.070. | B: HT rooms are one vocabulary block, sites share less than random. C: separate site administrations. |

Linear A paperwork splits by site: HT rooms cohere (2.5x shuffle), sites do not (below shuffle, more separate than LB kingdoms on 2 of 3 measures). | B (negative map; descriptive site islands), C (political reading). |

N (no institutions) recovered 654/658 (LA); S 468/643; T, I and M confuse with each other. Centre AUC 0.76-0.81 (LA), but calibrated P(centre) never exceeds ~0.5 (bin 0.3-0.5 -> 53 % true). Parent link accuracy 0.04-0.14 (chance about 0.08). | A (method): paperwork at Linear A size separates 'institutions vs none' and 'shared offices' well, the kind of hierarchy poorly, and who-depends-on-whom not at all. |

Centre: KN 0.23 vs others 0.11-0.14 (single at KN); HT 0.17 vs 0.12-0.14 (single at HT); peers HT 0.21, others 0.15-0.19. | B (method): planted centres are only faintly recovered (1.5-2x background); a planted capital-with-offices or empty world is recovered strongly. |

Shuffled: N 0.017 (max of 20 far below 0.44), S 0.35. Centre P flat 0.08-0.13 (HT 0.09, KH 0.10, KN 0.10, ZA 0.10, Syme 0.13, Iouktas 0.12); shuffled 0.10-0.19. Centre probabilities flat in all three (LA 0.01-0.06; LB 0.09-0.11; Ur III 0.21-0.26). | B: both controls give the right type class (one state with shared offices for Ur III; no shared institution for kingdoms centuries and hundreds of km apart). LA reads as 'no shared institution', more strongly than LB and than any shuffle. Centres are not located in any corpus (agrees with 1a-1e). |

| la61:LA-61.2b | ARITHMETIC PUZZLE: 27 KU-RO sections with >= 2 entries (12 close as written). Global hypothesis = COUNT / EXCL / DED for each of 127 label words, signs and logograms + scope (reset at each total / running from document start); score = closing sections - 0.5 per non-COUNT role (MDL); 20000 random hypotheses + 40 hill-climbs x 3000 steps (1,137,000 scored in all). Controls: 20 DECOY copies (every total = entry sum +- a real discrepancy, nothing closes as written) through the same search; leave-one-section-out | best: 15 close (+3) with 3 roles, scope reset: I-KA DED, PI-SA DED, RI-RU-MA DED; newly closed ['HT102', 'HT118', 'HT123+124b']; broken []. DECOY: the same search closes +3.3 (range 0-9) with 3.2 roles. Leave-one-out: 12 / 27 sections close with roles fitted without them; 0 of the 15 failing sections closed out of sample | real gain 3 vs decoy 3.3: no better than decoys: the flexible arithmetic closes invented totals as easily; the 15 failures are not explained (damage / other-side totals remain the simplest account) |

It does improve held-out decoding in LB (+353/+865 bits) and Ur III (+137/+1,689), but through word-specific slot and commodity habits, not through right type names. Linear A: typing makes held-out decoding WORSE (-271 bits; v2 -313), like the word-shuffled controls (-244), and worse than its own permuted types (-195); 0 of 155 words stable across restarts, halves agree 3/35 (chance 4.1). Glossary types as la60 grammar roles cost 1,277 bits. Puzzles: the arithmetic search closes 3 of the 15 failing totals, but closes invented decoy totals as easily (+3.3), and 0 of 15 out of sample; single-sign roles gain 31 bits in selection and lose 27 on evaluation (decoys -14 +- 35). Post-1985 test: only 2 uses of glossary words (no test). | KILLED. No glossary: no Linear A word gets a type above 'failed'. The words cannot be read this way at this corpus size: about 2 uses per word in each fold, and a method that cannot name known Linear B or Ur III words at the same size. Not cracked; this step moves no closer. |
| la64:LA-64.final | MINOAN NUMBERS MUST OBEY PHYSIOLOGY (pe55 ported; tools/la64_lib.py, la64_c1.py, la64_c2.py, la64_c3.py, la64_c3b.py; loops/la64_cycle1-3.txt, la64_final.txt). Thousands of random hypotheses (unit size per commodity key incl. la57 words, nuisance fraction values, count-key labels) scored by how many quantities fall in physical windows at once (rations from FAO/WHO energy needs, Cato, Foxhall; household output; Neopalatial pithos and jar capacities), fitted on halves and tested held out. Nulls: numbers shuffled between documents, random units, 30-60 nonsense rulers. Controls: Linear B (units and subunits hidden), Ur III (sila hidden), planted archives (entry-level and per-head) | Entry size alone is off by x10-x50 in LB and Ur III and recovers planted units within x2 in only 35%. The per-head instrument recovers LB GRA at 100-110 l (truth 96, 10/10 at LA size), but nonsense rulers fit as well. On LA's 66 person/commodity pairs no key beats shuffles, and a planted perfect-ration archive on the same pairs is not recovered. With true units, entry size does not mark ration entries (LB AUC 0.57, Ur III 0.47). LB subunit T is recovered at 0.073, but nonsense rulers recover it too in 47% (consistency, not physics). Day reading of HT 27a/89/100: VIN 7-24 l per unit, so ZA Zb 3 (VIN 32) holds 232-766 l (measured ~400 l, Notti 2025), but the chance agreement is P 0.34 | A (calibrated negative): physics fixes no Linear A unit, and no ration word or fraction value can be read. C-: jar-sized wine unit; would kill: THE Zb 13 (VIN+TE 120) on a vessel under ~500 l, or KN Zb 35 (OLE 100) on one under ~20 l |

Words before fractions: L:CYP, L:CYP+D (NUM-L z 4.2), *308 (z 4-6), *131C (z 3.8), L:OLE+RI (3.4): the fraction-takers carry fraction information; KI-RO, SA-RA2, A-DU, KA-PA, KU-PA SILENT | la57 candidates not confirmed (untestable singly); between-line signs are not quantity markers |

| la63:LA-63.3b | Linear B role groups (700 docs), 8 models | QUAL (commodity-bound qualifiers and measured nouns) LOGO z 6.2 p 0.001 classed; BEG:KO (ko-wo, ko-wa, ko-to-na) LOGO z 8.5; END:WO / HAS:WO HEAD (+LOGO 4.6-5.3); places, transaction words, totals SILENT; persons ENT z 4.9 p 0.004 (just below); classed groups 6/74 vs matched random sets 7/296 (0.024) | PASS: in Linear B the game, run on word classes, recovers the commodity-bound class and leaves places, totals and transactions silent |
| la63:LA-63.3c | Linear A groups, 40 models | 0/47 groups classed; matched random word-sets 8/188 (0.043) classed, i.e. Linear A's word classes are LESS informative than random word-sets of the same size. LA57: SILENT, LOGO z -1.7 (erasing them hurts commodity prediction LESS than erasing random tokens of their documents), NUM-L z 0.9. BETWEEN: SILENT, NUM-L z -1.9 (anti-number, as RA alone in cycle 2). FRACW: SILENT (NUM-L z -0.8). Best sign families: HAS:SU TOT z 3.6 p 0.007, LOGO 3.3; HAS:NI TOT 2.6; all below threshold | No Linear A syllabic class carries number, fraction or commodity information beyond random words. The la57 candidates do not behave like commodity words or logograms (CYP: own-entry NUM z 33) |
| la65:LA-65.1a | Template search, LA with metadata. A random template = 1-4 features drawn (rarity-weighted) from one random tablet: a word, logogram base, entry, exact entry+number, logogram+fraction, header entry, closing entry, entry-type skeleton, entry count, logogram set, site, scribe, findspot, block of 6 consecutive numbers (2 offsets). Group = every tablet with all features (2-40). Score = HELD-OUT cohesion (mean pairwise tablet-calibrated z of IDF-weighted content similarity after removing what the template defines). 2,000,000 templates per run. NULL: 5 corpora with every entry moved to a random slot of the same site, kind (number/context) and position class (metadata kept). Family-wise threshold = max of all nulls per size class (2 / 3 / 4-5 / 6+) | 2,154 distinct groups; thresholds 15.0 / 7.4 / 7.7 / 4.0; 1 significant group: ZA4, ZA5, ZA15 (score 8.2, template VIN logogram set + Zakros). Each null scored against the other four: 2, 0, 1, 0, 1. Top sub-threshold pairs: HT16~HT20 (13.8, same scribe), HT86~HT95 (12.4), MA4~MA6, HT17~HT19 | Strict level: Linear A has one template series, at the null false-positive rate (0-2). |
| la65:LA-65.1b | Same search with content features only (LAN; no scribe, findspot or numbering, as for Linear B), 5 nulls; also an FDR level (smallest score per size class with mean null count / real count <= 0.2) | Family-wise: 1 group (ZA4/5/15 again). FDR 0.2: thresholds 9.75 / 3.5 / 2.25 / 1.5, 107 groups, merged into 26 dossiers covering 109 tablets, e.g. HT86+HT95+HT120 (GRA, SA-RU, DI-DE-RU), HT16+HT20, HT17+HT19(+KH100), HT38+THE tab.4 (CAP), ZA4+ZA5+ZA15(+PH9) (VIN), HT23/30/32/33/34/35/60 (MI, VIN, QA2, OLE small fractions), MA4+MA6+PH12+PH13 (HIDE), a 17-tablet OLE/OLIV/GRA/*304 group, HT7/10/13/85/122 (DA-RE, PA name lists), HT27/89/94/100/110/130 (CYP + KU-RO; this recovers blind the group that archive studies call personnel-plus-commodities lists) | FDR dossiers are mostly topical groups (same commodities, same site), not copies. |
| la65:LA-65.1c | CONTROL Linear B: 3 random draws of 246 KN+PY tablets (sign identities, content features only; series code used only to score), 3 nulls each, same search | Family-wise: 14 / 10 / 22 significant groups, 7 / 5 / 9 dossiers covering 32 / 20 / 31 tablets; series purity 0.87 / 0.90 / 0.90 vs random groups 0.36 / 0.31 / 0.39 (KN Sc, PY Eb, PY Ma, PY Es, PY Sh, KN Gg, KN Fs, PY Jn). FDR: 20 / 17 / 13 dossiers, purity 0.70-0.81 vs 0.29-0.37 | PASS: the search finds real Linear B series at Linear A size (grade A). |
| la65:LA-65.1d | CONTROL planted dossiers: 5 seeds; a real LA tablet copied 6 times: slot A word swapped alone (number fixed), slot B word and logogram swapped together (3 fixed pairs) with a new quantity scale, slot C word fixed with a regular x(j+1) quantity step. LA family-wise thresholds | 30 of 30 planted tablets recovered (6/6 in every seed); the only extra member is always the source tablet itself (a true near-copy) | PASS (grade A). |
| la65:LA-65.1 | Cycle verdict | At the strict level Linear A has 1 template series (ZA4/ZA5/ZA15, wine) where the same search finds 5-9 series in each Linear B draw of the same size (purity ~0.9). At FDR 0.2 Linear A has 26 dossiers, mostly topical groups. | B (calibrated): Linear A tablets were rarely written from one template, or such series are lost (5-9x fewer dossiers and 10-22x fewer significant groups than LB at equal size; sampling caveat: the corpus is a small fraction of what was written). |
| la65:LA-65.2a | CONTROL planted (5 seeds): type the recovered planted dossiers | slot A -> ID 5/5, slot B -> COM 5/5, slot C -> STEP 5/5; all other slots FIX (59), no false roles | PASS (grade A). |
| la65:LA-65.2b | Word-logogram coupling: word swaps that co-occur with a logogram-base swap vs logogram-change flags permuted within dossier (2,000). Fraction test: fraction letters change with vs without a logogram change (permutation). LB draws (FW + FDR dossiers) and LA (LANF dossiers) | LB: coupling 2 vs 0.7 (P 0.10), 12 vs 10.6 (P 0.31), 0 vs 0; LB commodity-bound qualifiers (key QUAL) in COM slots 1/106, 0/73, 0/64 (they stay fixed inside a series or swap with a logogram subtype). Fraction test LB P 0.35 / 0.59 / 0.47. LA: coupling 35 vs 34.8 (P 0.52); fractions change 102/107 with and 259/264 without a logogram change (P 0.96) | CONTROL FAILS for COM and FRAC: at this size Linear B series contain no commodity-bound swaps to recover, so 'no COM in Linear A' is uninformative (A, method limit). |
| la65:LA-65.2c | Dossier alignments vs matched random groups (same sizes, same site mix, 60 draws): share of fixed slots (FIX/NUM/GEN/STEP), STEP slots, whole-list rescales (two members with >= 3 aligned numbers in one ratio != 1) | LB: fixed share 0.36 / 0.48 / 0.64 vs 0.29 / 0.28 / 0.27 (P 0.16 / 0.03 / 0.016); rescales 4 vs 0.5 (P 0.016, PY Ma/Es proportions) / 0 / 0; STEP 0. LA: fixed share 0.39 vs 0.30 (P 0.033); rescales 3 vs 2.0 (P 0.30); STEP 0; RATE 0 | LA dossiers keep template words like LB series (B); no rescale, step or rate beyond random groups (only the known HT86->HT95 halving). |
| la65:LA-65.2d | Word typing: TPL = word kept constant in an aligned slot, VAR = word only in swapped slots. Enrichment of known function words among TPL (LB key TOT/TRA/QUAL; LA la60 frozen roles, scored only) vs random-group typing | LB: +0.015 (P 0.66), +0.32 (P 0.066), +0.008 (P 0.69). LA: TPL 18/40 la60-typed vs VAR 18/257, +0.38 vs +0.18 (P 0.033) | Calibration passes in 0/3 LB draws at P<0.05 (1/3 at 0.07): the LA enrichment cannot validate anything (C). |
| la65:LA-65.2e | What varies inside the LA dossiers (read off the alignments) | ZA4/ZA5/ZA15: *28B-NU-MA-RE and SI-PI-KI fixed with free numbers (13/20/6; 5/1/2), names in the other slots swap alone; both words occur on no other tablet (0 of 243; < 3/243). HT86/HT95: DI-DE-RU, DA-ME, MI-NU-TE, KU-NI-SU fixed and halved (20 -> 10), one SA-RU line stays 20; QA-RA2-WA/QE-RA2-U swap (10/7). HT16/HT20: header and one name swap alone. HT7/10/13/85/122 and HT7/87/117: long name lists, almost every slot ID with number 1. MA4/MA6/PH12/PH13: HIDE fixed, numbers free. No word swaps with its logogram in any LA dossier beyond chance | B: in LA dossiers names swap alone and numbers float; C: *28B-NU-MA-RE and SI-PI-KI are series-only line words of one Zakros wine template. |
| la65:LA-65.2 | Cycle verdict | Minimal-pair typing recovers planted roles exactly, but Linear B series at this size have no commodity-bound, step or rate slots for it to find (only fixed template words and proportional series). Linear A shows the LB-like fixed-word excess, nothing else. | NOT CRACKED. A (method limit): COM/FRAC typing has no power at LA size. B: LA dossier slots are identifiers and free counts. |
| la65:LA-65.3a | Reuse: outside every dossier, TPL words should return on more tablets than VAR words.
