LA: +2.57 (TPL 3.1, VAR 0.53) vs 2.66 (P 0.52) | No transfer in LB or LA: the TPL/VAR split is a frequency effect (grade A as a method limit). |
| la65:LA-65.3b | Entry shape: the dossier slot's shape (with logogram? with number?) predicts the word's outside occurrences; permuted shapes and random-group typing | LA: 21 words, agreement 0.48 vs 0.42 permuted (P 0.15), not above random groups (P 0.77). LB: 6 words 0.48 vs 0.34 (P 0.14); 5 words 0.24 vs 0.24; too few in LB0 | KILLED at this size in both scripts. |
| la65:LA-65.3c | la60 frozen reading: a la60-typed word found only in swapped dossier slots counts as a contradiction; share vs random-group typing | LA: 18 agree (NI, SA-RA2, *304, *308, E, KU-RO, A-DU, KA-PA ...), 18 contradict (KI-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO, KA-NA, KU-PA, PA-DE, *301 ...): 0.50 vs 0.76 (P 0.016). LB with its key: 0.94 vs 0.86 (P 0.75), 0.55 vs 0.85 (P 0.016), 0.75 vs 0.79 (P 0.36). Inspection: most LA 'contradictions' are total lines (KU-RO, PO-TO-KU-RO, KI-RO) aligned against name lines of lists of different length | C: dossier slots agree with la60 more than random alignments do, but the LB control passes in 1 of 3 draws, and the contradictions are alignment artefacts. No change to la60. |
| la65:LA-65.3d | Series-only words (pe63 M009 M003 analogue): words found in one dossier and on no other tablet | ZA dossier: *28B-NU-MA-RE (3/3 tablets in the dossier), SI-PI-KI (3/3); HT86/95: DI-DE-RU (2/2 tablets), KU-NI-SU (2/3, plus HT10 without a number) | C prediction: on a new Zakros VIN tablet of this template, *28B-NU-MA-RE and SI-PI-KI come together with a number; would kill: either word on a tablet of another template or with another commodity. |
| la65:LA-65.3 | Cycle verdict | Roles typed inside dossiers do not transfer outside (reuse, shape) in Linear A, and the same tests fail in the Linear B control, so this is a power limit, not a property of Minoan. The la60 comparison is not calibrated. | NOT CRACKED. No reading changes. |
| la65:LA-65.final | Idea: treat tablets copied from one template as a laboratory. (1) 2 M random templates per run (words, logograms, entries, header/closing, skeleton, counts, logogram set, site, scribe, findspot, numbering blocks) scored on held-out cohesion against 5 entry-shuffled nulls (family-wise max and FDR 0.2). (2) Align the members; type each slot by what changes with it (word alone = ID, with logogram = COM, regular step = STEP, constant ratio = RATE, fraction with logogram = FRAC); compare with matched random groups. (3) Transfer outside the dossiers and check against la60. Controls: planted dossiers (30/30 tablets; roles 15/15); Linear B KN+PY draws at LA size (5-9 series-pure dossiers each, purity 0.87-0.90 vs 0.31-0.39) | LA: 1 strict dossier (ZA4/ZA5/ZA15, wine) vs 5-9 in each LB draw; 26 FDR dossiers (HT86/95/120; HT16/20; HT17/19; HT38/THE 4; MA4/6/PH12/13 HIDE; HT23-35/60; HT27/89/94/100/110/130 CYP+KU-RO; name lists HT7/10/13/85/122). Inside them names swap alone and numbers float; fixed-word share above random groups (0.39 vs 0.30, P 0.03, as in LB); no word-logogram coupling (35 vs 34.8), no step, no rate, rescale only HT86->95. LB shows no COM, FRAC, STEP slots either at this size, and roles do not transfer outside dossiers in LB or LA | NOT CRACKED. A (method): the search finds LB series and planted dossiers; A (limit): COM/FRAC/transfer tests have no power at LA size. B: Linear A has 5-9x fewer template dossiers (10-22x fewer significant groups) than Linear B at equal size. B: in LA dossiers the variable slots are identifiers and free counts. C: *28B-NU-MA-RE and SI-PI-KI are series-only words of one Zakros wine template (would kill: either on another template). C-: dossier slots agree with la60 (P 0.016, LB control 1/3). |

Shrunk magnitudes (true 0.9 -> 0.6). Extra (non-planted) descriptors 5-17 per run: identity noise of recurring strings is indistinguishable from a weight. Held-out gain of words over document context alone +0.02 (sys) / +0.05 (tab) | A (method works at LA size, power ~60 %, false descriptors ~10 per run) |

Mostly single-sign words; JA and TA appear in both modes; *516, TA, RU, ZU, *307 also appear in the signs-shuffled null (they are single-sign words that null leaves intact) and A-DU appears in a quantity null (+0.17, +0.21) | goes to the per-feature null filter (cycle 2) |

| la69:LA-69.final | Reader-independence fingerprint calibrated on administrations of known purpose: Linear B leaf vs page tablets, PY Eb/Eo drafts vs Ep/En fair copies, Ur III undated+unsealed vs dated+sealed tablets (date lines and seals removed), Old Assyrian memoranda vs legal texts; planted notes (context deleted) from every final set and from LA; OB, proto-cuneiform, khipus as reference archives. Three methods: 13 document features (attestation of words, pairs, line shapes and openings in other documents, labelled numbers, headers, totals, words per number, private 1-sign words, repetition, cross-site attestation); 10,000 random fingerprints (2 modes) with leave-one-contrast-out, random-sign and label-permutation nulls; a cloze reader (outsider vs insider) per token; fixed-pool cross-site re-test with 6 seeds. | Planted notes are always detected (VOC/PRIV/XGRP AUC 0.7-1.0; cloze P 0.001 in 4/5). Real working notes are not: no feature, no length-free fingerprint (held-out pass 3-8%, most reverse) and no cloze measure orders notes below finals in all of LB, Ur III and OA; LB leaves are MORE readable than pages, only Ur III internal tablets are less. Length-dominated fingerprints transfer but do so equally with random signs (= shortness). LA: raw fingerprints put it beside LB leaves (shortness); length-free and cloze put it level with LB pages and proto-cuneiform (-0.136 vs -0.130/-0.135), far above its own planted notes (-0.233); insider gap 0.007, the lowest of 17 sets. Cross-site loss LA 0.26-0.62 vs LB 0.16-1.01, Ur III 0.29-0.91 at equal pools. | NOT SUPPORTED. A (calibrated negative, method): 'note-to-self' has no shared statistical signature across three administrations of known purpose, so no corpus can be placed on a notes-vs-records scale. B: Linear A is not context-deleted text and is as readable from its own corpus and site as Linear B; its poor cross-site transfer (la58/la60) is within the LB and Ur III range. C: Khania is the most formulaic LA site, Zakros the least readable from anywhere (kill: Zakros above HT with 50+ documents). |
| la69:LA-69.1a | Corpora (value-free tokens, numbers, lines). Calibration contrasts of known purpose, NOTE vs FINAL: LB_shape = Linear B KN+PY leaf-shaped (unnumbered/lettered lines, 1,157) vs page-shaped (numbered lines, 554), PY E-series excluded; LB_draft = PY Eb+Eo drafts (67) vs their fair copies Ep+En (10); UR3 = Ur III admin undated AND unsealed (2,500) vs dated AND sealed (2,500), date lines and seal surfaces removed before scoring; OA = Old Assyrian merchants' memoranda (50) vs legal debt notes/contracts (226). Reference archives: OB admin, proto-cuneiform, khipus (400 each). LA: 320 documents with >= 2 words and >= 1 number. PLANT = each FINAL set (and LA itself) with context deleted (header lines, totals, 30% of labels, 50% of words replaced by doc-private abbreviations, 40% of line breaks merged). | Built; 13 base features per document scored against random pools of 300 other documents of the same corpus that never contain it: VOC/VOC3 (words attested elsewhere / in >= 3 docs), BIG (word pairs attested), LSH (line shapes attested), FIRST (opening word used to open other docs), LAB (numbers with a word before them), HDR (number-free first line), TOT (a number = sum of >= 2 earlier ones), ENT (words per number), PRIV (1-sign words unattested elsewhere), REP (within-doc repetition), BARE (number-only lines), XGRP (words attested at another site). Vocabulary features minus a type-shuffled pool expectation; all features also residualised on log words and log lines (fit on calibration documents). | setup |
| la69:LA-69.1b | Per-feature calibration: AUC(FINAL more reader-independent than NOTE), label-permutation P (200); planted notes vs their source. | Planted notes are detected by VOC 0.71-0.99, BIG 0.71-1.00, PRIV 0.76-1.00, XGRP 0.67-1.00 in all four corpora (positive control passes). Real known notes are NOT: no feature orders NOTE < FINAL in all four contrasts (raw or residualised). Leaves are MORE predictable from the rest of LB than pages (VOC 0.36, BIG 0.37 residual, P 0.005); OA memoranda as predictable as legal texts (VOC 0.44, P 0.16); only Ur III internal tablets are less predictable (VOC 0.68, BIG 0.71). Explicitness flips too: LAB 0.73 LB, 0.80 LB_draft, 0.64 OA but 0.26 Ur III; TOT 0.78 LB_draft but 0.26 LB_shape, 0.32 OA. ENT is the most consistent (0.78-0.83 in UR3/OA, 0.39 and 0.03 in LB). | Context-deletion notes have a fingerprint; real working notes of known purpose have no shared fingerprint across Linear B, Ur III and Old Assyrian. The premise 'notes omit what the writer knows' is true only of the artificial plant (A, calibrated negative at feature level). |
| la69:LA-69.1c | Linear A feature means (sign-corrected, length-residualised) against every set. | LA sits with the FINAL/archive side on LAB (0.50, the highest of all sets; LB pages 0.33), HDR (0.22 vs LB pages 0.17) and TOT (0.11), and on the NOTE side on BIG (-0.27; only LB_draft and planted sets lower), FIRST (-0.22) and ENT (-0.29). LA vs its own planted notes: lower on VOC3 (-0.20 vs 0.06), PRIV (-0.45 vs -0.04), XGRP (-0.17 vs 0.09), so LA is not context-deleted text. | Linear A is explicit (labelled numbers, headers, totals) but its word pairs and openings are not reused; mixed profile, unlike the planted notes (B for 'not context-deleted', C for any placement). |
| la69:LA-69.2a | 5,000 random fingerprint definitions per mode (2-6 of the 13 features, Dirichlet weights, a-priori 'reader-independent' signs), length-residualised (res) and raw. Leave-one-contrast-out: select on 3 real contrasts + 5 planted (AUC > 0.55 each), test on the held-out real contrast. Nulls: same weights with random signs; NOTE/FINAL labels permuted inside each corpus (20 replicates). | res: held-out pass 0.071 (LB_shape), 0.083 (LB_draft), 0.031 (UR3), 0.46 of only 13 (OA); held-out median AUC 0.10-0.51, i.e. most selected fingerprints REVERSE on the held-out administration. Only 6/5,000 pass all 4 real + 5 planted contrasts. raw: held-out pass 0.48 / 0.77 / 0.61 / 0.69, but random-sign fingerprints transfer nearly as well (0.40 / 0.75 / 0.55 / 0.52); label-permuted selections are ~0 (max 6). Features enriched among control passers: ENT (words per number) x2.5-2.8, XGRP x1.8-2.0. | Once document length is removed there is NO transferable 'working-note' fingerprint across Linear B, Ur III and Old Assyrian (A, calibrated negative). With length left in, note-likeness transfers but does not depend on the hypothesis's signs, so it is mostly shortness and terseness (B). |
| la69:LA-69.2b | Placement of Linear A under the fingerprints that pass the user's controls (LB_shape, LB_draft and Ur III NOTE below FINAL, all planted notes detected): res n = 13, raw n = 448 (all-pass 6 / 308). Null: LA with lines re-dealt across its documents (3 copies, scored the same way). | raw: LA scores -0.41 (all-pass -0.49), between LB leaves (-0.53) and LB pages (-0.28), below every FINAL set in 81-88% of fingerprints, below every NOTE set in 32-40%; below its own line-shuffled copies (-0.22) in 79-86%. res (13 fingerprints): LA 0.10, at the FINAL level (LB pages 0.05, UR3 finals 0.07; position 1.0-1.06 on the NOTE=0 / FINAL=1 scale), not distinguishable from its shuffled copies (LA above them in 39-50%). LA's own planted notes always score far below LA (-1.05 raw, -0.45 res). | LA looks note-like only through length and terseness, like Linear B leaf tablets; with length removed it sits with final records. It is never as context-poor as its own planted notes. 'Notes to self' NOT SUPPORTED: no calibrated fingerprint exists to place it, and the length-only one puts LA beside LB leaves (B). C: LA documents are slightly less reader-independent than a re-dealing of their own lines (raw, 79-86% of fingerprints). Would kill: the gap vanishing under a length-matched re-dealing. |
| la69:LA-69.3a | Reader-independence measured directly, per token (length-insensitive): hide each word and let an OUTSIDER who knows only 300 other documents of the corpus (no sign values) guess it from its neighbours (top-5, naive-Bayes left/right context). An INSIDER also knows the rest of the same document. Notes to self predict low outsider and high insider-minus-outsider gap. Same calibration sets; planted notes scored with their source excluded from every pool; doc-level permutation P (1,000). | Calibration disagrees again. Outsider rate FINAL - NOTE: LB_shape -0.054 (P 0.095; leaves MORE readable), LB_draft -0.028 (P 0.28), UR3 +0.148 (P 0.001), OA -0.032 (P 0.25). Gap: LB pages > leaves (+0.148, P 0.001) and Ep/En > Eb/Eo (+0.47, P 0.03), but UR3 notes > finals (-0.06, P 0.053), OA 0 (P 0.79). Planted notes: outsider drops in LB_shape, UR3, OA, LA (+0.08 to +0.29, P 0.001); LB_draft (10 docs) n.s. | Only Ur III internal tablets are harder for an outsider; Linear B leaves and drafts and Old Assyrian memoranda are not. No outsider/insider signature of working notes holds across administrations (A, calibrated negative, second independent method). |
| la69:LA-69.3b | Linear A on the same reader. | LA outsider 0.143 [0.124-0.164], length-adjusted -0.136: level with LB pages (-0.130) and proto-cuneiform (-0.135), below Ur III / OA / OB / khipus, above LB drafts (-0.235) and every planted set (LA's own -0.233). LA insider gap 0.007, the LOWEST of all 17 sets (LB pages 0.159, Ur III notes 0.142): knowing the rest of a LA document does not help read a hidden word, because LA documents almost never repeat a word internally. | LA is as readable from the rest of its corpus as Linear B page tablets, and far more than context-deleted text (B). It is the opposite of a private note on the insider axis: nothing in a LA document helps its own reader either (B, descriptive; the gap is not a calibrated note-marker, see 3a). |
| la69:LA-69.3c | Cross-site outsiders: each site's documents read by a same-site pool vs an other-site pool of equal size; LA (HT, Khania, Zakros), LB (KN, PY), Ur III (Umma, Girsu, Puzrish-Dagan). LA document types: length-adjusted outsider vs the rest of LA, doc permutation. | Other/same ratio: LA 0.55 / 0.33 / 0.47; LB KN 0.50, PY 0.10; Ur III 0.70 / 0.73 / 0.49. LA types: Khania +0.102 (P 0.001) most readable; Zakros -0.082 (P 0.023) and minor sites -0.080 (P 0.005) least; HT +0.018 (P 0.40); attributed-scribe docs +0.059 (P 0.007). | LA's failure to transfer across sites (la58, la60) is inside the range of Linear B and Ur III at equal pool size; it is not evidence for private notes (B, re-test in cycle 4). |
| la69:LA-69.4a | Cycle-3 cross-site and site results re-tested with 6 fresh seeds, every site's 29 (or fewer) documents read by a pool of exactly 28 same-site or 28 other-site documents (the Zakros limit), LA, LB and Ur III alike. | Same-site outsider rate: LA HT 0.101, Khania 0.258, Zakros 0.094; LB KN 0.082, PY 0.309; Ur III 0.25-0.40. Other/same ratio (min-max over seeds): LA HT 0.62 (0.42-0.94), Khania 0.26 (0.15-0.36), Zakros 0.29 (0.16-0.37); LB KN 1.01 (0.18-2.80), PY 0.16 (0.07-0.32); Ur III Umma 0.66, Girsu 0.91, Puzrish-Dagan 0.29 (0.26-0.36). Zakros read by other sites: 0.027, the lowest of all eight sites. | Survives: LA sites are as readable inside the site as Linear B sites (HT ~ KN, Khania ~ PY), and lose about as much across sites as PY or Puzrish-Dagan (B). LB matched true reading gains 1.5 %; a third-corrupted copy gains the same 1.4 %. Pseudo-outside (45 documents): FULL 8 vs shuffled 6.5 (P 0.12), STRICT 0, order 6/5. Predictions: 37 + note, sha256 2d7c6c9b... | NOT CRACKED. V2 is cleaner than la60 (13 killed and 9 untested la60 items out, KI, *308 and the staple order in) and slightly better on held-out STRICT documents, but it still reads no word: every gain is ledger arithmetic and commodity order, the calibration shows the pipeline cannot tell a right partial reading from a two-thirds-right one, and the outside-like test gives nothing. The deciding data are new documents (RILA-S1, Anetaki II); the hashed predictions are ready for them. |
| la71:LA-71.1a | Inventory of what the sources encode. lineara.xyz (LinearAInscriptions.js, GORILA-based): every transliterated token and every character in the 1,721 records was scanned for brackets, half-brackets, '?', vacat, illegible-sign marks and private-use marks. SigLA (779 document pages, already cached by la22): popup classes and reading fields. Control: the explorer.js / utils.js source of lineara.xyz itself, to see how the site treats each mark. | lineara.xyz has NO per-sign GORILA apparatus (no underdots, no half-brackets ⸢ ⸣, no [restored] signs, no vacat). It has: the private mark U+1076B (2,171 uses), which the site strips as a break mark; it sits at broken edges, on its own for lost lines, and 33 times inside a word that the edition joins across a break; [[ ]] erasures (11 tokens: HT 34 [[7]], HT 36 [[20]] [[6]], HT 46b [[4]], HT 127b [[KI+MU]] [[14]], KH 8 [[NI]] [[1/2]], PKZg 22 [[VIN]] ...); ']X' / 'X[' partial brackets (6); '+[?]' and '+[ ]' unread or lost ligature parts (~80); '—' lost sign (56), '≈' (8), '?' unread fraction (4), 'None' (3). SigLA has per sign: sure / unsure, '[?]' unidentified sign (385), 'erasure' role (104 occurrences), sign boxes; stipple (worn surface) is drawn on the copies but not coded; SigLA has no integer numerals. | The only restoration-type information in our main source is the break mark and the 11 erasures. Damaged-but-read signs are invisible in lineara.xyz; SigLA's copies are the only per-sign damage source. |
| la71:LA-71.1b | What our parsers kept. Read build_corpus.py (corpus.json, used by 72 tools), la22_data.py (docs.json, 23 tools), la60_common.load_la, la45/46/47/51/56/58 loaders, la22_sigla.py. Control: re-parse of the cached SigLA pages with a pattern that keeps every 'occ-N' popup. | corpus.json DROPS every break mark (build_corpus.py calls it a 'line-continuation mark'), so words cut at a break and numbers at a broken edge count as complete; it KEEPS the 11 erasures as live text (erased numbers 7, 20, 6, 4, 14, 1/2 enter sums and lists); the 33 bridged words count as whole words; ligatures with '[?]' stay as logograms. la22_data keeps the break mark (gap tokens). la22_sigla.py lost 639 of 5,099 SigLA occurrences: its reading pattern [^<]* fails on every subscripted reading (ra2, pa3, ta2 ...), so la22's SigLA comparison (98.8 % agreement, 13 'SigLA extra signs') ran on 87 % of SigLA and miscounted RA2 / PA3 / TA2 positions as gaps. SigLA codes with variant letters (AB131a, AB21f) were also unread. | Same failure as pe73, in a milder form: nothing marked restored is counted as read, because lineara.xyz marks no restorations, but break-edge tokens and erasures are counted as ordinary text. The la22 SigLA side result rests on a parse that drops subscripted signs (to be re-graded in cycle 3). |
| la71:LA-71.1c | Restoration-aware parser (tools/la71_parse.py -> data/corpus_ra.json). Token schema identical to corpus.json plus 'st' (read / damaged / restored / erased / illegible) and 'fl' flags: edgeL, edgeR (touches a break), cont (the edition's repeat of a break mark at the start of the next line), bridge (word joined across a break = restored), part (bracketed or unread ligature part), erased, and from SigLA (Needleman-Wunsch alignment on sign codes, A/AB prefixes unified): unsure, unid (SigLA '[?]'), variant (SigLA reads another sign), nodraw (no SigLA counterpart), worn (stipple covers >= 0.20 of the sign box; for a number, of the strip right of the preceding sign). Stipple measured on all 779 SigLA drawings (fetched, measured, deleted; dots = small round ink components). Versions: all (= corpus.json), rd (read + damaged; restored and erased become '#R' placeholders that keep their slot), read (read only). Control: version 'all' must equal corpus.json token for token. | 'all' = corpus.json for 1,721 / 1,721 records (0 mismatches). 699 records align to SigLA (4,097 aligned signs). Tokens (words, logograms, numbers, unk) 5,312: read 3,596 (0.68), damaged 1,603, restored 31 (bridged words: libation formulas IOZa 2/9/14/15/16, PKZa 8/12, SYZa 3/8, TLZa 1, KNZc 6 ...), erased 15, illegible 67. Flags: edgeR 830, edgeL 340, cont 475 (not counted as damage), worn 695, part 95, variant 93, nodraw 61, unid 30, unsure 29. Numbers 1,566: read 1,162, damaged 398, erased 6. | Parser built; regression control passes. |
| la71:LA-71.1d | Hand check against SigLA drawings. (i) Wear: 96 sign boxes, 32 from each stipple stratum (cover < 0.1, 0.1-0.3, >= 0.3), shown as unlabelled crops on 4 contact sheets and labelled blind (W = stipple touches the strokes); population estimates weighted by stratum size. (ii) Numbers: every number on 8 KU-RO / list tablets (HT 2, 11b, 85a, 88, 89, 104, 118, 122b; 59 numbers) judged from the whole SigLA drawing as clear or damaged (in stipple / at a break). (iii) Break-mark convention: HT 2 (GORILA facsimile and SigLA), HT 3, HT 85a, HT 122b. (iv) nodraw: HT 11b, HT 24a, HT 27b. Control for (ii): flags placed at random at the parser's rate (0.25). | (i) 43 % of all sign boxes have some stipple touching the strokes. The 0.20 cover rule flags only stippled signs (precision 1.00) and catches 52 % of them (the heavily covered half); 0.05 would catch 95 % at precision 1.00. (ii) numbers: precision 0.71 (5/7), recall 0.63 (5/8); random flags at the same rate: precision 0.14. 49 of 52 numbers the parser calls read are clear (0.94). Misses: HT 104 20+J and 29 (tens written as dots inside stipple; the strip rule misses them), HT 122b 97; false alarms: HT 85a KU-RO 66 (a crack band between word and number, the number is whole), HT 122b 7. (iii) The edition repeats the break mark at the start of the next line after a line that ends in a break (HT 2: left edge intact) -> recorded as 'cont', not damage; marks inside a line are real breaks or cracks. (iv) nodraw signs are drawn by SigLA but read differently (HT 27b VIN, HT 24a ligatures); first 259 'nodraw' were the la22 subscript bug; after the fix 61 remain, treated as damaged (disputed), not restored. | Parser grade B (method): 'read' tokens are ~94 % clean on the checked numbers; 'damaged' is conservative at the edges and misses part of the surface wear. 'Restored' can only be shown for 31 bridged words: lineara.xyz does not mark GORILA restorations, so restorations inside a break may still hide among 'damaged' break-edge tokens (upper bound: the 1,170 edge tokens). |
| la71:LA-71.1e | Counts by site (final parser). | Haghia Triada 3,250 tokens: read 0.77 (2,487), damaged 726, erased 12, illegible 25; numbers 967: read 767, damaged 195, erased 5. Khania 688: read 0.53, damaged 311, restored 4, erased 2; numbers 223: damaged 86. Zakros 368: read 0.60, damaged 144; numbers 139: damaged 35. Phaistos 200: read 0.42, damaged 107; numbers 60: damaged 28. Knossos 124: read 0.52, damaged 55, restored 4; numbers 18: damaged 10. All other sites 682: read 0.55, damaged 260, restored 22 (mostly libation formulas). Whole corpus: 32 % of tokens and 26 % of numbers are not cleanly read. | Hagia Triada, where every arithmetic result lives, is the best preserved (77 % read). Outside HT about half of every token is damaged, erased, restored or illegible. |
| la71:LA-71.1 verdict | Cycle 1 | corpus.json treated break-edge tokens, 31 bridged (restored) words and 15 erased tokens (6 of them numbers) as clean text; there is no hidden GORILA restoration layer in lineara.xyz to strip. The restoration-aware parser marks 30 % of tokens as damaged, restored or erased with 0.71 precision on numbers and 0.94 clean 'read' numbers in a hand check. Side bug: la22's SigLA parse drops subscripted signs (13 % of SigLA). | Grade A (data): the counts above. Grade B (method): the parser and its versions. Cycle 2 re-runs the B results in all / rd / read. |

In rd/read the 14 is gone and the section does not close. | Grade C -> C- (the running total rests on an editor's reading of erased traces that the independent copy does not show). | la71:LA-71.3a | Make the parser the default. la71_parse.load(ver='rd') is the loader for new loops ('read' = robustness check, 'all' = legacy). la60_common.load_la (base of la60-la70 and la67_lib) now loads 'rd' unless LA71_VERSION=all (cache la_docs_rd.json beside the legacy la_docs.json); removed tokens appear as ['X', kind, section word] so a section that loses a number is untestable. build_corpus.py: the U+1076B comment corrected (break mark, not line continuation) and corpus.json declared legacy, kept byte-identical so earlier loops reproduce. tools/la71_run.py runs any older script on any version without touching the repo. Why 'rd' and not 'read': 'read' drops every break-edge token (the hand check found 2 of 7 flagged numbers whole) and every sign with 20 % stipple cover, and loses 57 % of la47 pages to alignment. Control: load_la under 'all' returns the old cache unchanged. | 'all': 1,721 documents, 0 placeholders (= old cache). 'rd': 46 placeholders (31 bridged words, 15 erased tokens). | Done. Rule for future loops: report rd as the primary run and read as the check; quote 'all' only to compare with pre-la71 numbers. |
| la71:LA-71.3b | Random-removal control (version 'rnd': as many tokens of each kind, per site group HT / other, as 'read' removes, chosen at random, seed 71). Same tests as cycle 2. | KU-RO totals: rnd 2/6 testable exact (P 0.08) vs read 5/11 (P < 0.0001). (1) 6,004 random digit/fraction fingerprint features, within-document residual correlation vs a stratum (site x commodity x magnitude) permutation null, maxT, 2-fold replication; planted estimator documents, digit taboo, fraction habit; shuffled worlds; LB KN/PY. (2) Habit profiles from 3,000 random feature subsets chosen blind by split-half reliability, frozen (sha256 a9f83d76...), and a KN-derived roundness score, then HT findspot and scribe. Damaged numbers create false round tens (div 10 P 0.006 with damaged, 0.26 read-only). (2) LA findspot P 0.42 and scribe P 0.40 on the roundness score; planted rooms P <= 0.013, KN hands P 0.0002. The random profile arm fails the LB hand control (uninformative). (3) LA copies 36 vs 41.8 (P 0.83); 40 planted copies found 4/5; KN 291 vs 244 (P 0.006), links follow hands (P 0.0005); LA links vs findspot P 0.46, scribe P 0.58. Grade C: SA-RA₂ 20/10 as a fixed allotment (would support: a new SA-RA₂ 20 or 10, or SA-RA₂ quantities heaping on 10/20 in held-out documents; would kill: further SA-RA₂ quantities spread like the shuffle). | la77:LA-77.1.1 | Sign-groups as organisms. Families = connected sets of clean sign-groups (corpus_ra.json, >= 2 syllabic signs, no edge/continuation/unsure flags, joins de-duplicated) linked by one substitution (words >= 3 signs, so >= 2 signs shared) or one insertion/deletion. Checked first: la13/la16/la32 used one-sign pairs for spelling rules and locality, la17/la31 tested spread on SHARED words; no tree-based phylogeography of near-variants found in LITERATURE.md, FINDINGS, STRATEGIES or a web search (palaeographic sign-shape studies only). | 568 types, 191 edit links, 70 families (47 of 2 types, largest 39), 49 span >= 2 sites. Edit classes: sub-initial 30, sub-medial 20, sub-final 35, indel-initial 32, indel-medial 32, indel-final 42. | Data built. |
| la77:LA-77.1.2 | 200,000 random mutation models (6 log-rates for sub/indel x initial/medial/final, tree noise 0-2, 0-30 % reticulate edges) x random trees (noisy min-cost spanning trees of each family). Controls: 5 within-site sign shuffles (word lengths kept); Linear B KN at LA size (3 samples) as a known inflected script. | Held-out gain over opportunity-only: LA +0.009 +- 0.042 nats/edge (admin-only -0.044 +- 0.081); shuffles -0.058 to -0.149; LB +0.145 to +0.182 (sd 0.001-0.014). Survivors put LA mass evenly (0.11-0.19 per class); LB survivors on substitutions at the start and middle (0.20-0.26). | LA mutation profile does not transfer to held-out families (LB's does). NOT SUPPORTED as a learnable mutation process. |
| la77:LA-77.1.3 | Which positions mutate: edit-class shares, real vs 200 within-site sign shuffles (no hypothesis search); LB KN (10 LA-sized samples) as calibration. | LA final substitution 0.183 vs 0.117 +- 0.025 (z +2.6, P 0.015; Holm over 6 classes 0.09); initial substitution 0.157 vs 0.118 (z +1.6, P 0.14); initial and medial indels below shuffle (z -1.9, P 0.075-0.085). LB: substitutions at the start 0.25 and middle 0.22 dominate, final 0.18, i.e. in a known inflected script name pairs sharing an ending outnumber case-ending pairs, so a final excess is not by itself an inflection signature. | Grade C: LA near-variants change the LAST sign more than chance strings (suffix-like slot). Picking 'the best affixes' from the past does not predict the next ones (B, la78 again). |

Would support: z >= 2 on >= 100 new non-HT entry pairs; would kill: z < 1 there. |
| la79:LA-79.1.4 | Consonant-group avoidance inside words (la10/la71, C, 'HT only'), conventional consonant rows vs 500 permuted row maps. First run used a global-frequency null; check run used a within-word shuffle null (200 perms), excluding the pure-vowel row, per site, read-only. | Global null: z 6.7, 59/62 histories, 40/40 random. Within-word null: z -3.6 with vowels (decoy 5 % at -2.8: at about the 2nd percentile), but excluding the vowel row only 74 vs 80.8 (z -1.4; read-only -0.9); per site HT -0.6, ZA -0.7, KN -0.9, KH +0.9, PH +0.9. | The large signal was position (pure vowels sit word-initially), not consonant avoidance; my first test was fooled and every history 'confirmed' it (lesson: history robustness does not fix a wrong null). Consonant-repeat avoidance C -> C- (would kill: z > -1 on a within-word null in 300 new words; would support: z < -2.5). Vowel-row avoidance is positional (B, trivial). |
| la79:LA-79.1.5 | Linear B shared words of 3+ signs (2e, A as a statistic): within-split sign shuffle (100 perms), plus a position-preserving null (first/middle/last shuffled separately, 300 perms) on later finds and per site. | Sign-shuffle: 55/62 histories, 3/3 cuts. Position-preserving: full 11 vs 3.2 (z 4.3); after 1950 6 vs 1.3 (z 4.3); after 1976 2 vs 0.4; non-HT 7 vs 1.65 (z 4.4); PH 3 vs 0.14, OTH 3 vs 0.6, HT 4 vs 1.6, KH 0 vs 0.2, KN 0 vs 0.2. | Holds in later finds and outside HT: A kept (as a statistic, not as a reading). |
| la79:LA-79.1.6 | Learned orders and ties. Commodity order (la20, A): order learned on the 'dug first' side, pair agreement on the later side vs 2,000 random orders. Fraction writing order (attack 1, A). Fraction order: 19/51 histories vs 33/38 random; HT <-> KH transfer 0.87-0.95; from the 1950 corpus 0.69 (pct 0.79, n 26). Fraction-commodity: 5/60 histories, 1/3 cuts; from 1950 the learned table LOSES 0.59 bits/letter on later finds. | Commodity order travels like a partly universal order but never significantly from a real cut: A -> B (would kill: <= 10 of the next 20 new pairs; la78 row). Fraction order: A kept for the existence of a strict order; its transfer is between HT and KH only (B). Fraction letter depends on commodity: true inside archives, but the mapping does not carry between archives or forward: B -> C as a general claim (B within one archive). |

Compare success rates site vs random per claim. | Rates (site / random): aggregate affixes 0.77/0.73, 0.84/0.73; -ME 0.62/0.50; LB 0.89/0.90; commodity order 0.59/0.58; fraction order 0.37/0.87; fraction-commodity 0.08/0.13; consecutive 0.13/0.05. | For the published claims random splits are close to site histories except the fraction order (random splits are 2.4x optimistic). The la78 anti-correlation is about choosing among many hypotheses, not about the big published effects (B). |
| la79:LA-79.2.1 | Survival rate: real vs shuffled dates vs planted decoys. | Real 19.2 % of 200,000 survive both later futures (two seeds 19.2 / 19.2 %); shuffled dates 50-69 %; planted decoys: stable worlds 21-42 % (mean 27 %), drifting worlds 15-28 % (mean 20 %). | The real survival rate sits with the drifting planted worlds, below every stable one but one: weak sign that Linear A sign-group roles change between the 1945 archive and later finds (C; would support: next 50+ new clean groups give survival <= 20 % again; would kill: >= 30 %). |
| la79:LA-79.2.2 | The law in real data: Spearman of each 1950-knowable property with the later-future gain, pooled and within family; survival by decile. | Pooled: ins -0.15, K -0.26, ent -0.05, loso +0.05, W1 -0.06. Within family: ins -0.20 to -0.45 (all 7), ent -0.20 to -0.33 (final, initial, second, word), loso +0.05 to +0.12 (sign families), W1 +0.06 to +0.12. Top-ins decile survives 5-13 % vs bottom decile 24-44 % (sign families). Top 0.1 % by past fit sit at the 3rd-26th percentile of W1 and 15th-35th of later gain. Same in both seeds to 3 decimals. | Reverse Occam holds in real data: the worse a random reading fits the 1950 corpus, the more likely it survives on later finds; readings whose classes encode site survive less (B as a description). |
| la79:LA-79.2.3 | Shuffled-date control (5 x 20,000). | Signs flip: ins +0.04 to +0.43, ent +0.02 to +0.38, W1 -0.06 to +0.23, loso -0.20 to +0.01; ins~W1 +0.20 to +0.41. | Under random-document splits the opposite law holds (fit wins), so inside-corpus testing would teach the wrong rule here (A, method; agrees with la78). |
| la79:LA-79.2.4 | Planted worlds: does reverse Occam need a grammar that changes? Rank of the truth and the decoy-pool law in stable vs drifting worlds. | Stable worlds reproduce the law among decoys: ins~W1 -0.18 to -0.68 (real -0.66), ins~later -0.17 (mean), ent~later -0.21, loso~W1 +0.12 to +0.34 (real +0.23). Truth: top on in-sample fit in 16/16, W1 percentile 1.0 in 8/8 stable vs 0.00-0.01 in drift; survives 8/8 stable, 1/8 drift. The top past-fitting DECOYS fall low on W1 in stable worlds too (0.00-0.97). | KEY: the la78 reversal (best past fit predicts the future worst) is produced by the real site mix in a world whose grammar is perfectly stable, as long as the truth is not in the pool. It shows that in-sample fit picks site-mix artefacts, not that Linear A changed (B). LA-78.2.3 ('real behaves like stronger drift than planted', B) -> C: with the site mix planted, real lies inside the stable range on rho(ins, W1) and rho(loso, W1); only the survival rate (2.1) leans to drift. Time travel still separates a true stable reading (W1 pct 1.0) from a drifting one (0.00) (A, method, kept). |
| la79:LA-79.2.5 | Practical rule for grading (what to require of any future Linear A reading). | From 2.2-2.4: require (i) leave-one-site-out gain > 0, (ii) low site entanglement (below the pool median), (iii) W1 time-travel percentile >= 0.95; do NOT reward in-sample or random-CV fit. In stable planted worlds the truth passes all three in 6/8 (2 fail loso > 0) and its entanglement is at the 24th-57th percentile; in drift worlds 0/8 pass (iii). | Adopted as the la79 grading filter (B, method). |
| la79:LA-79.2.6 | Does the 2.5 filter lift survival among random readings? Real pool vs planted and shuffled. | Stable planted worlds: filtered decoys survive 0.37 on average vs 0.27 base (lift in 6/8; 0.12-0.44); drifting worlds: 0.13 vs 0.20 (lift in 2/8); shuffled dates: 0.65-0.76 vs 0.50-0.69. Real: 434 of 200,000 pass; they survive 0.184 vs 0.192 base (no lift). | The filter works where the grammar is stable and fails where it drifts; in real Linear A it gives no lift, again like the drifting worlds (C, joint with 2.1; would kill: filtered hypotheses survive >= 25 % on the next 50+ new clean groups). Not cracked. |
| la79:LA-79.3.1 | Backcasting: treat the <= 1950 corpus (97 % HT) as the unknown. Real labels: 3 seeds x 1,500 (+ 4,000 in c3_back). Controls: 10 within-site label shuffles, 10 planted universal grammars with the real site label mix (fresh hypotheses each). | Share of readings that gain: forward 0.09-0.11, backward 0.23-0.25; backward - forward +0.12 to +0.15 (4,000-run: 0.107 vs 0.232). Real lies beyond all 20 controls; real forward is below 9-10 of the 10 label shuffles. | Asymmetric transfer: what later finds teach holds in the Hagia Triada archive, but what that archive teaches fails on later finds, and does worse than readings fitted to shuffled labels (B). Reading: the HT archive = a shared core plus local conventions, not a different system (C; would kill: the asymmetry vanishes (<= +0.03) when RILA-S1 / post-2020 finds are added to the 'later' side). |
| la79:LA-79.3.2 | Site-pair transfer matrix (6 x 6, 4,000 readings): which archive teaches and which learns? | Real: HT teaches 0.22 / learns 0.03, OTH teaches 0.23 / learns 0.03, KN teaches 0.00 / learns 0.20. Only the pooled backward/forward contrast (3.1) beats its controls. |
| la79:LA-79.3.3 | Forecast lottery: 100,000 random sign sets (3-30 signs with >= 10 tokens), each with a random direction ('its share will rise / fall in what is found next'); survivors = right at both the 1950 and 1976 cuts; held-out test = finds published after 1988. Consensus: rising JA, I, A, WA, TA, MA, NA, PI, NU, TE, O, TO, SE, DE, *164; falling KU, KA, *301, RO, RA2, SI, ZE, PA3, DI, KI, PA, QE, JU, RI, DA. Tablets alone: falling-set share 0.34 (<= 1950) -> 0.24 (later), rising 0.22 -> 0.33. By site: almost all of it is HT vs the rest (HT <= 1976 falling 0.52; non-HT 0.21-0.26), OTH flat (0.20 -> 0.23). | The digging order has a stable direction: each new decade of finds moves away from the HT sealing-heading signs (KU, KA, *301, RO, SI, ZE) toward vowel and JA/WA/TA/MA/NA signs (B as a description, held out). It is a site-mix effect, not a change in Minoan writing (C for anything more). |
| la79:LA-79.3.4 | Freeze for RILA-S1, Anetaki II and post-2020 finds: P1 sign drift (current corpus rising 0.271, falling 0.385); P2 survival of random role readings <= 0.20; P3 backward > forward transfer; P4 kill lines for the claims that passed cycle 1 (aggregate affixes, -ME, Linear B shared words, commodity order). | data/la79_frozen_predictions.json, sha256 6f75f0d356b1525cf54d1d97fef736b61242d2423757421c66c1dd9d7bc58758 (of json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True); also in la79_frozen_predictions.sha256). | Frozen. P1 support: on >= 200 new non-HT sign tokens rising > 0.271 and falling < 0.385; kill: both fail. |
| la79:LA-79.final | Counterfactual excavation histories and time-travel re-grading (built on la78). Cycle 1: 13 earlier claims re-scored on the 'found later' side of 62 site-subset histories, the 1950/1976/1988 publication cuts and 40 random-document splits, each against its own decoy family (other signs, 300 random sign hashes, 500 permuted consonant maps, 2,000 random orders, commodity shuffles); planted universal vs HT-only prefix and commodity order. Cycle 2: 200,000 random role readings; which 1950-knowable property predicts survival into the 1976 and 1988 futures; 5 shuffled-date runs, 16 planted stable/drifting worlds. Cycle 3: backcasting (later finds teach the 1945 archive) vs 10 label shuffles and 10 planted grammars; 100,000-set forecast lottery for sign drift (200 shuffled-date runs); freeze. | Planted HT-only truths pass 73-80 % of random splits but 0 of 31 histories whose later finds lack HT. Aggregate affixing passes 48-52/62 histories and 3/3 cuts; -ME 3/3 cuts; I-, A-, KI-, SI-, consecutive first-sign sharing, fraction-by-commodity fail; consonant avoidance was a vowel-position artefact. Reverse Occam in real data (in-sample fit vs later survival rho -0.20 to -0.45 within families) but the same law appears in planted STABLE worlds; real survival 19 % sits with drifting worlds (20 %) below stable (27 %). Backward transfer 0.23-0.25 vs forward 0.09-0.11, beyond all 20 controls. Lottery survivors right on post-1988 finds 86 % vs 51 %. | A (method): history splits kill archive-local truths that random splits accept. Grade changes: affixing (aggregate) B -> A; commodity order (la20) A -> B; fraction letter by commodity B -> C (holds only within an archive); individual prefixes I-, A-, KI- -> C- (A- killed on corpus_ra); SI- C -> C-; consecutive first-sign sharing C+ -> C-; consonant-repeat avoidance C -> C-; LA-78.2.3 B -> C. Kept: Linear B shared words A, fraction order A (transfer B), -ME B. New B: HT-taught readings fail forward while later-taught ones hold on HT (shared core + local HT conventions, C). Frozen sha256 6f75f0d3... Not cracked. |
| la80:LA-80.1.1 | 20,000 random (text feature x outline feature) pairs on 116 HT tablets, partial Spearman net of size, lines and break share; survive at |z| >= 2.5 with the same sign in >= 3 of 4 HT deposits (Villa Magazine 57, Casa 7 23, Casa del Lebete 20, Casa 9 10). Kill: share <= 0.60 or not above the shuffle 95th pct. Power: each survivor planted into the test shapes at 0.15 / 0.25 / 0.35 sd. | Real 0.53 (mean signed r +0.008; tablet bootstrap 0.31-0.74); shuffle-run survivors 0.505 (max 0.655), real ranks 10th of 21; random pairs 0.49. Post-1976 finds (27 tablets) 0.81 (rank 1 of 21), but bootstrap 0.39-0.86 and this subset was not in the frozen plan. By shape: rectness 0.95 (19), fd3 1.00 (46), endw 1.00, solidity 0.95, roughness 0.95 (worn share -> rough outline: damage, trivial), but taper 0.14, min corner 0.15, corner sd 0.00, fd5 0.39. 'Numbers per line -> rectangular' held weakly (+0.067); 'numbers per line -> square corners' did not (-0.006). | KILLED (B, calibrated: power 0.99 at a sixth of the HT effect): the text of Hagia Triada tablets does not predict the clay outline of other sites' tablets. HT text-shape links are local, like the role links la78-la79 found. C-: numbers-per-line -> more rectangular blank (+0.067 out of site; would support: >= +0.15 on 50 new complete tablets; would kill: <= 0). Exploratory only: post-1976 finds agree 0.81 (n 27). |

Signs (cosine): 8 vs 0-360 (median 0). Planted 230-558 survivors. Best word metric: corner fill, min corner, roughness, harmonic 3. | Weak at best inside HT (P ~0.1). Primary: share of frozen survivors with r < 0 vs null-run survivor sets; pre-specified single test: best HT metric, within-site permutation p. Kill: share <= 0.60 or p > 0.05. Power: the planted text component (0.5 sd on the metric's 3 heaviest features) vs the permutation spread. | Words: survivors r < 0 in 0.21 (null-run sets 0.28 mean); best metric r +0.007 (HT -0.133), p 0.57; planted -0.056 vs permutation 5th pct -0.059 (power ~50 % at the planted size, ~100 % at the HT size, about 3.5 sd). Across all 5,000 random metrics only 19 % (words) and 38 % (signs) are negative on non-HT pairs. | KILLED (B): objects made alike do not carry related text outside HT; the HT effect size is excluded. C- (exploratory, not frozen): on other sites alike-looking blanks lean toward LESS shared vocabulary (19 % of random metrics negative), which would fit blanks drawn from a common stock rather than made per job. Would support: the same lean on post-2000 finds; would kill: share >= 0.5 with fragments (break share > 0.3) removed. |
| la80:LA-80.final | The object as outside data: outlines, size and tone of the clay measured blind from lineara.xyz photographs (GORILA plates and later photos; 2-of-3 agreement between two photo thresholds and the facsimile outline, rule fixed before any text) and scored against the text, always selected on Hagia Triada, frozen by sha256, and tested on other sites' objects fetched and measured only after the freeze. ## Summary

See FINDINGS.md for the state of knowledge before 4 Oct 2026.
